# CLAUDE.md — Oddzilla agent doc

This file is loaded into every agent's context. Read it before touching code.
For deeper references see [`docs/`](./docs/).

## TL;DR

Oddzilla is a B2C esports sportsbook MVP. Go services handle the hot path
(Oddin AMQP feed, odds publishing, settlement, bet-delay, wallet watching).
TypeScript handles the REST API, WebSocket gateway, and Next.js frontend.
Postgres 16 + Redis 7 + Caddy, all on one Hetzner box via Docker Compose.

| Question | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| What sportsbook are we building? | B2C, esports-only. Every Oddin esport is surfaced; CS2, DOTA2, LOL, Valorant pinned at the top of the sidebar; `efootballbots` + `ebasketballbots` excluded (backend blocklist + DB inactive). |
| Which markets? | All Oddin markets — the `provider_market_id` whitelist was dropped at both feed-ingester and `/catalog/matches/:id`. Known labels for match-winner (`1`) and map-winner (`4`); others render as "Market #N". |
| Which feed? | Oddin.gg, **protocol level** (raw AMQP + REST, no SDK) for esports. Traditional sports come from the public **Fonbet** line via [`services/fonbet-ingester`](./services/fonbet-ingester/) — `fon.bet` **in English** since 2026-09-04 (was `fonbet.kz` in Russian; same line, same ids) — (HTTP poll, gated by `FONBET_ENABLED`; settlement from Fonbet's results feed via the `settlement.external` stream — see [`docs/FONBET.md`](./docs/FONBET.md)). |
| Which chains? | USDC on ERC20 (Ethereum) only. Migration 0032 renamed the currency from USDT and dropped Tron + per-user HD addresses. |
| Where does it run? | Hetzner CPX31 at `178.104.174.24` (SSH key-based access as `team`). 4 vCPU / 8 GB / 160 GB — upgraded from CPX22 on 2026-05-11 to give the storefront SSR replica fan-out (3× Next.js processes) room to breathe alongside postgres, api, and the Go workers. Public URLs: **`oddzilla.cc`** (storefront, apex) + **`sadmin.oddzilla.cc`** (admin; `/` redirects to `/admin`). The legacy `s.oddzilla.cc` host 301s to the apex via a hard-coded Caddyfile block — drop the block and DNS record once the old subdomain stops receiving traffic. Let's Encrypt via Caddy. |
| How is money stored? | `BIGINT _micro` (6 decimals = 1,000,000 micro per unit) on every wallet/ledger/ticket row, with a sibling `currency CHAR(4)`. Two currencies: `USDC` (real, on-chain via ERC20) and `OZ` (demo, every signup gets a 1000 OZ bonus for testing the bet flow). Withdrawals + deposits stay USDC-only. |
| Auth? | Email + password (argon2id), JWT access (15 min) + refresh cookie (30 d). Cookies are **host-scoped** (no `Domain` attribute) — a session on `oddzilla.cc` and a session on `sadmin.oddzilla.cc` are independent, so an operator can be logged in as a bettor on the apex and as an admin on the backoffice in the same browser. The pre-2026-05-18 model shared the cookie via `Domain=.oddzilla.cc`; the legacy cookie is killed alongside every new set so in-flight sessions migrate cleanly. **The same email may back a bettor AND an admin row** (migration 0065): email uniqueness is split into two partial indexes keyed by `users.role` (`'user'` = bettor namespace, `'admin'`/`'support'` = admin namespace), and `/auth/login` filters by the namespace implied by the request host (`oddzilla.cc` → bettor, `sadmin.oddzilla.cc` → admin) so the subdomain implicitly picks which account to authenticate. |
| Shell widths? | 240px sidebar + 900px main cap + **380px** right rail = 1520px shell max-width. The rail went 300 → 380 on 2026-09-05: 300 was sized for a rail holding the bet slip and little else, and it now also carries the Sportradar Head to Head and Live Table embeds — a ten-column league table had ~267px of usable width and grew its own horizontal scrollbar. The Live Table frame full-bleeds (cancels the section's 16px side padding) for another 32px. **380 is not a measured minimum** — the widget is cross-origin on a host the dev environment cannot load, so if the table still clips, the rail width and the shell max-width move together. |
| UI aesthetic? | Premium / quiet editorial: Instrument Serif display + Geist UI + Geist Mono for odds. Light theme default (`#f4f2ec`), dark via `data-theme="dark"` (toggle in the top bar; choice persists in `localStorage["oz:theme"]`). Ported from the Claude Design handoff bundle. **No emojis.** |

Current phase: **Phases 1–8 complete + security hardening + catalog cleanup
+ frontend redesign + USDC payments rewrite (migration 0032).** News
scraper was cancelled during Phase 8 (migration 0003). The site went
live at `s.oddzilla.cc` on 2026-04-18 and moved to the apex
`oddzilla.cc` shortly after; market whitelist active, and
`payback_margin_bp=0` globally (Oddin already sends margined odds —
see [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md) before re-enabling).
Migration 0032 (2026-05-07) replaced the per-user HD-address USDT
flow with a single shared ERC20 address; deposits are now attributed
by matching the on-chain sender against the bettor's signature-verified
linked wallet addresses (there is intentionally NO user-facing
paste-your-tx-hash route — that would be a deposit-theft vector), and
unlinked-sender deposits fall through to admin review. wallet-watcher
is intent-driven. Next: pre-launch
exit gates (KYC, wallet reconciliation, off-server backup rsync,
monitoring, runbook). Full roadmap in
[`docs/PHASES.md`](./docs/PHASES.md).

## Architecture map

```
Oddin AMQP+REST ── feed-ingester (Go) ──► Postgres (markets, outcomes, odds_history)
                                       └─► Redis Streams (odds.raw)

Fonbet HTTP JSON ──── fonbet-ingester (Go) ──► Postgres (same tables; fb: URNs,
   (poll every 5 s,                              provider_market_id >= 1 000 000)
    traditional sports)                     └─► Redis Streams (odds.raw)

Redis Streams ──► odds-publisher (Go) ──► Postgres (published_odds)
                                       └─► Redis pub/sub (odds:match:{id})

Redis pub/sub ──► ws-gateway (TS) ──► browsers (WebSocket, 1 MiB/socket buffer cap)
                                       │
                                       └─ also user:{id} channels for ticket frames

Browser ── Caddy (lb_policy least_conn) ──► web1 / web2 / web3 (Next.js SSR)
                                                  │
                                                  ▼
                                              api (TS Fastify) ──► Postgres
                                                  │
                                                  ├─► POST /bets → ticket → pg_notify('bet_delay')
                                                  │      ▼
                                                  │   bet-delay (Go) ──► Postgres (promote/reject)
                                                  │
                                                  └─► POST /wallet/withdrawals → locks stake → admin queue

Oddin AMQP ──► settlement (Go) ──► Postgres (settlements + tickets + wallet_ledger)
                                    └─► Redis pub/sub (user:{id} ticket frames)

Oddin Bifrost GraphQL ──wss──► bifrost-feed (Go) ──► Redis stream `oddin.backup`
  (backup; standby until feed-ingester's        ├─► feed-ingester (odds_change, fixture_change)
   `feed:primary:last_msg_unix` stamp is stale) └─► settlement    (bet_settlement)
  feed-ingester automap: REST fixture fails ──► Bifrost `match` query (same FixtureResponse shape)

Tron + ETH RPC ──► wallet-watcher (Go) ──► Postgres (deposits + wallet_ledger)
                                            (per-chain block scanner + confirmations
                                             tick + atomic credit)

Browser ── /widgets/* (api proxy, x-brand-token) ──► Oddin Disir REST
                                                       └─► returns iframe URL for
                                                           prematch / live widgets

Browser ── /video/match/:id (api) ──► Oddin /v1/catalog (Redis 60s, one call
   │                                   covers every match: "is there a stream?")
   └─► @oddin-gg/havik-player in-page ──► *.oddin-video.gg
         (no iframe; LL-HLS + Widevine/FairPlay direct from the browser using
          the publishable key the api handed it. feed=playback+catalog,
          playback=manifest/segments, drm=licence, events.feed=SSE, beacons=QoE)

support-ai-bot (IN the stack since 2026-09-01; outbound-only, listens on nothing):
  support-ai-bot ──────► api:3001 /webhooks/support-ai/<secret>
                    └──► hosted OpenAI-compatible LLM (LM_STUDIO_BASE_URL)

Operator PC (NOT in the stack; dials OUT only, server never connects in):
  zillaboost-banner-gen ► api /webhooks/banner-gen/<secret>  ──► LM Studio + ComfyUI 127.0.0.1:8188
                          (poll queue → render → upload image)
    ^ stays on the PC permanently: ComfyUI is unauthenticated admin-level
      code execution and must remain bound to loopback. support-ai-bot was
      only ever there because its model was local; a hosted endpoint removed
      that anchor, so it moved to the box and now answers 24/7.

External SMTP ── port 25 ──► mail-receiver (Go) ──► api /webhooks/sendgrid-inbound
                                                     └─► email_inbound / email_threads
                                                         (backoffice inbox)
```

See [`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`](./docs/ARCHITECTURE.md) for the full picture, and
[`docs/ODDIN.md`](./docs/ODDIN.md) for the Oddin protocol details.

## Tech stack (locked)

- **Go 1.23** — `services/{feed-ingester, fonbet-ingester, odds-publisher, settlement, bet-delay, wallet-watcher, signer, bifrost-feed}`.
  Each service is its own Go module. Shared libs across services:
  `rabbitmq/amqp091-go`, `encoding/xml`, `jackc/pgx/v5` + `pgxpool`,
  `redis/go-redis/v9`, `rs/zerolog`. wallet-watcher uses stdlib `net/http`
  for both Ethereum JSON-RPC and TronGrid REST — no go-ethereum or
  gotron-sdk dependency for read-only scanning. Address derivation is
  done in TS (`services/api/src/lib/hdwallet.ts`); signing for
  withdrawals is intentionally not implemented yet (manual broadcast
  via admin UI for MVP).
- **Node 22 / TypeScript** — `services/{api, ws-gateway}`, `apps/web`.
  Fastify 5, `ws`, `ioredis`, `pino`, `zod`, `jose`, `@node-rs/argon2`,
  `ethers` v6 + `bs58` (HD derivation in API), Next.js 15.x, Tailwind v4,
  `qrcode.react`. shadcn/ui not yet adopted — primitives are inline in the
  dark theme; switch when a real component library is needed. The api
  container starts via `tsx src/server.ts` rather than compiled JS because
  the `@oddzilla/db` workspace ships source TypeScript; switch to a build
  step when packages get heavier.
- **Postgres 16** (extensions `pgcrypto`, `citext`; note `pg_partman` is
  NOT installed in the image despite migration 0001 trying for it — odds_history
  partition retention runs via the `oddzilla-odds-retention` cron instead, see
  `docs/OPERATIONS.md`), **Redis 7**,
  **Caddy 2**. All via Docker Compose.
- **Drizzle ORM** owns the schema. Hand-written SQL migrations in
  `packages/db/migrations/`. Go services read the same tables via raw pgx
  queries (sqlc was planned but not adopted — the query surface is small
  enough that hand-written SQL is clearer than codegen).

## Invariants (non-negotiable)

These rules are load-bearing. Breaking them causes money or data loss.

1. **Money is `BIGINT _micro` (6 decimals) per currency.**
   Column suffix is `_micro`. Never `NUMERIC` for balances/stakes. Never
   `number` in TS (precision loss > 2^53). Use the `MicroUsdt` branded bigint
   from `packages/types/src/money.ts` for arithmetic (the math is currency-
   agnostic — OZ also has 6 decimals). JSON serialization: bigints go over
   the wire as decimal strings (`"10000000"` not `10000000`).

   Every wallet read/write is scoped by `(user_id, currency)` since
   migration 0014. Forgetting the `currency` filter on a `wallets` /
   `wallet_ledger` query will silently touch the wrong row. The supported
   set is hard-coded in
   [`packages/types/src/currencies.ts`](./packages/types/src/currencies.ts)
   (`SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES = ["USDC", "OZ"]`). Withdrawals + on-chain
   deposits hard-code `currency='USDC'`; OZ has no chain.

2. **Specifier canonicalization.** A market is uniquely keyed by
   `(match_id, provider_market_id, specifiers_hash)`. The hash is sha256 of
   `k1=v1|k2=v2` with keys sorted lexicographically. Four implementations
   that MUST stay byte-identical:
   - TS: [`packages/types/src/specifiers.ts`](./packages/types/src/specifiers.ts)
   - Go (feed-ingester): `services/feed-ingester/internal/oddinxml/specifiers.go`
   - Go (settlement): `services/settlement/internal/oddinxml/specifiers.go`
     (intentionally duplicated per the per-service-Go-module rule)
   - Go (fonbet-ingester): `services/fonbet-ingester/internal/specifiers/specifiers.go`
     (same rule; the Fonbet grader also emits canonical specifiers on the
     `settlement.external` stream, which the settlement copy re-hashes)

   All four are tested against the shared
   [`docs/fixtures/specifiers.json`](./docs/fixtures/specifiers.json)
   golden table. If they diverge, settlement silently fails to match
   tickets to settled markets.

3. **Apply-once settlement.** Every Oddin settlement/cancel/rollback message
   inserts into `settlements` keyed by
   `(event_urn, market_id, specifiers_hash, type, payload_hash)` with
   `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. If no row is returned, it's a replay — ack AMQP
   and move on. Implemented in
   `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` `InsertIfNew()`.
   Retention boundary (2026-07-03): the dedup only needs to outlive the
   identical-replay window — AMQP redelivery plus the `RecoveryWindowCap`
   24 h recovery clamp — so the nightly `oddzilla-settlements-retention`
   cron deletes `settle`/`cancel` rows after 45 days (open-ticket-guarded;
   `rollback_*` rows kept forever since a re-applied rollback is the one
   replay class that would claw back a real payout). Bettor-facing ticket
   history (`tickets`, `ticket_selections`, `wallet_ledger`,
   `market_outcomes.result`) is never deleted by anything. A late
   CORRECTION from Oddin carries a different `payload_hash` and is meant
   to apply even with the old row present. See docs/OPERATIONS.md →
   "settlements retention".

4. **Apply-once wallet credits.** `wallet_ledger` has a unique partial index
   on `(type, ref_type, ref_id) WHERE ref_id IS NOT NULL`. Every credit
   (deposit, payout, refund, manual void, withdrawal debit) supplies a
   stable `ref_id` (ticket UUID, deposit UUID, withdrawal UUID, etc.).
   Replays are no-ops at the row level — three layers of defense
   (settlement insert, wallet_ledger unique index, transactional updates).

5. **Drizzle is the schema source of truth.** Schema changes start in
   `packages/db/src/schema/*.ts` AND a hand-written SQL file in
   `packages/db/migrations/`. Go services use raw pgx queries that
   read those tables directly — no codegen.

   **Name it `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS_lower_snake_case.sql`** — get the prefix from
   `date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%S`, never by looking at the directory and adding
   one. A sequence number read from your branch is not unique across
   branches: two branches off the same commit both see the same max and both
   claim it, git merges them cleanly because they are different files, and
   the collision only exists in the merged tree. That happened 11 times
   (0045 three ways) before the numbers were frozen at `0110`. It is not
   cosmetic — `migrate.ts` sorts by filename, so a tie is broken by the
   description text, and production applies migrations in merge order while
   a fresh database applies them alphabetically. When those disagree the
   same repo yields two different schemas with no error anywhere.
   `pnpm db:check-migrations` enforces it; it is chained onto `packages/db`'s
   `lint`, so `pnpm lint` and CI both run it — on pull requests and on every
   push to main, which is the backstop for two PRs that never saw each other.

   Do NOT hand-append to `migrations/meta/_journal.json`. The runner reads
   the directory, not the journal; the file has been unmaintained since
   `0058` and 65 migrations have landed without it. It stays only because
   drizzle-kit owns it.

   Never rename or edit a migration that has been applied anywhere.
   `_migrations` keys on the filename with no checksum, so a rename re-runs
   the file. Fix a bad name while the PR is still open — that is the only
   window in which it is free.

6. **No localhost in code.** Every inter-service URL/host comes from env.
   `packages/config/src/env.ts` parses with zod and fails fast on missing
   required vars.

7. **Redis Streams = internal bus. Redis pub/sub = best-effort fanout.**
   Postgres `published_odds` is always the source of truth when a WS client
   reconnects. Pub/sub may drop; streams do not. Two pub/sub channel
   namespaces in use:
   - `odds:match:{matchId}` — one publisher (`odds-publisher`), fan-out
     via `ws-gateway`. Not rate-limited: a sportsbook cannot drop price
     ticks, and the outbound token bucket that used to be here was
     removed in `c005882`.
   - `user:{userId}` — two publishers (`api` on placement, `bet-delay` /
     `settlement` on lifecycle), fan-out unrate-limited (low volume,
     high-value-to-user ticket frames).

   **The fanout is bounded by buffer, not by rate.** `ws.send()` never
   blocks — a consumer that stops draining (throttled phone, laptop
   asleep on a half-open TCP connection still reporting `readyState ===
   OPEN`, OS-frozen tab) has every subsequent frame queued in the
   sender, reachable, and therefore un-collectable. That is unbounded
   heap growth keyed to FEED VOLUME rather than client count, and it
   OOM-killed the 256 MiB gateway four times on 2026-09-03 while it
   served a couple of dozen sockets. Every fan-out send goes through
   `sendToClient()` in
   [`services/ws-gateway/src/server.ts`](./services/ws-gateway/src/server.ts),
   which disconnects a socket past `WS_MAX_BUFFERED_BYTES` (1 MiB)
   rather than queueing behind it. Disconnecting is the correct
   remedy precisely because of the first line of this invariant: the
   browser reconnects, resubscribes, and re-reads current prices from
   Postgres. Silently dropping frames instead would leave that client
   quoting a price the book has already moved off, with no signal.

   **A WS socket's identity is fixed at upgrade time.** ws-gateway reads
   the `oddzilla_access` cookie once, during the HTTP upgrade, and never
   re-reads it — so a socket opened while logged out never joins its
   `user:{id}` channel, no matter what happens afterwards in the tab.
   Signing in is a client-side route change, so nothing reopens it on
   its own. [`ws-session-sync.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/ws-session-sync.tsx)
   compares the gateway's `hello.userId` against the SSR-resolved
   session and reconnects on a mismatch (bounded — an expired cookie
   must not spin). Anything that depends on a `user:{id}` frame needs a
   transport-independent fallback anyway; see the ticket poll in
   [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx).

   **A stream consumer must survive losing its consumer group.** Production
   Redis is `maxmemory 256mb` + `allkeys-lru`, so it can evict a stream key
   under pressure, and evicting a stream destroys its consumer groups. The
   producer's next XADD recreates the key but nothing recreates the group,
   so any consumer that only calls `XGROUP CREATE` at boot backs off on
   `NOGROUP` forever. That is what emptied every price on the storefront for
   92 minutes on 2026-09-03. Every XREADGROUP loop must detect `NOGROUP` and
   re-run its group creation inline — see `isNoGroupErr` in
   [`services/odds-publisher/internal/bus/consumer.go`](./services/odds-publisher/internal/bus/consumer.go)
   and the same branch in settlement's
   [`internal/extstream`](./services/settlement/internal/extstream/consumer.go)
   (the `settlement.external` consumer, which also creates its group from
   `0` rather than `$` so a recreate replays what is still on the stream
   instead of silently skipping unapplied settlements).
   Same lesson as the feed-source keys: Redis is a cache, not state.

   **Stream caps are a Redis-memory budget, not a burst buffer.** `odds.raw`
   is trimmed to 100k entries by BOTH producers (feed-ingester and
   fonbet-ingester must use the same value — MAXLEN is applied by whichever
   XADD runs); `settlement.external` to 20k. A producer with more to say than
   the consumer drains (fonbet-ingester's ~200k-price cold start) paces itself
   on the consumer group's lag (`bus.OddsBacklog` + `ingest.waitForOddsBacklog`,
   high-water 50k, bounded wait) instead of raising the cap: the first cut
   raised it to 400k, which at ~300 B per entry is half of the 256 MB
   `maxmemory` on its own.

8. **No emojis anywhere — UI text, logs, commit messages, code comments.**
   User requirement.

8a. **No data-provider names in bettor-facing UI.** Sportradar, Oddin,
   Fonbet and Bifrost are suppliers, and which supplier is behind a
   widget, a price or a stream is a backend fact — the storefront
   presents them as Oddzilla's own surfaces. The Sportradar chips beside
   the tracker / Head to Head / Live Table titles and the "Oddin HD"
   stream tab were removed on 2026-09-05 for exactly this reason; keep
   new widgets to a plain feature title. `/privacy` named Oddin too,
   under "Who we share it with" — that bullet was removed the same day
   because it was FACTUALLY WRONG, not merely off-brand: no bettor data
   reaches Oddin. We consume their feed and proxy their widget calls
   server-side; the only thing that ever touches them from a bettor is
   an HTTP request their embed makes, which is what the third-party
   media note covers, now phrased generically ("statistics and
   match-tracker widgets served by third-party data providers") so it
   also covers Sportradar, which it previously missed. Keep the real
   processors named there — Hetzner, Resend, Google/Firebase and the
   streaming platforms DO receive user data, and naming them is the
   disclosure's whole function. The one thing outside our reach is
   branding drawn INSIDE a third-party iframe. Admin pages are
   unaffected — an operator needs to know which feed a row came from.

9. **`markets.status` terminal codes (`-3` settled / `-4` cancelled) are
   sticky.** Once the settlement service flips a row to `-3` or `-4`, no
   subsequent `odds_change` from feed-ingester may revert it. Oddin's
   recovery snapshots replay older market state (a product=1 prematch
   snapshot received after an in-band `bet_settlement` will carry the
   same market with `status="1"` and an older `timestamp`); without this
   guard, settled markets get resurrected in the catalog with
   `market_outcomes.result` already decided — bettable at stale odds
   with a known winner. Enforced via the `CASE WHEN markets.status IN
   (-3,-4) THEN markets.status ELSE EXCLUDED.status END` branch on the
   `ON CONFLICT` clause in both [`UpsertMarket`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/markets.go)
   and `UpsertMarketsBulk`. Rollbacks (`rollback_bet_settlement`) go
   through `settlement`'s direct `SetMarketStatus` UPDATE (not the
   feed-ingester upsert path), so they correctly re-activate the row.
   Second-layer guard: bet placement
   ([`services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts))
   additionally rejects any leg with `market_outcomes.result IS NOT NULL`
   (`outcome_already_settled`), so a future desync between `markets.status`
   and `outcomes.result` can't be exploited to place at stale odds with
   a known winner.

10. **A provider may only ever flush its own catalog.** Every
   catalog-wide suspend must be scoped to the provider that owns the rows
   — `matches.provider_urn LIKE 'od:match:%'` for Oddin,
   `'fb:match:%'` for Fonbet. Two feeds now write the same
   `markets` / `market_outcomes` / `matches` tables, so an unscoped
   `WHERE markets.status = 1 AND ma.status IN ('not_started','live')`
   takes the OTHER provider's entire offer down as collateral.

   This is not theoretical. On 2026-09-04 the alive watchdog ran
   `store.FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog` — correctly, from its
   forced-Backup branch, because bifrost-feed briefly went unhealthy —
   and that flush predated the second provider and matched on match
   status alone. It suspended 116 079 markets and nulled 1 976 370
   outcome prices — about 104 000 of those markets Fonbet's — and every
   traditional sport went dark while Oddin's own esports offer stayed up
   on the backup feed. Note what the trigger was NOT: any watchdog fire
   does this, from either branch, so it needed no Oddin outage at all —
   a momentary blip in whichever source is currently feeding the
   catalogue was enough. Scoped now in both copies of that flush: the Go one in
   [`services/feed-ingester/internal/store/recovery.go`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/recovery.go)
   (all three steps — markets, outcomes, AND the `live` → `suspended`
   match flip) and the admin `POST /admin/feed/recovery` twin in
   [`services/api/src/modules/admin/feed.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/feed.ts).

   **The second half of the invariant is recovery.** A delta-based
   ingester cannot see a write it did not make: fonbet-ingester diffs each
   snapshot against an in-memory picture of what it last wrote, so with
   its own view still saying `status = 1` it had nothing to re-assert and
   the offer stayed dark until the container was restarted by hand. Any
   such service needs a divergence check —
   `Ingester.ReconcileExternalSuspend` in
   [`services/fonbet-ingester/internal/ingest/ingest.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/ingest/ingest.go)
   runs one `COUNT` a minute against `store.CountActiveProviderMarkets`
   and, when the DB holds under half the markets it believes are active,
   mirrors the flip in memory so the next cycle re-upserts everything
   the feed still quotes. Scoping the flush removes the known trigger;
   this bounds every unknown one (the admin endpoint, hand-run SQL, a
   third provider) to a minute of dark odds instead of until the next
   restart.

## Where things live

| Concern | Path |
| --- | --- |
| Fonbet sports feed (second provider) | [`services/fonbet-ingester/`](./services/fonbet-ingester/) — `internal/fonbet` (client + factor catalogue index), `internal/mapper` (snapshot → matches / markets / specifiers / outcome ids), `internal/ingest` (diff against the previous snapshot, pg + Redis writes, staleness suspend), `internal/store` + `internal/bus` (copies of the feed-ingester adapters, `provider='fonbet'`, `fb:` URNs). Storefront hook: `loadMatchWinnerOdds` in [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) accepts Fonbet match-winner rows (`provider_market_id >= 1_000_000` + outcome ids `1/2/3`) — **plus, since 2026-09-07, the head-to-head tables (399 / 25020) as a FALLBACK for a match with no such pair**. The mapper rewrites factor ids to the canonical `1`/`2` only on tables Fonbet flags `isMain` whose column captions are literally "1" / "2", and a head-to-head table is neither (its captions are the placeholders `%1` / `%2`), so a cycling stage duel had its ONE market — the winner of the whole fixture — priced on the match page and blank on every list card (measured: 14 matches on 2026-09-07). Widening the ingester's rule is the wrong lever and deliberately not taken: outcome ids ARE market identity, so re-keying live rows strands every open ticket on them (`twoWayWinner` in [`internal/settle/rules.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/rules.go) reads the shape for exactly that reason), and the api persists nothing. It is an ALLOWLIST rather than a shape rule because the shape does not separate the winner from the sideshow — "To win the toss" (496) and "Who will start the penalty shootout" (920) are two-way `%1` / `%2` tables too, and a toss price under a "Match winner" header is worse than no price. Which side is home comes from the feed's own outcome template (`outcome_descriptions.name_template` = the bare word `home` / `away`, which [`descriptions.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/mapper/descriptions.go) writes for a `%1` / `%2` caption and `renderOutcomeLabel` already turns into the rider's name on the match page) — never from row order or name-matching, and an outcome whose side does not resolve is skipped, since an inverted card quotes each rider at the other's price. **Site + language are a matched set of env vars, and `FONBET_LANG` is not display-only.** Defaults read `fon.bet` in English (`FONBET_SITE_ORIGIN` / `FONBET_URLS_JSON` / `FONBET_LINE_HOSTS` / `FONBET_COMMON_HOSTS` / `FONBET_LOGO_CDN` / `FONBET_SCOPE_MARKET=1600`); the Kazakhstan estate `fonbet.kz` needs all six moved together — `scopeMarket` is per site (each 404s on the other's value) and the static host lists are the trust anchor for `urls.json` discovery, so a half-move rejects every discovered host. The two sites are the SAME line: measured 2026-09-04, they share the event id space, the 965-node sports tree with the same 32 root ids, and byte-identical catalogues (580 tables, identical match-winner / param-kind / outcome-id derivation per factor), so switching site or language changes **no** `provider_market_id`, `outcome_id` or `specifiers_hash` and open tickets keep resolving. What language DOES change is the settlement grader's input: `internal/settle` reads the catalogue table names, the sub-event labels and the results feed (`locale`) in `FONBET_LANG`, and `rules.go` carries an English vocabulary only (the Russian one was removed 2026-09-06; `config.Load` refuses any other `FONBET_LANG`) — see the grader row below. **Tournament marks have TWO upstream sources and neither is a superset**, merged in [`ingest.ApplyLogos`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/ingest/logos.go) with `line/logos` winning: that endpoint's `competitions` map (keyed by segment id, so it joins straight onto `fb:tournament:<id>`), plus [`fonbet.Client.TournamentIcons`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/fonbet/logos.go), which reads the `tournamentInfos` block off the `events/list` snapshot we already fetch — a different asset tree (`/Logotypes/Tournament/` vs `/Logotypes/CompetitionLogos/`) and therefore free of an extra request. Widening the first source is NOT the lever: `competitions:"all"` returns 125 593 mappings against `"actual"`'s 767 and adds exactly **zero** logos, because every id we hold is already in the narrow answer — usually as the literal `"none"`. **Fonbet simply has no mark for about half its leagues** (measured 2026-09-05: 384 real marks over 761 live segments; England's Championship, League 1, League 2 and League Cup all return `"none"`) and falls back to the country flag — fon.bet's own Championship page draws `NewFlags/Circle/England.svg`. That fallback is deliberately NOT imported (`isFlagPath` drops it): the sidebar groups tournaments under a category header that already carries the country flag, so it would stamp the same flag down a whole England bucket. A logo-less tournament keeps `logo_url` NULL and renders no mark — see the sidebar row below for how the row still stays aligned. **Category spelling is canonicalised, because Fonbet has no category ids and the category IS a league-name prefix** — so the same competition splits into two sidebar buckets whenever Fonbet writes that prefix two ways, and it does. `categoryKey` + `canonicalCategories` in [`internal/mapper/mapper.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/mapper/mapper.go) fold case, punctuation and WORD ORDER (lowercase, punctuation to spaces, tokens sorted) and resolve every spelling in a group to the one on the **lowest Fonbet segment id**. That rule is chosen for stability, not tidiness: the winning name is what `Slugify` turns into `categories.slug`, the row's identity, so a name that flapped between cycles would keep minting category rows and stranding the operator's pin and hidden flag on the old one — a segment id is permanent, where "most segments wins" moves whenever Fonbet adds one and ties on the football group anyway (6 spellings each). Measured over the whole live line 2026-09-07: exactly **four** groups merge and no others — `UEFA Champions League` / `Champions League UEFA` (football, word order), `National teams` / `National Teams` (cricket), `Short-hockey` / `Short Hockey`, `Formula-1` / `Formula 1`. **Only the football one was ever a two-ROW split**, because `Slugify` already folded case and punctuation, so the other three shared a row all along and merely had a flapping display NAME (`EnsureCategory` overwrites the name, so the last spelling upserted won). Fonbet's own tree cannot supply this instead: all 12 Champions League segments have the ROOT SPORT as their parent (`parentId: 1`), so there is no intermediate node to read — the string is all there is. The limit: a genuine typo or one competition named in two languages still splits, and there is no operator-facing category merge to fall back on; the losing row of a merge is left with no tournaments, which renders nowhere (the sidebar builds buckets from `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments`, which selects FROM tournaments) but still lists on `/admin/categories` at 0 bookable. Protocol + mapping in [`docs/FONBET.md`](./docs/FONBET.md). |
| Fonbet settlement grader — vocabulary and rules | [`services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/rules.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/rules.go) + [`settle.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/settle/settle.go). The grader reads Fonbet's own text — catalogue table names, sub-event labels, results-feed statistic rows — and since 2026-09-06 carries an **English vocabulary only**: the Russian one that served the fonbet.kz era was removed (operator decision; the estate moved to fon.bet in English on 2026-09-04 and is not going back), and `config.Load` refuses any `FONBET_LANG` but `en` rather than let the grader run blind. Three vocabularies: (1) `unsafeTableWords` / `unsafePrefixWords` — the names `Grade` must refuse (overtime / shootout / penalt… / odd / even / exact / correct / series / minute), pinned by `TestTableUnsafeEnglish`; a threshold table called "Total missed penalties" looks exactly like an ordinary over/under to the shape checks, so the name guard is all that stands between it and a settlement off the goal score. (2) `periodEnPrefixRe` / `periodEnSuffixRe` — English marks the period at EITHER end of a label ("1st half corners" but "Yellow cards — 1st half"), and its unit list is only what the line emits (half / period / set / quarter / inning / map); plural "innings" is excluded because Fonbet uses it for a cumulative "first N innings" row. (3) `overtimeRows` / `shootoutRows` — the tie-breaking results rows ("extra time", "penalty shootouts"). **"Nth half" is ambiguous and is resolved per sport**, not guessed: `sportRule.half` maps football / futsal / handball / rugby / bandy → period N and basketball / american football / australian football → periods 2N-1 + 2N, and every other sport REFUSES the label (`ambiguous half label`). Aggregate specials stay out of scope by shape — "8 matches 1st half" parses a period but leaves a statistic name the results feed does not carry, so `scoreFor` reports no score. One known naming gap: football's "hit the woodwork" (the line and the results feed spell it differently; deliberately not papered over with fuzzy matching, since on a payout path a missed row costs an operator decision and a wrong match costs money). **Shapes added 2026-09-06** after the coverage measurement in [`docs/SETTLEMENT_COVERAGE_PLAN.md`](./docs/SETTLEMENT_COVERAGE_PLAN.md): both teams to score (nameless table 2800, read off its two factor labels — 3 774 open markets on the 09-05 fixtures), the plain two-way "To win the match" table (491, not flagged main by Fonbet, so recognised by shape: one row, no line, factor labels 1 / 2 — hockey's OT and shootout rows break the tie), and **sport rules for australian football, bandy, beach soccer, padel, MMA, boxing and darts** — the operator's temporary rules, the industry-standard convention for the few shapes Fonbet quotes on each, read off the 09-04 / 09-05 results documents. Fight sports are their own branch (`gradeFight`): the feed scores a bout "<round>:0" / "0:<round>", so the winner is the non-zero side and total rounds settle off the finishing round when that round decides the line; a finish IN the deciding round (needs the clock), a "0:0" and any handicap are refused. Darts is graded only when the results section says "legs" (`ScoreSet.Section`) — set-play headlines are sets and would grade a legs line wrongly. Every rule is pinned by `rules_test.go` / `rules_en_test.go`. The grader also writes the fixtures it cannot find in the results feed to `fonbet_settlement_misses` (migration `20260906T103343_settlement_operator_tools`) for the Unmatched results tab on `/admin/unsettled` — and the first hour of that table named the dominant miss: **606 fixtures created while the line was Russian, whose Cyrillic team names the English results feed can never spell** (33 630 open markets). For exactly those, `resultIndex.find` falls back to the (competition, start time) key when it is unambiguous on BOTH sides — one results row at the key, one fixture of ours in that tournament at that kick-off (`PendingMatch.SameSlot`), a postponed game's same-time neighbour making it two and refusing. Cyrillic-only on purpose: a general loosening would reopen the mirrored-row inversion the substring fallback was narrowed to avoid. Measured before shipping: 290 fixtures / 19 398 markets resolve, every sampled pair correct. |

Per-RPC `token` metadata key carries the Oddin access token. Graceful-idle: when `ODDIN_OBB_HOST` is empty the factory returns null and `/betbuilder/*` 503s `betbuilder_disabled`. |

Server bypasses the same-match guard, asserts every leg comes from one match, calls OBB `SessionInfo` to revalidate, then debits stake. Frozen `BetBuilderMeta` lands on `tickets.bet_meta`. |
