New outbox kinds `admin_outbound` (new conversation) and `admin_reply` (within existing thread) drain through the same LISTEN/NOTIFY worker; replies bump `last_outbound_at` + `outbound_count` on their thread. Threading resolves inbound messages via RFC 5322 In-Reply-To → outbox.provider_message_id / inbound.message_id → References chain (newest first) → normalised-subject fallback against active threads in the last 30 days. Webhook secret in path (`/webhooks/sendgrid-inbound/<secret>`, constant-time compared, 404 on miss); CSRF skipped for `/webhooks/*` since they're server-to-server with their own auth.), `0075_audit_subject_and_notes` (per-bettor audit visibility + operator notes — adds `admin_audit_log.subject_user_id UUID` (FK `users(id)` ON DELETE SET NULL) with a partial composite index `admin_audit_subject_idx (subject_user_id, created_at DESC) WHERE subject_user_id IS NOT NULL` so the new `GET /admin/users/:id/audit-log` filters in one indexed scan. Backfilled in-migration: every `target_type='user'` row gets `subject_user_id = target_id::uuid`; every `bettor_odds_adjustment_config` / `bettor_promo_visibility_config` row extracts the subject from the `user:<uuid>:…` prefix via `substring(target_id from 6 for 36)`. The audit-log hash chain (migration 0026) only hashes actor / action / target / before / after / ip / created_at — adding a column does NOT break the chain, the verifier is unaffected, and the new column is denormalised for query, not security. Also adds `users.notes TEXT` (CHECK ≤ 4000 chars) for free-text operator context surfaced on the RiskZilla bettor page; `PUT /admin/users/:id/notes` audit-logs as `user.notes_update`.), `0075_livesupport_chat` (live support chat between bettors and the backoffice — `support_threads` keyed by user with a partial unique index `support_threads_one_open_per_user` gating one open conversation per bettor at a time; denormalised `unread_user` / `unread_admin` counters on each row power the floating widget badge + the admin sidebar badge as O(1) reads (maintained inside the same tx as every message insert / mark-read so they can't drift away from the message log). `support_messages` is the append-only log with a `sender_kind` enum (`user` / `admin` / `system`) and a CHECK enforcing `sender_user_id IS NOT NULL` for any non-system row. Realtime fan-out rides the existing `user:{userId}` Redis pub/sub channel ws-gateway already forwards to authed sockets — no gateway changes; the front-end's shared WS dispatcher in `apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts` gains a `support_message` branch + `supportListeners` set, consumed by [`use-support-stream.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-support-stream.ts). User-facing routes at [`services/api/src/modules/support/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/support/routes.ts), admin routes at [`services/api/src/modules/admin/support.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/support.ts), floating widget at [`apps/web/src/components/support/support-widget.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/support/support-widget.tsx) mounted from (main) layout, admin pages at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/support/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/support/) with a sidebar entry under Operations polling `/admin/support/unread-count` every 60 s.), `0082_zillabuild` (pre-built BetBuilder combos — `zillabuild_config` singleton (master on/off + eligible `provider_market_id` allowlist + cards-per-map / map-count / legs range + min combined-odds floor + response cache TTL) and `zillabuild_cards` per-(match, map, slot) card compositions (`legs` jsonb = `[{marketId, outcomeId}]`; odds re-quoted from OBB on read, never stored). Also extends the `bettor_promo_kind` enum from migration 0071 with a third value `zillabuild` so ZillaBuild joins the per-bettor promo-visibility cascade.), `0083_analytics` (first-party FE analytics — `analytics_sessions` (client-generated per-tab UUID; links to a user on the first authed flush; denormalised view/click/event counters), `analytics_events` (append-only journey log — page_view / click / heartbeat / session_end with a per-session monotonic `seq`, apply-once via `UNIQUE (session_id, seq)` so pagehide double-delivery never double-counts), `analytics_mouse_batches` (sampled ~8 Hz mouse trails as `[[dtMs,x,y]]` JSONB per flush with viewport dims; same seq counter space). `kind` / `section` are open TEXT on purpose (new event kinds without a migration — zillapass predicate_key rationale). Retention enforced by an hourly Redis-locked sweep in the api: 90 d sessions + events, 14 d mouse batches.)., `0084_fe_market_groups` (custom market groups + configurable tab order on the match-detail page — new `fe_market_groups` table keyed by `(sport_id, scope)`. Custom groups carry `scope='custom_<key>'` (random 12-hex key, stable across renames) + a NOT NULL `label` and behave exactly like the curated `top` scope: their market list lives in `fe_market_display_order` under the same scope string and renders as an extra tab with the operator-authored label. Built-in scopes (`match` / `top` / `map_<N>`) get label-NULL anchor rows that only carry `display_order` when the admin reorders tabs — tabs WITH a row sort first by `display_order`, the rest keep the default order (Top, Match, Map 1..N), so a sport with zero rows is byte-identical to pre-0083 behaviour. The `fe_market_display_order` scope CHECK is relaxed to accept `custom_<key>`. Admin API: `/admin/fe-settings/market-groups/:sportId` (list / create / rename / delete / PUT order — creating the first group or saving an order seeds anchor rows for every built-in tab so new groups append at the end); UI at `/admin/fe-settings/markets-order/[sportId]/groups`, linked from the scope chip strip + the landing table. Storefront: `/catalog/matches/:id` loads `fe_market_groups` in its parallel batch, synthesises custom groups through the same representative-pick helper as Top, and sorts the group array once — the web client needs no changes since `scopeLabel()` already falls through to `group.label` for unknown ids.), `0085_boosted_odds` (Custom Boosted Odds — `boosted_odds_config` rules pinned to one scope each: sport / tournament / match / competitor (team) / market, with `boost_pct NUMERIC(5,2)` (Netwinstable key-delta pp, same math as ZillaFlash), optional `ends_at`, optional `min_risk_score` (bettors with `users.risk_score` below the threshold see standard prices; anonymous viewers count as the 1.000 default). Partial unique per (scope, ref). Resolution per market: market > match > competitor > tournament > sport, competitor ties resolve to the higher pct. Boosted prices are recomputed from live `published_odds` on every read — never stored. Also adds `ticket_selections.boost_rule_id uuid` (FK ON DELETE SET NULL) so a boosted leg records the rule that priced it and the bet-delay worker skips per-leg drift for it — `odds_at_placement` is deliberately above the raw published price), `0086_zillaboost_banner` (adds `boosted_odds_config.banner BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` — banner-enabled ZillaBoost rules surface on the storefront home page per scope: market → ZillaFlash-style offer card, match → scoreless match card with original + boosted match-winner prices, tournament → ZillaBoost banner linking to `/sport/:slug?tournament=N`, sport → **a home banner linking to `/sport/:slug` AND** the bolt icon next to the sport in the sidebar (banner added 2026-08-28 — before that, sport scope had the bolt only, so ticking "create promo banner" on a sport-wide boost appeared to do nothing; the lobby section also computed its "anything to show" total from tournament+match+market counts only, hiding the whole section for a sport-only boost). Sport + tournament banners deliberately carry NO odds — the scope spans every market of every match under it, so there is no single price to quote; the banner is a signpost and the list cards under it carry their own boosted prices. Neither is gated on `matchCount`; served by `GET /catalog/zillaboost-banners`, RS-gated per viewer), `0087_boosted_odds_outcome_scope` + `0088_boosted_odds_outcome_rules` (selection-level ZillaBoost — a rule may target ONE (market, outcome) cell instead of a whole market, so "Team Falcons to win map 2" can be boosted without moving the opposing price. 0087 does nothing but `ALTER TYPE boosted_odds_scope ADD VALUE 'outcome'` — deliberately its own file, because Postgres forbids REFERENCING a new enum value in the transaction that added it and the migration runner wraps each file in one tx, so 0088's `WHERE scope = 'outcome'` index predicate would fail with "unsafe use of new value of enum type". 0088 adds `outcome_id text` (reusing `market_id` for the other half of the `market_outcomes` PK), rebuilds `boosted_odds_scope_consistency` with the new tier (every pre-existing scope additionally pins `outcome_id IS NULL`), and adds the partial unique `boosted_odds_outcome_uniq (market_id, outcome_id) WHERE scope='outcome'` — which leaves `boosted_odds_market_uniq` untouched, so a market can carry a market rule AND per-outcome rules at once. **No FK on `(market_id, outcome_id)`** on purpose: validating one takes SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `market_outcomes`, which the feed writes to continuously, and the lock queue behind it is the failure mode 0023 was added to fix; the admin route checks the row exists on write and an outcome that later leaves the market just stops resolving. Math is `boostSelectionKeys` (netwinstable.ts): the delta comes out of the boosted outcome's own implied probability, clamped both by the fair-book floor and by `SELECTION_BOOST_MAX_PROB_SHARE` (half of that outcome's probability — the fair-book clamp alone can't bound one cell, since a market's headroom can exceed a longshot's entire probability and drive its price to infinity), with scarce headroom shared proportionally across co-boosted selections so the result is order-independent. A selection rule REPLACES any coarser rule on its market rather than stacking — composing them would push the book past fair). `0089_zillaboost_graphics_banner` (AI-generated ZillaBoost banner art — `boosted_odds_config.graphics_banner` flag + `zillaboost_banner_image_jobs` pull queue (rule_id PK, pending/done/failed, 15-min claim lease, hourly failure backoff, image BYTEA + mime on the job row so the hot rule-table selects never drag bytes). Drained by the operator-PC worker `services/zillaboost-banner-gen` via `/webhooks/banner-gen/:secret/*`; PC off = jobs wait), `0090_banner_gen_prompt` + `0091_banner_gen_render_meta` (`zillaboost_banner_image_jobs.last_prompt text` + `last_render_meta jsonb` — the diffusion prompt and the params behind it (checkpoint / latent class / cfg / steps / sampler / size / **seed** / negative). Image quality is iterated by changing prompts, and before these the prompt existed only in the operator PC's worker log, so "why does this Dota banner have soldiers in it" was unanswerable from the backoffice. The seed is the reproducibility handle. Both nullable + optional in the upload route so an older worker build still completes jobs; `/fail` records them too, COALESCEd so a failure report can't erase what a previous attempt stored), `0092_boosted_odds_starts_at` (**scheduled boosts** — `boosted_odds_config.starts_at timestamptz`; NULL = live immediately, which is what every pre-existing row means, so no backfill. A rule is deliverable only INSIDE its window, and the two halves are one shared predicate — [`boostWindowIsOpen()`](./services/api/src/lib/boosted-odds.ts) for SQL, `ruleWindowIsOpen()` in memory — because a reader that checked only `ends_at` would price, display, and PAY OUT a boost scheduled for next week. All four readers use it: both pricing loaders, `validateCustomBoostForBet` (rejects `boosted_odds_not_started`), and the banners endpoint. `boosted_odds_window_order` CHECK enforces `starts_at < ends_at` independently of the route. Deliberately NOT gated: the graphics job queue — generating artwork ahead of the start time is the point, so the image is ready when the boost goes live), `0093_boosted_odds_competitor_markets` (**team boost market span** — `boosted_odds_config.competitor_markets text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'all'` with CHECK `IN ('all','team_only')`. `all` = every market of the team's matches (the original behaviour, so the default leaves every existing row unchanged); `team_only` = only the team's own outcome in team-shaped markets (`provider_market_id IN (1,4)`), priced through the selection path so the opponent's price never moves. Meaningful only for `scope='competitor'` — left at the default and ignored elsewhere, the same convention `outcome_id` follows, rather than a CHECK that would need rebuilding every time a scope tier is added). `0094_tournament_risk_tier_lock` (**manual risk tier** — `tournaments.risk_tier_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`. TRUE when an operator assigned `risk_tier` on `/admin/tournaments`; feed-ingester's `UpdateTournamentRiskTier` skips locked rows so the REST refresh and the offline backfill never overwrite it. Exists because the Bifrost backup feed carries no risk tier: a tournament first seen while Oddin's meta API is down would otherwise sit at NULL and RiskZilla would price it off the tier-0 fallback. Default FALSE keeps every existing row on today's behaviour). `0095_feed_control` (**durable feed source switch** — singleton `feed_control` row: `source` auto/prod/backup, `switched_at`, `switched_by`, feed-ingester's `flushed_at` acknowledgement and `applied_source`/`applied_at`. Replaced the Redis `feed:source*` keys the same day they shipped, because `allkeys-lru` evicted them when the backup stream filled Redis and a forced Backup silently reverted to Auto). `0096_riskzilla_velocity_decision` + `0097_bet_intent_velocity` + `0098_behaviour_scores` (**bot controls**, 2026-09-03 — the answer to "can placement be restricted to a real click?" is no, so these make automation gain nothing and get noticed. 0096 adds the `rejected_velocity` value to `riskzilla_decision` in its own file (enum-in-same-tx rule, as 0087). 0097 adds the `riskzilla_bot_controls` singleton (intent token required / TTL, `min_human_ms`, velocity on/off + base caps for bets and distinct matches per minute, behaviour alert threshold + min sessions) and `tickets.quote_to_place_ms` — ms between the placement intent being issued and `POST /bets` landing. 0098 adds `analytics_sessions.behaviour_score / behaviour_features / behaviour_scored_at` plus the `bettor_behaviour_scores` per-bettor rollup with `alert` / `alert_since` / `acknowledged_at`.) `0099_feed_control_fonbet` (**Fonbet feed on/off switch** — adds `fonbet_enabled BOOLEAN NULL` (NULL = follow the `FONBET_ENABLED` env default; TRUE/FALSE = the operator's position, wins over env, survives restarts and deploys), `fonbet_switched_at` / `fonbet_switched_by`, and fonbet-ingester's acknowledgement `fonbet_applied_enabled` / `fonbet_applied_at` to the `feed_control` singleton. Written by `PUT /admin/feed/fonbet` (audit-logged `feed.fonbet_switch`), read every 2 s by fonbet-ingester's switch watcher: OFF suspends every Fonbet market (status -1, prices kept) and stops polling; ON boots the feed and re-activates whatever Fonbet still quotes. Card on `/admin/feed` via `GET /admin/feed/fonbet-status`, which also reads the service's Redis status hash `fonbet:feed:status`). `0100_sportradar_match_map` (**the third id space** — `match_sportradar_ids` (match_id PK → `matches`, `sr_match_id`, `sr_sport_id`, `status` candidate/confirmed/rejected, `source` admin/auto, `confidence`, `evidence` jsonb, reviewer + timestamps) plus the read-only view `match_external_ids` presenting every id we hold for a match in one shape. **The Oddin and Fonbet halves are DERIVED, not stored**: `matches.id` is already our own id for every fixture from every feed, `matches.provider_urn` already carries the provider's, and both prefixes are exactly 9 characters, so the view slices them with `substring(provider_urn FROM 10)` — copying them into a table could only ever drift. Sportradar is the only one that needs a row, because it is the only one no feed carries (Fonbet's events have no external-id field at all; Sportradar's gismo feed 403s off a licensed origin). `od:tournament:%` rows in `matches` (587 on 2026-09-04 — the auto-mapper's outright placeholders) are deliberately outside the view: they are not fixtures and have no Sportradar counterpart. The partial unique index on `sr_match_id` excludes `rejected`, so a tombstoned pair never blocks the correct match from claiming that id; a CHECK pins `confidence` to [0,1] and another pins reviewer-and-timestamp together. The file opens with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` because the FK takes a brief SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `matches`, which both ingesters write to continuously — failing the deploy beats queueing every feed write behind it.) `0101_sportradar_map_source_llm` (**adds `llm` to `sportradar_map_source`** — its own file, because Postgres forbids REFERENCING a new enum value in the transaction that added it, the same rule migration 0087 follows. The matcher queues what it cannot settle and nearly all of that turns out to be one provider abbreviating the other — Sportradar writes `Ipswich` where Fonbet writes `Ipswich Town`, two tokens against one, which scores 0.667 and drags an otherwise perfect pair (0-minute kickoff delta, identical away team) under the auto-confirm bar. No string metric fixes that: the missing ingredient is knowing that Ipswich Town is called Ipswich. A language model adjudicates the queue, and this value records that it was the deciding party — so the desk can show who decided, and so a bad batch is revertible with `WHERE source = 'llm'` without touching a matcher or human decision.) `0102_category_hidden_from_lists` (**a category can be carried in the tree but kept out of the match lists** — adds `categories.hidden_from_lists BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`. Fonbet files EA FC simulations ("FC 26. ESportsBattle. La Liga. 2x4 min.") under the real Football sport, so 184 of ~1770 bookable Football matches — and 10 of 23 live ones — were computer-played 2x4-minute games sitting above the actual football offer; same shape as NBA 2K26 under Basketball and NHL 26 under Ice Hockey. A flagged category is dropped from the lobby, `/live`, `/upcoming`, the sport page's default view and the per-sport live badge, while STAYING in the sidebar tree — any explicit narrowing (`?category=`, `?tournament=`, `?team=`) drops the predicate, so the offer is one click away rather than gone. Seeds `TRUE` for `name ~ '^FC [0-9]{2}$'` (exactly `FC 24` + `FC 26` on the current line, verified against production before shipping); every other simulation category is an operator call on `/admin/categories`. Deliberately NOT `categories.active = false` — these matches are real, bettable and settle normally, so the merchandising decision and the kill switch stay independent). `0103_catalog_display_order` (**operator pin ordering for sports and categories** — adds a nullable `display_order INTEGER` to BOTH `sports` and `categories`. Until this, the sport rail was ordered by a hard-coded flagship list (cs2, dota2, lol, valorant) then alphabetically, and the sidebar's category buckets purely alphabetically, with no operator input at all — so Football's tree opened on Albania and the rail could not put Football above an esport the operator no longer leads with. NULL means unpinned, which is what every existing row gets, so ordering is byte-identical to before until an operator pins something; a pinned row sorts by this value ascending ahead of every unpinned one, and unpinned rows keep the old rule among themselves. **Scope differs by table and that is the point**: sports are one global sequence (a sport has no parent), categories one sequence PER SPORT (a category only ever renders inside one sport's tree, so “second from the top” is a statement about Football). The pinned set is stored dense 1..N and **renumbered on every action**, which is why the write goes through [`reorderPinned`](./services/api/src/lib/pin-order.ts) — a pure transform of the ordered id list, unit-tested — rather than arithmetic on one row: it makes the sequence self-healing against gaps and duplicates, and “up” / “down” are only definable relative to the other pinned rows anyway. **No index**: both tables are small enough (under a hundred sports, a few thousand categories) that an index would cost writes to serve a sort that is free. **No unique constraint** either — a deferred one would fight the renumber, and the single writer keeps the invariant. Endpoints `POST /admin/sports/:id/order` and `POST /admin/categories/:id/order` take `{action: top|up|down|clear}`, are audit-logged (`sport.order_update` / `category.order_update`, with the resulting `pinnedOrder` in `after`), and bust the caches their surface reads (`catalog:sports:v1` / `catalog:tournaments:v1:<sportId>`) so a reorder is not invisible for the cache TTL. Both take **every lock in one primary-key-ordered statement** — locking the clicked row and then the pinned set is two acquisitions in click-dependent order, which is exactly how two admins reordering the same scope deadlock each other). `0104_tournament_display_order` (**pin ordering for tournaments** — nullable `display_order` on `tournaments`, completing the three-level tree started by 0103. Scope is the CATEGORY, matching where the rows render: a tournament only appears inside one bucket, so “second from the top” is a statement about England. NULL = unpinned, so the tree is byte-identical until an operator pins something; same dense 1..N renumber, same single-statement primary-key-ordered lock, same absence of an index and a unique constraint as 0103). `0105_drop_russian_duplicate_categories` (**cleanup of the feed language switch** — the Fonbet line was read from fonbet.kz in Russian until 2026-09-04; moving to fon.bet in English created a fresh category row per country and orphaned the Russian one beside it (“Швеция” next to “Sweden”). Measured before writing: 211 such categories, 197 holding no tournament at all, the other 14 carrying 18 tournaments / 49 matches / 1054 markets — **none of it live offer** (every one of those matches has zero markets at status 1, so `hasActiveMarket` already hid them) with 0 settlements and 0 ticket_selections underneath. **Only the 197 EMPTY ones are deleted, and the FK is why**: the first cut deleted on the Cyrillic name alone and failed at deploy with `matches_tournament_id_fkey` — categories cascade to tournaments, but `matches.tournament_id` carries no on-delete action, so a tournament holding matches cannot be removed. That constraint is correct (a match is what tickets, settlements and odds history hang off, and it must not vanish because somebody tidied a category), so the migration was narrowed to fit it rather than the constraint being worked around; the transaction rolled back cleanly and nothing was half-applied. The remaining 14 stay in `/admin/categories` — they carry no bettable offer, so they cost a row in a list, and removing them means deleting real match rows with their own FK chain (`match_sportradar_ids`, `feed_messages`, `markets`, `odds_history` partitions), which deserves its own deliberate change. The `NOT EXISTS` ticket guard is kept even at a count of zero because the DELETE runs at deploy time, not authoring time). `0106_tournament_risk_tier_source` (**who decided a tournament's risk tier** — `tournaments.risk_tier_source` (`auto` / `manual` / `zagi`, CHECK-constrained TEXT rather than an enum so a fourth reviewer needs no two-file dance) plus `risk_tier_note`, `risk_tier_reviewed_at` and `risk_tier_attempts`. Until this there were two provenances and one bit to express them: `risk_tier_locked` (0094) meant an operator typed it, everything else was "auto" — which lumped *feed-assigned* together with *nobody has ever looked*, and 1 231 of 1 870 tournaments were the second kind. **The direction of the risk is what shapes the whole feature**: RiskZilla prices a NULL tier at `UNTIERED_RISK_TIER = 10`, the STRICTEST row in `riskzilla_settings` (50 USDC match liability against tier 1's 50 000), so an untiered competition is never over-exposed — it is invisible and under-traded, and every tier assigned to it LOOSENS the book. There is no cautious-by-omission assignment here, which is why the reviewer clamps in code rather than trusting the prompt and why an unparseable reply writes nothing. `risk_tier_attempts` is bounded retry (max 3) so a name the model will not judge stops being re-sent forever. No index — under 2 000 rows, same reasoning as 0103; `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` because ADD COLUMN takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on a table both ingesters write to, and **a pre-deploy or 03:00-cron `pg_dump` holds AccessShareLock on every table for minutes**, so this migration aborts the deploy cleanly rather than queueing the catalog behind itself — retry once the dump finishes). `0106_fe_market_scope_sub_events` (**the match page's Fonbet sub-event tabs became configurable** — relaxes the `scope` CHECK on `fe_market_display_order` and `fe_market_groups` to accept `fb_<kinds>`: one scope per sub-event, id taken from the `variant` specifier (`fb:400100/10100201` → `fb_400100_10100201`), every per-player variant collapsing into `fb_players`. Those tabs — 1st half, Corners, Yellow cards, Players — have been on the storefront since the Fonbet line landed, but the backoffice enumerated a FIXED esports tab set instead, so it offered football Match plus Map 1..5, which no football fixture has ever had, while the tabs bettors were actually looking at could be neither ordered nor reordered. **The tab set is not stored anywhere** — the match page derives it per request from the markets it is about to render — so [`services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts) re-derives it over the sport's CURRENT offer (open matches, markets in a status the match page renders; the whole-table alternative reads ~17M rows in ~9 s and answers a different question — tabs from fixtures that finished months ago). Grammar and derivation live in ONE shared module, [`packages/types/src/market-scope.ts`](./packages/types/src/market-scope.ts), read by the api and the web admin and mirrored by these CHECKs, because a backoffice that disagrees with the storefront configures a tab nobody sees. Additive: no row changes, and a sport with no configuration keeps the order it has today). `0107_zagi_tier_safety_and_outright` (**the two operator tightenings, applied retroactively** — the first ZAGI sweep had already tiered 1 211 tournaments when the operator asked for a standing +1 safety margin on every machine verdict and a further +3 for outright markets. Both only ever raise the tier number, so this is a pure de-risking pass. The outright predicate mirrors `OUTRIGHT_PATTERNS` in the reviewer and was measured against the live catalogue first — deliberately NOT matching bare `head-to-head` (12 of 31 such rows are single-event: `Vuelta a Espana. Stage 13`, `Formula-1 … Race`) nor `champion` (`Scotland. Championship` is a league, not a market). Scope is `risk_tier_source = 'zagi'` only: an operator's tier is a human decision and a feed tier is Oddin's own, and neither is touched. **The `+1 ZAGI safety margin` marker in `risk_tier_note` is load-bearing, not decoration** — it is the statement's own idempotency key, so a row can never be stepped twice; verified against production in a rolled-back transaction where a second run touched 0 rows. Effect measured there: `England. Premier League. Season 26/27` T1 → T5, `Italy. Serie A. Season 26/27` T2 → T6, no ZAGI row left at T1). `0108_tournament_logo_provenance` (**where a tournament's logo came from** — `tournaments.logo_source` (`fonbet` / `wikidata` / `liquipedia` / `manual`, CHECK'd TEXT), `logo_source_url`, `logo_attempts` (bounded retry, max 3) and `logo_checked_at`. Backfills the 462 existing rows as `fonbet`, which is provably right because the feed is the only writer of tournament logos to date and every stored URL points at their CDN. **Provenance is load-bearing here rather than nice to have**: the remaining ~1 400 marks have to come from third parties, Liquipedia's are largely NON-FREE, and a single `WHERE logo_source = 'liquipedia'` has to be enough to undo the lot. Measured before writing: naive lookup is wrong often enough to matter — Wikidata's "EuroLeague" resolves to `EuroLeagueWomen.png` and Finland's "Liiga" to `Naisten Liiga logo.png` (the MEN'S entities carry no logo claim, so a sport-only filter falls through to the women's competition), and Liquipedia's search for "PGL Wallachia" returns the page **Team Falcons**, whose infobox image is a TEAM crest. A wrong badge is worse than a blank one). `0109_revert_season_outright_step` (**undoes a shipped mistake in 0107** — its outright pattern set included `season NN/NN`, which reads like an outright and is not: Fonbet names the CURRENT SEASON OF A LEAGUE that way. Measured after the fact, `England. Premier League. Season 26/27` carried 12 fixtures and 812 active markets, `Italy. Serie A. Season 26/27` 16 and 1 102, `Russia. Premier League. Season 26/27` 13 and 1 170 — those are the leagues themselves. Stepping them cut the per-match budget on the biggest football markets in the book by three tiers (Serie A from ~10 000 USDC a match to ~1 000) and pushed the Premier League BELOW the Championship in the sidebar's tier ordering, which is how it surfaced. **The pattern had been validated against NAMES and never against what the rows contained** — a genuine outright has 0-2 active markets and no fixtures under it, against a league's hundreds, and that is the signal to check. Reverses exactly the +3 on the eight season-named leagues, identified by the `+3 outright` marker 0107 wrote, and rewrites the marker so it cannot be applied twice; the real outrights (`Head-to-head in the tournament`) keep theirs. An audit across every category for the same inversion — a lower division out-ranking its own top flight — found no other instance). `0109_fe_market_order_variant` (**a curated tab picks a market, not a market TYPE** — adds `variant TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` to `fe_market_display_order` and re-keys its unique to `(sport_id, scope, provider_market_id, variant)`. `provider_market_id` is the catalogue TABLE, and Fonbet reuses one table across every sub-event, so football's ~470 markets collapse to 14 ids: the Top picker offered "Total" once, with no way to feature "Corners: Total" or "1st half: Match result" at all. The empty string keeps its original meaning — ANY copy, resolved by the representative pick the storefront already does — so every pre-existing row means what it always did and nothing is backfilled; an esports row for a market that only ever appears with an Oddin variant (`way:two`, `mr:12`) keeps resolving. Meaningful only for the curated scopes: a feed tab IS one sub-event, so its rows leave it empty, the same convention `boosted_odds_config.outcome_id` follows for its one scope tier. Storefront: `buildCuratedGroup` filters candidates by variant when the row sets one and dedupes the result, since a wildcard row and an explicit one can resolve to the same market; the inline Top market on LIST cards de-dupes by id instead, because a card renders one market with no tab to say which sub-event it is). `0110_drop_live_chat` (**removes the live match-room chat**, migration 0045 — `live_chat_messages`, `live_chat_picks` and the `live_chat_message_kind` enum. The feature came out whole on 2026-09-06 (API module, ws-gateway fan-out dimension, the rail's Chat tab, the "N watching" pill) and will be rebuilt from a fresh design rather than resumed, so the rows are deleted rather than orphaned: nothing else in the system references them — no ticket, settlement, ledger or audit row — and the ephemeral half of the feature (reactions, viewer counts, the last-50 message cache) only ever lived in Redis. Takes `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` because DROP TABLE needs ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on each table it REFERENCES in order to remove the FK trigger, and `matches` is written continuously by both ingesters — the same reason 0100 and 0106 carry it. `IF EXISTS` throughout so a database created after this lands applies it as a no-op). `20260906T103343_settlement_operator_tools` (**the two tables behind the settlement-coverage work of 2026-09-06** — landed as `0111_...` and renamed to a UTC timestamp the same day, because numbering was frozen at 0110 and `pnpm db:check-migrations` refuses a new numbered prefix; the rename is safe because every statement in the file is idempotent (`CREATE TABLE / INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, seed `INSERT`s with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), so re-running it under the new name is a no-op and the old `_migrations` row is left behind as a harmless orphan. **That re-run has since happened and was clean, so this is measured rather than predicted**: the `54d78d0` deploy on 2026-09-06 at 18:39Z reported `migrations=2` — this file under its new name, plus `custom_events` — and three deploys have landed on top of it. Production therefore carries BOTH rows, and neither needs removing: deleting the `0111_` one would buy nothing and is the kind of hand-run DELETE against `_migrations` that has no safe general form. That rename was only free because the runner keys on filename with no checksum AND the file happened to be idempotent — the general rule against renaming an applied migration still stands, [`docs/SETTLEMENT_COVERAGE_PLAN.md`](./docs/SETTLEMENT_COVERAGE_PLAN.md) — `fonbet_market_denylist`: the Fonbet catalogue tables (`kind='table'`, full `provider_market_id`) and sub-event label prefixes (`kind='label_prefix'`) the ingester must not turn into markets because no grader can settle them from the results feed, seeded with 1007800 (winner of point N in a set), 1004500 / 1004551 (winner of game N in a set), `Player specials` and `Special bets` — 36% of the Fonbet markets still open after their match had closed on 09-05 were these shapes; a CHECK pins each kind to its column and two partial unique indexes stop duplicates. `fonbet_settlement_misses`: one row per pending Fonbet match the grader could not find in the results feed, with the `candidates` the document DID list for the same competition, upserted every pass and deleted the pass it is found — before this the grader logged `no_result: 797` and nothing said which fixture. `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` for the FKs on `matches` / `users`, same reason 0100 and 0106 carry it). `20260906T133515_custom_events` (**operator-authored offer that comes from no feed** — `markets.custom_name`, plus `custom_market_config` (overround + liability-trading settings per market) and `custom_outcome_config` (the operator's own probability per outcome). Seeds the `Custom` sport (`cu:sport:1`, `kind='traditional'`), the category `Limburg Barbara` and the tournament `Simakov`, all idempotently, since the backoffice manages them from there. **The events themselves are ORDINARY rows in `matches` / `markets` / `market_outcomes`** — that is the whole design, and it is what makes the bet slip, RiskZilla, bet-delay, cashout, ZillaBoost, community tickets and settlement work on them with no changes, because none of those care which provider filled the tables. A separate "custom bets" table would have meant reimplementing the betting stack for a handful of rows. Three consequences are load-bearing: URNs are `cu:` so both feeds' catalog-wide flushes (invariant 10) leave them alone — an Oddin outage must not suspend the operator's own book; `provider_market_id` is ONE shared constant (2 000 000, above Fonbet's base) because that column is a market TYPE everywhere else (`riskzilla_market_factors` keys a multiplier off it, `fe_market_display_order` orders tabs by it), so a per-market id would make all of that per-market and unusable — two custom markets on one event are told apart by the `custom` specifier instead; and settlement goes out on the existing `settlement.external` Redis stream, the same provider-neutral path fonbet-ingester uses, so custom payouts run through the same apply-once settler with **no Go changes**. `custom_outcome_config.base_probability` is kept APART from `market_outcomes.probability` on purpose: the traded probability is written to the latter (where cashout, ZillaTips and the storefront read it) while the former stays the anchor each repricing blends from — without it every pass would compound on the last and the operator's view would be lost after one bet). `20260906T015446_combozilla_config` (**ComboZilla becomes configurable** — the lobby's prebuilt 3-fold carousel had its whole selection policy hard-coded in the web builder: tournaments at risk tier 1..3 only, and only cs2 / dota2 / lol allowed more than one card. That band mattered more once ZillaAGI tiered the traditional line, because its standing +1 margin means a machine verdict can never yield a T1 and most football sits at T4-T6, so the Fonbet offer was invisible to the carousel by construction. Two tables: `combozilla_config` singleton (`enabled`, `eligible_risk_tiers smallint[]` CHECK'd to 1..10, `allow_untiered`, `multi_card_sport_slugs text[]`) whose defaults reproduce the old constants exactly, and `combozilla_scope_rules` — `allow` / `block` at sport / category / tournament scope, one typed FK per tier with ON DELETE CASCADE, a scope-consistency CHECK and a partial unique index per scope, the same shape as `riskzilla_live_delay_config`. Resolution is most-specific-wins (tournament > category > sport > tier default), and **`allow` is unconditional** — it admits the scope regardless of tier, because anything already eligible needs no rule and that is the only meaning "manually add" can have. `scope` and `mode` are CHECK'd TEXT, not enums, so a fourth scope is one ALTER. Carries `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` for the FKs on the three catalog tables both ingesters write to). `20260906T230247_bettor_labels` (**operator labels on bettors** — `users.labels TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, closed vocabulary `vip` / `sharp` / `regular` / `fraud` / `shady` / `suspicious` / `prematch` / `live` enforced by the `users_labels_allowed` CHECK and mirrored in `packages/types/src/bettor-labels.ts` — the two are pinned together by [`bettor-labels.test.ts`](./packages/types/src/bettor-labels.test.ts), which parses the CHECK straight out of the migration file, so adding a value in one place and not the other fails the build rather than the insert; partial GIN index `users_labels_gin_idx` serves the `?label=` containment filter on `GET /admin/users`. Descriptive only — nothing in the placement path reads labels; the levers stay `risk_score` (surfaced on the admin bettor card as the **risk factor**, 0.1–10 in 0.1 steps, 1 = 100%), `global_limit_micro` and `bet_delay_seconds`, all edited inline on `/admin/users/:id`), `20260906T230248_risk_alerts` (**alert center** — `risk_alert_rules` (one row per rule kind: enabled, severity, jsonb params, seeded with 14 B2C sportsbook rules), `risk_alerts` (the queue: kind, severity `critical|serious|warning`, status `open|acknowledged|resolved`, title/body, `dedupe_key` with a partial unique index over unresolved rows as the sweeper's ON CONFLICT target, subject bettor / ticket / match, amount, payload, assignee, ack/resolve stamps, `occurrences` + first/last seen) and `risk_alert_events` (append-only trail: created / acknowledged / assigned / comment / resolved / reopened). A resolved key never re-fires; persistent conditions bucket their key by time. Advisory only — nothing in the placement path reads these). **Both were authored as `0104_` / `0105_` and renamed to timestamps before merge** — those numbers are taken on main and applied on production, and `pnpm db:check-migrations` refuses a new numbered prefix at all since the freeze at 0110. That rename was free only because the PR was still open; see invariant #5. No migration for settlements retention (2026-07-03) — it's purely a cron: [`infra/hetzner/backup/settlements_retention.sh`](./infra/hetzner/backup/settlements_retention.sh) (`45 3 * * *`) deletes `settle`/`cancel` rows older than 45 days gated on the market having no open (`pending_delay`/`accepted`) ticket; `rollback_*` rows are kept forever. See invariant #3 and [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md) "settlements retention". |
| Drizzle schema | [`packages/db/src/schema/`](./packages/db/src/schema/) |
| Seed script | [`packages/db/src/seed.ts`](./packages/db/src/seed.ts) — seed sport URNs are `od:sport:{3,2,1,13}` matching real Oddin feed (do NOT revert to synthetic `od:sport:cs2` etc. — it breaks auto-mapping) |
| Money helpers | [`packages/types/src/money.ts`](./packages/types/src/money.ts) |
| Odds display formatting + the quote ladder | [`packages/types/src/odds.ts`](./packages/types/src/odds.ts) — `quoteOnLadder` / `ladderStep` / `LADDER_FLOOR`, `formatOddsDisplay` + `ODDS_PLACEHOLDER`, and `isBettableOdds` / `MIN_BETTABLE_ODDS` (`odds > 1`). **A price is snapped DOWN onto a quote ladder before it is rendered** — 0.01 rungs below 10, then 0.1 / 0.5 / 1 / 5 as it lengthens — and **prices under `LADDER_FLOOR` (1.01) keep all four decimals**. That split is the whole point: near 1.00 the extra digits are a real price (Oddin genuinely quotes a near-certain live favorite at 1.003, and both 1.00 and 1.01 are prices that do not exist), while at 5.1410 they are the feed's arithmetic rendered verbatim, which no book prints. The 4dp rule shipped for the first case and was applied to the whole range, so a live CS2 map reached the storefront on 2026-09-06 as `5.1410 / 3.6860` and `1.3095 / 5.1410 / 5.1410`; the ladder was added 2026-09-07. Flooring means a price only ever moves toward the house — the same convention `quoteOnLadder` in [`custom-events.ts`](./packages/types/src/custom-events.ts) has followed since 2026-09-06, and the two ladders are **byte-identical and duplicated on purpose** (both modules are pulled into apps/web as VALUES and must stay free of relative imports — see the `oddzilla-types-barrel-imports` footgun), pinned against each other by a sweep in [`odds.test.ts`](./packages/types/src/odds.test.ts). **The ladder is applied in SIX places and they must not diverge** — a mismatch between the displayed price and the drift reference surfaces as bets rejected for phantom drift. The load-bearing one is Go `formatPublishedOdds` in [`odds-publisher`](./services/odds-publisher/internal/publisher/publisher.go), whose string IS `market_outcomes.published_odds`, so laddering there is what makes the catalog payload, the storefront, the WS tick, the drift check and `ticket_selections.odds_at_placement` agree by construction. The other five ladder their own inputs (a per-bettor adjustment multiplies straight off the rungs) and are idempotent no-ops otherwise: `formatOdds` in [`catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts), `formatOddsTrimNum` in [`bettor-odds-adjustment.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/bettor-odds-adjustment.ts), [`ws-gateway/bettor-adjustment.ts`](./services/ws-gateway/src/bettor-adjustment.ts), Go `formatOddsTrim` in [`bet-delay/adjust.go`](./services/bet-delay/internal/worker/adjust.go), and `formatOddsDisplay` itself. The two Go ports work in units of 1e-4 (exact integer division, no float dust); `odds.test.ts` pins the TS float form against that integer form across a sweep. `isBettableOdds` is the shared "is this cell clickable" rule, mirroring the `authNum <= 1` reject in `POST /bets` so the UI never offers a price placement would refuse. Import via the `@oddzilla/types/odds` subpath, never the barrel. Do NOT use `formatBoostedOdds` (netwinstable.ts) for general display — that one floors to 2dp on purpose because a boosted quote is agreed to 2dp at placement. |
