| API route modules | [`services/api/src/modules/`](./services/api/src/modules/) — `auth`, `users`, `wallet`, `bets`, `catalog`, `admin/{routes,odds-config,tickets,withdrawals,dashboard,users,audit,feed,wedged-matches,logs,fe-settings,competitors,community}`, `community` (Phase 10.1–10.3 — `GET /community/feed?sort=recent\|best&currency=&sport=`, `GET /community/users/:nickname/{profile,tickets}`, `GET /community/me`, `PATCH /community/me/{visibility,profile}`, `POST /community/copy/:communityTicketId`; admin `POST /admin/community/backfill`), `widgets` (Oddin Disir proxy — `GET /widgets/match/:id/prematch`, `GET /widgets/tournament/:id/prematch`, `GET /widgets/match/:id/live`; returns `{url}` for the storefront iframe `src`. The `x-brand-token` is held only by the api process and never reaches the browser. URLs cache in Redis for 120s with key `disir:url:{kind}:{env}:{urn}:{qs}`. When `DISIR_BRAND_TOKEN` is empty, all three routes 503 `widget_disabled` and the storefront silently skips rendering — same gracefully-degrades pattern wallet-watcher uses without an RPC URL.). `users` accepts `?roles=admin,support` (CSV) for the Admin user-management view. `logs` powers the sport→tournament→match browser with per-match odds-history + raw feed-message endpoints. `fe-settings` exposes storefront-display knobs (currently per-sport market order). `competitors` exposes team-branding management (`GET /admin/competitors`, `PATCH /admin/competitors/:id`, `POST /admin/competitors/bulk-logos`). |
| ZillaTips / ZillaFacts visibility (operator cascade) | Migration `20260907T093356_insight_widget_rules` + schema [`insight-widgets.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/insight-widgets.ts). **Both widgets were unconfigurable until 2026-09-07** — no page, no route, no record anywhere of whether they were on; turning one off meant editing code, and the first stopgap was a pair of env vars needing a container recreate. One table serves both, keyed by `widget`, the same choice `bettor_promo_visibility_config` makes for its promo kinds: identical decisions about the same catalogue, and a second table would mean a second resolver to keep in step. Resolution is **most-specific-wins — market > tournament > category > sport > global** — in ONE place, [`services/api/src/lib/insight-widgets.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/insight-widgets.ts) (pure half unit-tested in [`insight-widgets.test.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/insight-widgets.test.ts)). `market` is a market **TYPE** (`provider_market_id`), never a market row — rows are created and settled per match and would make every rule garbage within a day — and it sits at the TOP because the widget renders on a market, at the cost that such a rule spans every sport quoting that id. The `global` row is seeded for both widgets so there is always an answer and the fallback is a row an operator can see; if it is somehow deleted the resolver **fails closed**, and `DELETE` refuses the global scope for exactly that reason. Both ship seeded **OFF** (where the operator left them on 2026-09-07). Routes apply two gates, both BEFORE the Redis cache read so switching a scope off stops the *work* and not merely the render: `isFullyDisabled` answers without touching the catalogue, then a one-probe match-level check; market rules are applied to the returned rows afterwards and cost nothing when the widget has none (`byMarket.size === 0`). Reading the cascade per request rather than caching it with the payload is what makes an operator's change take effect on the next call instead of after the 5-minute TTL. Admin API [`admin/insight-widgets.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/insight-widgets.ts) (config + rule upsert/delete + scoped pickers; every mutation audit-logged), UI at [`/admin/zillatips`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/zillatips/) and [`/admin/zillafacts`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/zillafacts/) sharing one editor, both showing a red **"This feature is currently disabled"** banner while it is off everywhere. |
| ZillaTips (TS) | [`services/api/src/modules/zillatips/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/zillatips/routes.ts) — `GET /catalog/matches/:matchId/zillatips`. One parameterised CTE: enumerates active markets × outcomes on the requested match, maps each outcome to its team-of-interest (Match Winner / Map Winner = team-specific; everything else = symmetric), pulls each team's last 5 closed matches with the same market signature via per-team `JOIN LATERAL`, translates the equivalent outcome (1↔2 swap for `provider_market_id IN (1, 4)` when role differs), hydrates `prematch_odds` + `result`, aggregates flat-stake ROI, and filters `roi ≥ ZILLATIP_MIN_ROI`. Redis cache: 5-minute TTL per match. Frontend hook + widget: [`apps/web/src/lib/use-zillatips.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-zillatips.ts) + [`apps/web/src/components/match/zillatips-widget.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/zillatips-widget.tsx). Shared types + thresholds (`ZILLATIP_MIN_ROI`, `ZILLATIP_TIER_GLOW` / `_FIRE`, `zillaTipTier`): [`packages/types/src/zillatips.ts`](./packages/types/src/zillatips.ts). |
| Disir widget React components | [`apps/web/src/components/widgets/`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/) — `disir-widget.tsx` (generic iframe wrapper that fetches the URL from `/widgets/*`, listens to the doc-defined `LOADED`/`RESIZE`/`DATA`/`CLOSE`/`SCROLL_TOP` postMessage events, hides itself until DATA:true for live widgets), `rail-prematch-panel.tsx` (renders below the bet slip in the right rail on desktop; reads `MatchPageContext`), `match-prematch-mobile.tsx` (mobile-only inline toggle on the match page — collapsed by default, expanded by button under the match info above the markets), `match-live-media.tsx` (combines Twitch/YouTube embed + live scoreboard widget; desktop stacks both, mobile pill-toggles between them), `supported-sports.ts` (sport-slug allowlist mirroring the doc table — prematch: cs2/dota2/lol/valorant/efootball; live: + ebasketball/ecricket). Mounted via `MatchPageProvider` ([`apps/web/src/lib/match-page-context.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/match-page-context.tsx)) in `(main)/layout.tsx`; the match page uses `<MatchPageRegistrar>` to publish the active match into the context so the persistent rail picks it up. |
| Sportradar Live Match Tracker (LMT Plus) | [`apps/web/src/components/widgets/sportradar-lmt.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/sportradar-lmt.tsx) — iframe of Sportradar's HOSTED standalone widget page, `https://widgets.sir.sportradar.com/<client>/<lang>/standalone/match.lmtPlus#matchId=<srMatchId>&sportId=<srSportId>&layout=single&collapseTo=momentum&expanded=<bool>`. **Not the `SIR('addWidget', …)` loader, on purpose**: the widgetloader licenses per EMBEDDING ORIGIN (`/<clientId>/licensing` keys on the page's origin, and the LMT feed carries an origin-bound signed `T=` token), and until Sportradar issues Oddzilla's Client ID (blocked on their side, Sept 2026) the public `betradar` client is licensed only for localhost + Sportradar hosts — `oddzilla.cc` gets `No packages licensed`. The standalone page runs ON a licensed origin and is served without `frame-ancestors`, so it embeds from oddzilla.cc; Sportradar confirmed this interim path in writing (2026-09-04). Props ride in the URL HASH (the query string is ignored; `wl-` prefix = widgetloader options, `adapter-` prefix = odds adapter); the page re-reads the hash on `hashchange`; no cross-origin resize, so height is fixed. CSP `frame-src` in [`middleware.ts`](./apps/web/src/middleware.ts) carries `https://widgets.sir.sportradar.com`. **Mounted from the mapping, never hard-coded** (was a single pinned test fixture until 2026-09-04): [`match/[id]/page.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/page.tsx) renders it under the video block whenever `/catalog/matches/:id` returns a `sportradar` block, which it does only for a CONFIRMED row in `match_sportradar_ids` — see the Sportradar match mapping row below. No mapping, no tracker, no gap in the layout. `sportId` comes from the mapping row rather than a constant, because Sportradar's sport taxonomy is theirs (12 rugby, 137 esoccer) and a wrong one renders the wrong pitch. When the Client ID arrives: swap `betradar` for it, have oddzilla.cc whitelisted, then optionally move to the direct loader for odds adapters, a client-specific theme palette and `onSizeChange` auto-height (following the storefront's dark mode does not need it — see the theming row below). **The tracker replaces our own scoreboard, it does not sit under it** (2026-09-05): `hasLmt` in [`page.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/page.tsx) drops `<LiveScoreboard>` from the header card whenever a confirmed mapping exists, because the tracker draws both teams with their crests and the running score itself — rendering both stacked two scoreboards on the same fixture. The header card keeps the status pill + tournament line (which the tracker does NOT carry) and loses its bottom margin. **Settings, all read from the widget's own PropTypes** (chunk `match.lmtPlus`, 2026-09-05, not guessed): `collapseTo=momentum` leaves the scoreboard + momentum strip when collapsed; `expanded` is TRUE on desktop and FALSE on mobile (≤ 1099px, resolved in an effect so SSR and hydration agree, then re-applied on viewport change). Because the hosted page reports no size across origins, the Expand / Collapse pill next to the title is OURS — the widget's in-frame chevron cannot tell us it was used, and a collapsed widget inside a 620px frame is a band of white space. **That pill was mobile-only until 2026-09-05**, on the reasoning that desktop opens expanded so the in-frame chevron suffices; it does not — collapsing from inside the frame on desktop left ~450px of white between the momentum strip and the first market, reported from production. It renders on every breakpoint now, and `COLLAPSED_HEIGHT` came down 260 → 180 (loose on purpose: too small CLIPS the momentum strip, which is worse than a gap). **The in-frame chevron is now GONE** (2026-09-05, second pass): the widget declares `hideExpand: bool` in its own PropTypes block (chunk `match.lmtPlus`, alongside `collapseTo` and `expanded`), and the standalone page forwards the entire hash into `addWidget`, so `hideExpand=true` reaches it unchanged. That leaves exactly one control — ours — and the frame height can no longer drift out of sync with what the widget is showing. `COLLAPSED_HEIGHT` came down again to 115 and then 104 once the chevron's ~27px went with it (collapsed body measures ~103px), and the toggle moved BELOW the frame as a full-width button on every breakpoint — a pill in the header is easy to miss, and it stands in for a control the widget itself draws centred under its own body, so that is where a bettor looks. **The EXPANDED height is derived, not fixed** (2026-09-05): the LMT's pitch is drawn to an aspect ratio, so natural height tracks WIDTH — a flat 620 was tuned for the ~900px main column and left ~277px of white under a 370px phone. A `ResizeObserver` feeds `expandedHeightFor(w) = clamp(118 + 0.59w, 300, 760)`, whose constants are measured off a production tennis fixture (chrome read as 131 and corrected to 118 once the render showed ~13px of frame left over below the tab strip; pitch 211 on a 358px frame) and which returns ~617 at desktop widths, so desktop is unchanged in practice. Other sports draw slightly different courts, so it is close rather than exact — erring tall costs a gap, erring short would crop the pitch. Leftover frame used to render as a tinted bar between the widget's content and the toggle, because the iframe's background was `--surface-2`; it is the `--sportradar-frame-bg` token now — white in light, matching the hosted page's own body (which sets no background of its own), and `--surface-2` in dark, where the transparent Sportradar theme paints no base and the frame IS the widget's surface (see the theming row below) — so a few px of overshoot on another sport reads as part of the widget rather than as a band. That forgives being too tall only — too short still crops. The chunk is reachable for this kind of archaeology: `GET /betradar/widgetloader` → its `.u()` chunk map → `https://widgets.sir.sportradar.com/assets/js/chunk.match.lmtPlus.<hash>.js` — note the assets live at the ROOT, not under `/betradar/`, and every request needs a browser User-Agent or the CDN answers 403. The page's only callback, `onDataChange`, just stamps a class on its own body and never posts to the parent, so auto-height still needs the direct widgetloader's `onSizeChange`, blocked on the Client ID. **Virtualised LMT** (`enableVirtualised` + `enableVirtualisedLight` + `vlmtLightPitchView=top`) is passed for SR sports 1 and 2 only — soccer and basketball are the two the product covers, per the demo bundle's `WIDGETS_LIST` for `demo.vlmt` / `demo.vlmt-light`; the light build is the bird's-eye one and the only variant that takes a pitch view. A fixture without skeletal tracking falls back to the ordinary pitch on its own. |
| Sportradar widgets follow the storefront theme | [`sportradar-theme.ts`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/sportradar-theme.ts), consumed by all four embeds (tracker, Head to Head, Live Table, Bet Assist). The hosted standalone page forwards `wl-` hash keys into the widgetloader, and the loader accepts `theme` there — from a FIXED list it validates against (read from `/betradar/widgetloader`, 2026-09-06): `<client>`, `<client>dark`, `<client>transparent`, `<client>darktransparent` for the `betradar` and `sportradar` clients, plus `default`, `neutral`, `mx`; each is a stylesheet at `widgets.sir.sportradar.com/<name>/css`, and an unknown name is dropped silently. **Dark uses `betradardarktransparent`, not `betradardark`**: rendered side by side on a `#1a1a1c` backdrop before choosing, `betradardark` paints every base tile Sportradar blue (`#0072b1`) — a blue box on a near-black page — while the transparent variant paints no base at all and lights the text, so the widget takes OUR iframe background as its surface. That background is the token `--sportradar-frame-bg` (white in light, `--surface-2` in dark) on all four frames. It is a CSS token rather than a per-theme value picked in JS because SSR renders the light theme and `useDocumentTheme` only resolves in an effect, while the pre-hydration boot script sets `data-theme` before first paint — so a dark user's frame is dark from the first frame instead of flashing white until hydration. **The loader reads `theme` once, at its own boot** — a later `hashchange` re-parses the hash but the stylesheet is already resolved — so every embed keys its iframe on the theme and a toggle remounts the frame (the tracker's expand/collapse still rides the hash without a reload). A dark user's frame therefore mounts once with the light URL during hydration and is replaced when the theme resolves; the discarded document has fetched at most the 4 KB page and the start of the loader, and nothing has painted. Light sends no `wl-theme` and is byte-identical to before. **Verifying any of this needs a real browser build**: the Browser pane blocks `widgets.sir.sportradar.com` by policy, Sportradar's CDN answers 403 to Playwright's bundled Chromium (and to a headless User-Agent), and framed from a synthetic parent origin the widgets never mounted — headless **Edge** through Playwright (`chromium.launch(channel="msedge")`, ordinary Chrome UA) loading each standalone URL top-level, with the backdrop injected via `add_style_tag` and `body.sr-data-loaded` as the ready signal, is what produced the comparison. |
| Sportradar Bet Assist (per-market statistics) | Mapping [`packages/types/src/bet-assist.ts`](./packages/types/src/bet-assist.ts) + control [`apps/web/src/components/match/bet-assist.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/bet-assist.tsx), rendered from the market header in [`live-markets.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-markets.tsx). Bet Assist is the case FOR one market on one fixture — form, head-to-head averages, a win-probability split — so it is keyed by `(SR match id, SR market key)` and rides the same confirmed mapping the tracker does. **The overlay is ours, not Sportradar's `betAssist.button`**: that widget draws its own popover, and a popover drawn inside an iframe is clipped to the iframe's box, so the control is a native button (label + bar mark, right-aligned in the market header, matching their demo) and the panel is a portal onto `document.body` framing `betAssist.standalone`, with the frame height fixed PER MARKET KEY (`FRAME_HEIGHT_BY_MARKET` in [`bet-assist.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/bet-assist.tsx)): the hosted page cannot report its size across origins and draws a different set of blocks per market shape. Measured 2026-09-05 with a ruler overlaid on the stretched frame on production at the panel's 460px width — a 1X2 (`3Way`: win probability + form split + last five) ~225px, a total (`totalOverUnder`: combined average + two goals bars) ~340px, a double chance or half-time result (one two-bar block) ~95px — so the table carries 240 / 350 / 110, half-market analogues take their full-match sibling's shape, and every unmeasured key takes the tallest measured (350), because too short clips while too tall only costs blank space. The one-size 560 before this left 300px of blank white under a 1X2 and 460px under a double chance. **The market key is the whole problem.** Sportradar's vocabulary is 189 keys, it is per SPORT (the same Fonbet id 1000305 "Total" is `totalOverUnder` in soccer, `totalPoints` in basketball, `totalRuns` in baseball, `totalGames` in tennis), and a key the widget does not know for that sport renders an EMPTY panel rather than an error — so a typo ships as a button onto nothing. Hence two tables, both read verbatim from Sportradar's demo bundle (`getMarketTypesPerSport`, 2026-09-05): the per-sport vocabulary, and our own `<provider_market_id>[@<variant>]` → key map, with `resolveBetAssistMarket` validating its answer against the first before returning it and [`bet-assist.test.ts`](./packages/types/src/bet-assist.test.ts) pinning that every mapped key exists. Bet Assist covers eight sports (SR 1 soccer, 2 basketball, 3 baseball, 4 ice hockey, 5 tennis, 6 handball, 16 american football, 29 futsal) — table tennis, darts, rugby, badminton, padel and every esport have none, which is why the map is keyed by SR sport id. Our side of the map is Fonbet's stable ids plus its sub-event `variant` (`fb:100201/202` halves, `fb:100301` 1st half for basketball / american football, `fb:100401..404` quarters, `fb:100101..103` periods, `fb:100501..505` sets, `fb:100601` 1st inning, `fb:101701` first 5 innings, `fb:400100` corners — all read off the live catalogue 2026-09-05). Deliberately partial in both directions: Bet Assist has no soccer handicap, so football's Handicap market carries no button, and Fonbet's card / player-special markets have no counterpart. An unmapped market renders without the control. |
| Sportradar Head to Head (right rail) | [`sportradar-h2h.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/sportradar-h2h.tsx) + the context-gated mount [`rail-head-to-head.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/rail-head-to-head.tsx), rendered by [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) directly under the bet slip and above the tabbed `RailMatchPanel`. Same hosted-standalone embed as the tracker (`headToHead.standalone`), with `layout=inline` — the widget's other layout, `overlay`, expects a host button to pop it open, which is the shape Bet Assist uses and not this one. Gated on the confirmed mapping, which reaches the rail through a new `sportradar` field on [`MatchPageContext`](./apps/web/src/lib/match-page-context.tsx) (the registrar depends on the two ids, not the object, so a live tick can't re-fire its set/clear effect). Height is fixed because the hosted page cannot report its size across origins, and since 2026-09-05 it is sized to the tab the widget OPENS on (280px; that tab measured ~245px on two live fixtures with a ruler overlaid on the stretched frame at the rail's 348px width on production, and the stats-comparison tab ~200px), not to its tallest — the original 560 left ~315px of blank white under the default view on every visit. The taller tabs (lineups, season table, previous meetings) scroll inside the frame, as they did at 560. |
| Sportradar Live Table (right rail, tournament views) | [`sportradar-live-table.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/sportradar-live-table.tsx) + [`rail-live-table.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/widgets/rail-live-table.tsx), mounted by [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) under the slip. **The id it takes is a MATCH id, on purpose.** `season.liveTable` resolves its season from any one of `matchId | tournamentId | uniqueTournamentId | seasonId` (read from the widget's own async-prop definition, chunk `season.liveTable`, 2026-09-05), and of those four we hold exactly one — migration 0100 maps matches, and no feed carries Sportradar's tournament ids at all — so one confirmed fixture under the tournament resolves the table and there is no second id space to map, review and keep true. `GET /catalog/tournaments/:id/sportradar` ([catalog/routes.ts](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts), 60 s cached, 300/min) picks that fixture, ordered **live/upcoming first, soonest kickoff** — a season table is per season, so pointing at a finished fixture renders last season's standings. **There is no /tournament route in this storefront**: a tournament view is `/sport/:slug?tournament=N`, so the rail reads the route (`usePathname` + `useSearchParams`) rather than taking a context push, and renders nothing on a sport page with no tournament filter. Gated a second time by `liveTableCoversSport` — tennis, table tennis, darts, badminton, padel and squash are draw or ranking formats with no standings, and since the hosted frame cannot tell us it rendered nothing, an ungated mount would ship a dead 620px box on our second- and third-largest mapped sports. 199 tournaments carried a confirmed mapping when this shipped. |
| Sportradar match mapping (Oddzilla ↔ Fonbet ↔ Sportradar ids) | Migration `0100_sportradar_match_map` + [`packages/db/src/schema/sportradar.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/sportradar.ts). **Oddzilla already owns a match id for every fixture from every feed** — `matches.id` (bigserial), traditional sports included; the feed's own id is `matches.provider_urn` (`od:match:<n>` / `fb:match:<n>`, both 9-character prefixes). So two of the three id spaces need no storage at all, and the view `match_external_ids` derives them rather than copying them, because a copy could only ever drift. **Sportradar is carried by neither feed**: measured 2026-09-04 across a full 13 667-event Fonbet snapshot, no event carries an external id of any kind, and a Fonbet match page loads statistics only from their own hosts — their stats are first-party, not Betradar. **Sportradar runs two feed hosts and they behave differently**, which is the crux of this whole feature: `lmt.fn.sportradar.com` (the tracker's own data feed) is licensed per embedding ORIGIN and answers `403 Unauthorized feed` to anything else, including plain server-to-server with no Origin header at all — that is why the widget is embedded through their hosted standalone page. `stats.fn.sportradar.com` (the statistics feed) answers ordinary server-to-server requests with no token and no Origin, verified across all 17 LMT-covered sports we carry. `GET <base>/gismo/sport_matches/<srSportId>/<YYYY-MM-DD>` returns a sport's whole day with `_id` (the SR match id), `_sid`, `_dt.uts` (kickoff UTC), both team names and `coverage.lmtsupport` — exactly the matcher's input, so the mapping builds itself. Client: [`services/api/src/lib/sportradar/fixture-source.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/sportradar/fixture-source.ts). **The mapping maintains itself**: [`sweeper.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/sportradar/sweeper.ts) runs every 30 min under a Redis NX lock (`SPORTRADAR_SYNC_INTERVAL_MINUTES`, floor 5; `SPORTRADAR_SYNC_DISABLED=1` skips) — without it the tracker would silently stop appearing on fixtures published after the last manual run. One pipeline serves all three entry points — the sweeper, `POST /admin/sportradar/sync` and the paste import — in [`sync.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/sportradar/sync.ts), so they cannot drift in what they accept; it fetches only the days our own open matches fall on, so a sport with nothing to map costs zero requests. The sweeper writes NO `admin_audit_log` row (that table records what an ADMIN did, and a NULL actor would both misrepresent it and feed the hash chain a null) and reports to the structured log instead, like every other sweeper here. No credential is involved and nothing is bypassed, but it is worth getting the same written confirmation from Sportradar that covers the standalone-page embed. Because there is no shared key on either side, the link is still INFERRED from kickoff and names, which is what makes it the only one of the three ids that can be wrong. So `match_sportradar_ids` STORES the SR id with the provenance needed to judge it: `status` (candidate / confirmed / rejected), `source` (admin / auto), `confidence`, and `evidence` jsonb holding the SR-side names, kickoff delta, per-team scores and runners-up. **Only `confirmed` reaches the storefront** — a wrong mapping puts another fixture's live statistics on the page. A `rejected` row is a tombstone excluded from the partial unique index on `sr_match_id`, so turning a pair down never blocks the right match from claiming that id. Matcher: [`services/api/src/lib/sportradar/matcher.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/sportradar/matcher.ts) (pure, unit-tested) pairs on kickoff + team names. Kickoff is a GATE not a score (±20 min); names compare by token with prefix-equality so "Man Utd" scores 1.0 against "Manchester United"; and **squad qualifiers are hard vetoes** — "Barcelona" vs "Barcelona W" and "Lokomotiv Moscow" vs "Lokomotiv Moscow (youth)" score ~1.0 on every text metric and are the false positives that actually matter, so a gender or age marker present on one side and absent on the other zeroes the pair. **Except in individual sports, where a single letter is an initial** (2026-09-05): the one-letter markers `b` / `c` (reserves) and `w` (women) are exactly how Fonbet AND Sportradar write a player's initials ("Tseng C H", "Harrison C / Skupski N"), so in tennis, table tennis, darts, badminton, squash, padel and snooker (`SPORTRADAR_INDIVIDUAL_SPORT_IDS` in [`packages/types/src/sportradar.ts`](./packages/types/src/sportradar.ts), keyed by SR sport id and applied by `scorePair`) they stay tokens. Before that, every player whose initial was B, C or W was vetoed against their own fixture — "Samrej K vs Tseng C H" sat unmapped 10 minutes from "Samrej, Kasidit vs Tseng, Chun Hsin" while 36 open tennis fixtures were in the same state; club sports are untouched, where "Atletico Madrid C" and "Lens (w)" still mean what they say. **And the two feeds put the qualifier in different places** (same day, the larger of the two): Fonbet marks the TEAM ("Chelsea (w)", "Poland U20"), Sportradar's day feed marks the COMPETITION ("Super League, Women", "U20 FIFA World Cup") and names the team bare, so compared team-to-team every women's and youth fixture was marker-vs-none — a veto — and women's football, handball, volleyball, basketball and rugby paired at exactly zero (~350 open fixtures). The parser now carries the enclosing `tournament` node's name on `SportradarFixture.tournament` (it had been discarded), `qualifiersFromCompetition` in the matcher reads women's / age markers off it (never a bare "B" — "Serie B" and "Group B" are divisions, and both feeds name a reserve side on the team), and `scorePair` restates them on the Sportradar team names before the veto compares; individual sports are exempt (a player in a women's draw is unmarked on both feeds). Re-measured against the same 2 238 still-unpaired open matches with the real day feeds: 3 proposals before both fixes, 63 after (36 auto-confirm, 27 to the adjudicator — abbreviations like "Fuchse Berlin" / "Berlin", which is its job; `hc` joined the club designators in the same pass, since "HC Dynamo" against "Dynamo Moscow" scored exactly the 0.5 the floor rejects). **That lever was pulled on 2026-09-06**, after Groningen vs Twente (Eredivisie) was live with no tracker: Sportradar's short `name` for FC Twente is its CITY, "Enschede", and Fonbet says "Twente" — score 0, so `scorePair` refused the pair before it could reach ZAGI, which only ever judges what the matcher queues. Three changes, all widening what reaches REVIEW and none widening what confirms on its own. (1) The fixture source carries Sportradar's longer `mediumname` as `homeTeamAlt` / `awayTeamAlt` and the matcher scores our name against every form, best wins ("Twente" vs "FC Twente Enschede" = 0.67 — a queue pair); a squad veto on EITHER form vetoes the side. (2) **Weak pairs**: one side ≥ `WEAK_ANCHOR_SCORE` (0.8), kickoff within 10 min, no home/away swap, no veto → proposed as a `candidate` with `evidence.weak = true`, never `autoConfirm`, sorted BEHIND every strong pair in the greedy claim so it can never take a fixture from one. In a club sport a team plays one match at a time, so the anchor plus the clock pins the fixture; the model decides the other name. Measured on the day's soccer feed against 792 unmapped open matches: the strict rules found 1 new pair, the new ones 44 (13 auto — all correct, "Kashima" / "Kashima Antlers", "Juventus (Juventus Turin)" — 4 queue, 27 weak); the only junk was Fonbet's season head-to-heads ("Barcelona vs Real Madrid — Head-to-head in the tournament", which borrows a real kickoff) pairing swapped against real fixtures, which is why weak pairs refuse a swap and why `isPseudoFixture` (outright-shaped or simulation-named tournaments, via the ZAGI risk-tier predicates) plus `categories.hidden_from_lists` now exclude such rows from the matcher entirely — they are not fixtures and can have no tracker. (3) **A ZAGI rejection is a verdict on a PAIR, not a lockout of the match**: `matchAndPersist` keeps `(source='llm', status='rejected')` rows eligible, passes the rejected id as `rejectedSrMatchId` so that one fixture is never re-proposed, and lets any other fixture claim the row (`setWhere` admits the llm-rejected case only with a different `sr_match_id`). A human rejection stays final. The adjudicator prompt shows both name forms — "Enschede (FC Twente Enschede)" — and the desk shows them plus a "one side matched by name only" note on weak rows. The adjudicator prompt also shows Sportradar's competition now, so "Chelsea vs Aston Villa" under "Super League, Women" no longer reads to the model as the men's fixture. What remains unpaired is coverage: Sportradar's day feed carried 5 american-football fixtures against our 79 and nothing at all for cricket or beach volleyball. `MIN_TEAM_SCORE` is 0.6 rather than 0.5 for the same reason: 0.5 is exactly what two two-word names sharing one word score ("Manchester United" / "Newcastle United"). Auto-confirm additionally needs the winner clear of the runner-up by `AMBIGUITY_MARGIN`. Measured coverage against production on 2026-09-04 (3 418 open matches in LMT-covered sports): **926 paired, 473 auto-confirmed** — football 751/1 926, tennis 58/218, rugby 27/75, handball 26/110, ice hockey 22/96, baseball 21/69. The rest is genuine Sportradar coverage: Fonbet's line is far broader (regional, lower-tier, women's and youth fixtures), and for those SR's best candidate is an unrelated match, not a near-miss. Table tennis pairs 0 of 541 for the same reason — our events are Ukrainian/amateur, SR's day feed is Czech leagues. Manual input still exists for anything the feed misses: [`fixtures-input.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/sportradar/fixtures-input.ts) parses Sportradar schedule JSON (`{sport_events:[...]}`, so a licensed API response works unmodified the day a key exists), a JSON array, or delimited lines. **LLM adjudication of the queue** — [`adjudicator.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/sportradar/adjudicator.ts), run at the end of every sweep and on demand via `POST /admin/sportradar/adjudicate`. It exists because the queue is almost entirely abbreviation, not ambiguity: measured on the first production run, 471 candidates had ZERO runner-ups and a 0-minute kickoff delta, and were queued only on the name score (`Hellas Verona` / `Verona`, `Philadelphia Phillies` / `Philadelphia`). **Three things bound what it can do.** It cannot pick a fixture — the pair is fixed by the matcher, and the model only says whether that ONE pair is the same match, so the worst case is confirming or rejecting something already proposed. It never sees a human decision (only `source='auto' AND status='candidate'` is eligible, re-asserted in the UPDATE's WHERE in case a human ruled while the model was thinking). And anything but a clean `same`/`different` — `unsure`, malformed JSON, an out-of-range index, a non-literal verdict, an HTTP failure — leaves the row in the human queue; `parseVerdicts` is strict and unit-tested for exactly that. Team names are feed data, so the prompt says so and the reply is parsed structurally rather than trusted as text. Validated before shipping: 26/26 correct on a spread of real candidates, and 14/14 on a negative control that included Manchester United vs Manchester City, Sporting CP vs Sporting Gijón, Real Madrid vs Real Sociedad, women's-vs-men's and U21-vs-senior traps — it refuses, it does not rubber-stamp. Graceful-idle: no `SPORTRADAR_LLM_API_KEY`, no adjudication, queue stays human-reviewed. Admin desk at [`/admin/sportradar`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/sportradar/) (Catalog group) — Sync from Sportradar, AI-review queue, (all sports or one, preview or commit), review queue, unmapped list, paste-and-preview import (`dryRun` writes nothing), manual id entry, confirm / reject / clear; routes in [`services/api/src/modules/admin/sportradar.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/sportradar.ts), every mutation audit-logged. Sport taxonomy bridge + LMT coverage list: [`packages/types/src/sportradar.ts`](./packages/types/src/sportradar.ts). |
| Oddin video (Havik player) — first-party match streams | API — [`services/api/src/modules/video/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/video/routes.ts) (`GET /video/match/:matchId` → `OddinVideoAvailability`; accepts a numeric `matches.id` or an `od:match:N` URN, 60/min). **Watching is signed-in-only**: the route stays reachable anonymously (the storefront asks on every match page, logged out included) but for `!req.user` it returns `signInRequired: true` and withholds `apiKey` / `matchUrn` / `baseUrl` — withholding the publishable key IS the gate, since without it the SDK can neither resolve playback nor sign a DRM licence request. Deliberately not a 401, which would be indistinguishable from "no stream" at the call site and would cost the sign-in prompt. The response is `cache-control: private, no-store` because it carries a credential and varies per viewer + [`catalog.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/video/catalog.ts) (Redis-cached 60 s snapshot of Oddin's `GET /v1/catalog`, flattened to `urn → {status, startsAt}`, in-process single-flight + 15 s negative cache). **Availability is resolved from the catalog, never by probing `/v1/playback/{urn}`** — that endpoint answers `503 UNAVAILABLE` for a URN Oddin carries no stream for, which is indistinguishable from a real transient outage, so a probe-based check would mount a broken player on every uncovered match. One catalog call covers all ~800 matches. Storefront — hook [`apps/web/src/lib/use-oddin-video.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-oddin-video.ts) (fetch on mount + 2 min poll, because Oddin adds matches to the catalog over time; stops at `ended`) and player [`apps/web/src/components/match/oddin-video-player.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/oddin-video-player.tsx), surfaced as the FIRST source in the existing stream tab strip ([`match-streams.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-streams.tsx)) and preferred over the fixture's Twitch / YouTube URLs since it is the rights-cleared first-party feed. SDK is `@oddin-gg/havik-player` (Mode A, the managed player — it owns the `<video>`, bundles hls.js, and does LL-HLS + Widevine/FairPlay itself; the DRM licence URLs are pre-signed per session and must be POSTed verbatim from the page, so a player of our own is not an option). **Three load-bearing details:** (1) the `<video>` is created imperatively, NOT rendered by React — with `controls:'custom'` the SDK reparents the element it is given into a `.havik-player` div it inserts as a sibling, and a React-owned video then unmounts with `NotFoundError: Failed to execute removeChild`; (2) the SDK is dynamically imported so hls.js only downloads for matches that actually have a stream; (3) CSP needs `connect-src https://*.oddin-video.gg` **plus** `media-src blob:` (MSE source) and `worker-src blob:` (hls.js demuxer worker) — the latter two were absent and inherited `default-src 'self'` / `script-src 'strict-dynamic'`, either of which kills playback. Hosts all sit under one apex: `feed[-dev]` (playback + catalog), `playback[-dev]` (manifest + segments, fetched by XHR so they fall under connect-src), `drm[-dev]` (licence + FairPlay cert), `events.feed[-dev]` (SSE live-state), `beacons[-dev]` (QoE). `waitForLive` with `kickoffAt` arms an upcoming match and attaches at go-live without a reload. Shared types [`packages/types/src/video.ts`](./packages/types/src/video.ts). No migration — nothing is persisted. |
| HD wallet derivation (Go, isolated container) | [`services/signer/`](./services/signer/) — holds `HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC`, exposes `POST /derive` and `POST /sign` over a Unix socket on a tmpfs volume shared only with the API container. Parity-tested against the canonical BIP44 abandon-mnemonic test vector for ETH (m/44'/60'/0'/0/0 → 0x9858EfFD232B4033E47d90003D41EC34EcaEda94) and the matching Tron derivation. |
| Live support chat (storefront ↔ backoffice) | Migration `0075_livesupport_chat` adds `support_threads` + `support_messages` (Drizzle schema at [`packages/db/src/schema/support.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/support.ts)). **One open thread per bettor** — the `support_threads_one_open_per_user` partial unique index gates the slot; closing a thread frees it so a future issue starts a fresh conversation, and historical threads stay around for audit. Denormalised `unread_user` / `unread_admin` counters on each thread drive the floating widget badge + the admin sidebar badge as O(1) reads — maintained inside the same tx as every message insert / mark-read. **User-facing API** ([`services/api/src/modules/support/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/support/routes.ts)): `GET /support/me/thread` returns the open thread + last 100 messages (ascending chronological); `POST /support/me/messages` locks the open row via `SELECT FOR UPDATE` then either appends or spawns a new row (with the partial unique index gating the concurrent-POST race), bumps `unread_admin`, publishes a `SupportMessageFrame` on `user:{userId}` Redis channel; `POST /support/me/mark-read` clears `unread_user`. **Admin API** ([`services/api/src/modules/admin/support.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/support.ts)): `/admin/support/threads` list with filter tabs (open / unread / closed / all) + email/nickname/subject search + cursor pagination; `/admin/support/threads/:id` detail with operator-name join on admin-sent messages; `/admin/support/threads/:id/reply` bumps `unread_user` + clears `unread_admin` + publishes frame + writes admin_audit_log; `close` / `reopen` each insert a `sender_kind='system'` row marking the transition; `/admin/support/unread-count` powers the sidebar badge (60 s poll, same pattern as `/admin/emails/unread-count`). **Realtime fan-out** reuses the existing `user:{userId}` Redis pub/sub channel already wired through ws-gateway for ticket frames — no gateway changes needed. The front-end's shared WS dispatcher in [`apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts) gains a `support_message` branch + `supportListeners` set; the widget consumes it via [`apps/web/src/lib/use-support-stream.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-support-stream.ts) (thin hook mirroring `use-ticket-stream.ts`). **Storefront** mounts a floating bottom-right button + panel ([`apps/web/src/components/support/support-widget.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/support/support-widget.tsx)) from the (main) layout; gates on signed-in state via `useSessionUserId()` so anonymous renders see nothing. **Admin UI** at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/support/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/support/) (inbox `page.tsx` + `support-client.tsx`; thread `[id]/page.tsx` + `thread-client.tsx` with reply form, close/reopen toggle, auto-mark-read on mount). Sidebar entry "Support chat" under Operations with unread-thread-count badge. Shared types live in [`packages/types/src/support.ts`](./packages/types/src/support.ts) so api + web agree on the wire shape. |
| Inbound email + backoffice inbox | Migration `0074_email_inbound` + Drizzle additions in [`packages/db/src/schema/email.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/email.ts). **Self-hosted SMTP receiver** — [`services/mail-receiver/`](./services/mail-receiver/) (Go binary on `emersion/go-smtp`, host port 25 → container :2525, accepts inbound for the storefront domain only, translates each RFC 5322 message into a SendGrid-Inbound-Parse-shaped multipart POST and forwards to the api's webhook on the docker network). Replaces SendGrid Inbound Parse after Twilio declined our signup citing regional service requirements (2026-05-21). Webhook — [`services/api/src/modules/email/inbound/`](./services/api/src/modules/email/inbound/) (`routes.ts` POST /webhooks/sendgrid-inbound/:secret with multipart parsing, `headers.ts` RFC 5322 header + address parsing, `threading.ts` In-Reply-To / References / subject-fallback thread resolution). The route slug retains `sendgrid-inbound` for code-stability — the payload shape is the contract, not the source. CSRF skip for `/webhooks/*` in [`csrf.ts`](./services/api/src/plugins/csrf.ts) — webhooks carry their own auth (path secret, constant-time compared, 404 on miss). Admin API — [`services/api/src/modules/admin/emails.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/emails.ts) (list / thread / reply / compose / archive / mark-read / unread-count; every mutation audit-logged). Admin UI — [`apps/web/src/app/admin/emails/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/emails/) (`page.tsx` + `emails-client.tsx` inbox with filter tabs + search + cursor pagination, `[id]/page.tsx` + `thread-client.tsx` thread view with reply form + archive toggle + auto-mark-read, `new/page.tsx` + `compose-form.tsx` standalone compose). Sidebar — "Emails" entry under Operations polls `/admin/emails/unread-count` every 60 s alongside the deposits-alerts poll. Attachments: metadata captured for visibility, **file contents are not persisted** — mail-receiver streams attachment bytes into multipart parts but the webhook drops them after recording filename / type / size. Activation = generate `SENDGRID_INBOUND_SECRET=$(openssl rand -hex 24)`, add to `.env`, set DNS (`mail.oddzilla.cc` A → box IP, `oddzilla.cc` MX → `mail.oddzilla.cc`), `make deploy`. No third-party signup required. |
| Transactional email (verify + reset) | Outbox table + token tables — [`packages/db/migrations/0073_email.sql`](./packages/db/migrations/0073_email.sql) + Drizzle schema [`packages/db/src/schema/email.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/email.ts). Module — [`services/api/src/modules/email/`](./services/api/src/modules/email/) (`client.ts` provider-agnostic interface + factory, `resend.ts` Resend HTTP client, `render.ts` HTML+text templates for verify_email / password_reset, `tokens.ts` 256-bit random + sha256 helpers, `enqueue.ts` tx-aware helpers `enqueueVerifyEmail` / `enqueuePasswordReset`, `worker.ts` LISTEN/NOTIFY drainer mirroring push worker shape). Wiring — signup enqueues inside the user-insert tx in [`services/api/src/modules/auth/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/auth/service.ts); routes at [`services/api/src/modules/auth/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/auth/routes.ts) (`POST /auth/verify-email`, `POST /auth/resend-verification`, `POST /auth/forgot-password`, `POST /auth/reset-password`); worker started from [`services/api/src/server.ts`](./services/api/src/server.ts) (`startEmailOutboxWorker`). Storefront — banner [`apps/web/src/components/shell/email-verification-banner.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/email-verification-banner.tsx) mounted in (main) layout when `emailVerifiedAt` is null; pages at [`apps/web/src/app/(auth)/verify-email/`](./apps/web/src/app/(auth)/verify-email/), [`forgot-password/`](./apps/web/src/app/(auth)/forgot-password/), [`reset-password/`](./apps/web/src/app/(auth)/reset-password/). Provider-agnostic by design: `EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend\|sendgrid\|ses\|smtp` selects the client; only `resend` ships today, the others are stubs ready for a one-file addition. Graceful-idle when `EMAIL_PROVIDER_TOKEN` is unset — worker stamps `last_error='email_disabled'` and drains so the table stays bounded. Activation = generate a Resend API key (3K emails/month free), set `EMAIL_PROVIDER_TOKEN` + verify the send-domain in Resend's dashboard, restart api. |

Producer — [`services/settlement/internal/store/push.go`](./services/settlement/internal/store/push.go) (`EnqueueBetWonPush`, `NotifyPushOutbox`) called from `maybeSettleTicket` + `applyMarketSettle` in [`services/settlement/internal/settler/settler.go`](./services/settlement/internal/settler/settler.go). Device intake — [`services/api/src/modules/devices/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/devices/routes.ts) (`POST /devices/register` etc.; migration 0045). Android client — [`apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/fcm/`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/fcm/) (`FcmService.kt` + `PushBootstrap.kt` + README), manifest service entry in [`AndroidManifest.xml`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml), conditional `com.google.gms.google-services` plugin in [`apps/mobile-android/app/build.gradle.kts`](./apps/mobile-android/app/build.gradle.kts) (gated on `app/google-services.json` existing so fresh checkouts still build), login-transition register/unregister hook in [`WebViewHost.onPageFinished`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/web/WebViewHost.kt), Android-13+ `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` prompt in [`MainActivity.kt`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/MainActivity.kt). Activation = drop `google-services.json` from the Firebase Console at `apps/mobile-android/app/`; everything else is already wired. |
| Performance metrics collector (Go, isolated container) | [`services/metrics-collector/`](./services/metrics-collector/) — separate sidecar that holds the three sensitive bind mounts (`/proc:/host/proc:ro`, `/:/host-root:ro,rprivate`, `/var/run/docker.sock:ro`) so the api container doesn't have to. Exposes `GET /healthz` + `GET /snapshot` on the docker default network at port 9090. Snapshot returns host CPU%/load/memory/swap/disk + parsed container list (state + health from the Docker Engine API `/containers/json` Status string). Distroless static, runs as nobody (UID 65534), `cap_drop: ALL`, `read_only: true`, `mem_limit: 64m`. The api process polls it via `METRICS_COLLECTOR_URL`, never the browser. |
| Performance monitoring (admin) | [`services/api/src/modules/admin/monitoring.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/monitoring.ts) — proxies `metrics-collector` for the live `GET /admin/monitoring/snapshot` and serves time-series via `GET /admin/monitoring/history?hours=N` (1..24) from a Redis sorted set (`monitoring:samples`). Background sampler runs every 60 s under a Redis NX EX lock (`monitoring:sampler:lock`, 90 s TTL) so a future multi-instance api still produces one sample per minute; trims older than 24 h via `ZREMRANGEBYSCORE`. Frontend at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/monitoring/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/monitoring/) — KPI grid + 24 h SVG charts (disk / memory / load) + container health table, with severity banding tuned to the box's incident history (disk >70% amber / >85% red, memory >75/>90, swap >25/>50, load >cpu_count / >2×cpu_count). |

| Signer client (TS, undici over Unix socket) | [`services/api/src/lib/signer-client.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/signer-client.ts) |
| Oddin XML structs (Go) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/oddinxml/` (msg + fixture decoders; also duplicated in `services/settlement/internal/oddinxml/`) |
| Oddin REST client (Go) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/oddinrest/` — `WhoAmI`, `Fixtures`, `SportEventFixture`, `Sports`, `SnapshotRecovery`, `InitiateRecovery` |
| Auto-mapping resolver (Go) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/automap/` — REST-driven sport/category/tournament/match auto-creation; `RefreshFromFixture` for fixture_change re-fetch |
| Recovery + alive-gap + alive-watchdog + handover sweeper | `services/feed-ingester/internal/handler/handler.go` (`TriggerRecovery`, `AliveState`, `mapFixtureStatus`, `handleMatchStatusChange`); `services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go` (`runHandoverSweeper`, `runRecoveryListener`, `runFixtureRefreshListener`, **`runAliveWatchdog`** + `feedSilence`). Two NOTIFY channels: `feed_recovery` (full AMQP replay) and `fixture_refresh` (single-URN REST refetch). No periodic phantom-drain — `<sport_event_status>` inside every `odds_change` is the lifecycle source of truth, and a stuck match is a real bug to surface, not noise to mop up. **`runAliveWatchdog` is the feed-down safety net**: when no AMQP message (alive heartbeat or odds_change) has arrived for `FEED_STALE_SUSPEND_SECONDS` (default 20s) it suspends the whole active catalog via `FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog`, covering token-revocation / partition cases where `OnConnect`'s suspend-before-recover never runs because reconnects fail. `handleAlive`'s gap-detection only triggers recovery on alives that *arrive*; the watchdog is the absence-detector for alives that *stop*. |
| Live scoreboard (per-map score + current-map indicator) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/oddinxml/messages.go` (`SportEventStatus`, `PeriodScores`, `PeriodScore`, `Scoreboard` decode the `<sport_event_status>` block carried inside every `odds_change`); `services/feed-ingester/internal/handler/livescore.go` (`buildLiveScore`, `deriveCurrentMap` write the persisted JSON shape); `services/feed-ingester/internal/store/catalog.go` (`UpdateMatchLiveScore` writes `matches.live_score` jsonb); rendered by `apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/page.tsx` (`MapScoreboard` with per-map rounds/kills, current-map highlight + secondary scoreboard line for live map). Persisted JSON keeps top-level `home`/`away` for back-compat with the list-card `match-row.tsx`. See [`docs/ODDIN.md`](./docs/ODDIN.md#live-scoreboard-sport_event_status). |
| Match lifecycle apply (forward-only) | `services/feed-ingester/internal/store/catalog.go` (`UpdateMatchStatus` — SQL guard rejects regressions from `closed`/`cancelled` and to `not_started`; returns `(changed bool, err)` so the caller can broadcast only on genuine transitions, not idempotent re-applies; `UpdateAllMarketsStatusForMatch` exists for the rarely-emitted `bet_stop groups="all"`); `services/feed-ingester/internal/handler/handler.go` (`handleOddsChange` forwards every `<sport_event_status status="…"/>` code through `UpdateMatchStatus` so `not_started → live → closed/cancelled` transitions land in real time, and publishes a `matchStatus` WS frame on the `odds:match:{id}` channel whenever the row actually moves — same channel as odds + market-status ticks, ws-gateway forwards the JSON verbatim; `handleBetStop` and `handleMatchStatusChange` are no-ops for bookmaker 142 since the broker doesn't emit those types — kept as graceful handlers for bookmakers that do); `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` (`MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal` — flips match to `closed` when no market remains at `1/0/-1/-2`, returns `(matchID, closed, err)`, called from both `handleBetSettlement` and `handleBetCancel` which publish the same `matchStatus` frame on close so the storefront drops the LIVE pill the moment Oddin stops re-asserting an active state — Oddin sometimes drops the final `<sport_event_status status="4">` once the last market settles, so this path is the safety net the WS pipeline rides). Storefront fan-out: [`apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts) (`useLiveMatchStatus` / `useLiveMatchStatusForMatches`) merged into the rendered `match.status` by `match-list-tabs.tsx`'s `mergeMatchWithLive` (sport / lobby / live / upcoming lists), the new [`MatchHeaderStatusPill`](./apps/web/src/components/match/header-status-pill.tsx) on both match-detail pages, `LiveScoreboard` (drops current-map highlight on close), `MatchLiveMedia` (hides the live-stats widget on close), and `MatchPageRegistrar` (republishes the live status into `MatchPageContext` so the rail's `RailMatchPanel` follows the transition without a hard reload). No background reconciliation: a match that drifts out of sync with the AMQP feed is a real bug to debug, not noise to paper over. |
| Bifrost backup feed (odds + settlement standby) | [`services/bifrost-feed/`](./services/bifrost-feed/) — consumes Oddin's Bifrost GraphQL API (the white-label esports front end behind `maxbet.rs/en/esport`; Oddin authorised MaxBet's brand key, client 101, on 2026-09-03; key in `BIFROST_API_KEY`, never in the repo). One `graphql-transport-ws` socket, one `onUpdateMatchLive` subscription per match on the active offer plus the global `onMatchStateChanged` stream; [`internal/translate`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/translate/) renders every snapshot as byte-compatible Oddin `odds_change` (market OPEN/SUSPENDED → 1/-1; outcome OPEN → active; `probabilities` = margin-normalised 1/odds; `simpleScore` → `period_scores` with rounds / kills / goals by sport family + `scoreboard` for the live period) and `bet_settlement` (WON/LOST → result 1/0, HALF_* → void_factor 0.5, VOIDED → void_factor 1) and publishes onto the `oddin.backup` Redis stream. **Settlement is a comparison of two current states, not a log**: a market is voiced only when Bifrost shows it CLOSED with terminal outcomes AND our `markets.status` is still non-terminal ([`internal/dbstate`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/dbstate/)), so restarts, reconnects and the primary's 24 h replay converge without coordination. [`internal/gate`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/gate/) reads two feed-ingester stamps — `feed:primary:last_msg_unix` (every AMQP delivery, at most once per second) and `feed:primary:connected_unix` (every 2 s with a 15 s TTL while the AMQP connection is open, deleted on disconnect via the consumer's `OnDisconnect` hook) — and counts the primary alive while EITHER is fresh: `BIFROST_MODE=auto` publishes after `BIFROST_TAKEOVER_AFTER_SECONDS` (45 s, past the 20 s watchdog suspend) with both stale and stands down the instant one is fresh. The connection stamp exists because the first production restart (2026-09-03) left the connection open but delivery-free for over two minutes (76 s OnConnect flush + 80 s Oddin replay ramp) and the backup took over for 33 s on a healthy feed; on activation every cached snapshot is re-emitted so the suspended catalogue comes back in one pass; `active` forces, `off` idles, empty key idles. **Operator switch**: the **Feed source** control on `/admin/feed` (Backup is branded **CommZilla**, "Communism Mode", in the backoffice; the stored value, the env vars and the service name are all still `backup` / `bifrost-feed`, so the joke lives only in operator-facing copy) (`PUT /admin/feed/source`, Postgres singleton `feed_control` row (migration 0095) = `auto` / `prod` / `backup`, audit-logged, `backup` needs a confirm string) overrides the env default. **Never move this state to Redis**: production Redis is `maxmemory 256mb` + `allkeys-lru`; the first cut's Redis keys were evicted on day one when the backup stream (then MAXLEN 50k × ~35–150 KB snapshots) filled it, a forced Backup silently reverted to Auto, and the stream is now trimmed by time (`XADD … MINID` 60 s). The two liveness stamps stay in Redis because they are refreshed every 1–2 s. `backup` is a real source switch: feed-ingester's `runSourceSwitch` **stops dialling Oddin's broker entirely** (`amqp.Consumer.Paused`, gated on `feedSourceIsBackup`, polled every 2 s so switching back reconnects promptly) and acks any in-flight deliveries without applying them. It used to hold the connection open and merely drop deliveries, on the reasoning that transport liveness stamps kept the alive watchdog quiet — but the watchdog's forced-Backup branch guards bifrost-feed's health and never reads AMQP anyway, so the connection bought nothing. What it cost was visible on 2026-09-04: with the Oddin credentials returning `403 username or password not allowed`, a source the operator had switched off a day earlier was still dialled every 30 s forever. A paused consumer logs the transition, not the poll, suspends the catalogue once and acknowledges with `feed:source:flushed_unix`; bifrost-feed activates only after that acknowledgement (15 s ceiling) so its full re-emit lands on the clean slate; switching back runs the reconnect-style flush + 24 h Oddin replay. Settlement is deliberately outside the switch (dual-source always; apply-once makes it safe and cancel / rollback exist only on AMQP). **Backup means Bifrost only, for the feed** (operator decision 2026-09-03): while forced, feed-ingester makes NO Oddin feed REST call — `automap.Resolver.WithRESTGate` sends fixtures straight to Bifrost and skips tournament-info / competitor-profile fetches, `handler.Deps.RestAllowed` skips recovery requests, the descriptions refresh and competitor backfill are skipped, an AMQP reconnect neither flushes nor replays, and settlement skips its OnConnect recovery request; the alive watchdog guards bifrost-feed's heartbeat + socket (`backupHealthy`) instead of AMQP. Labels then come from Bifrost: the translator sets `<market name=…>` (a non-Oddin attribute; `store.SeedMarketDescriptionIfMissing`, DO NOTHING so REST overwrites) and `<outcome name=…>` (real Oddin attribute → `market_outcomes.name`; player URNs also seed `player_profiles`), and the catalog's competitor / player label chain falls back to that per-instance name before the raw URN. Switching back re-enables REST, replays, and refreshes descriptions + competitor profiles immediately. While active, every resync re-emits all snapshots and sweeps 3 h of results (72 h on activation and every sixth resync — it was 24 h until 2026-09-06, when the unplayed-map cancels started riding the same pass and a day proved too short to reach the fixtures that had closed before the cancel path existed: 940 markets on the 09-05 matches were waiting for a snapshot the 24 h window would never fetch again; a wider window costs list pages only, since detail fetches are limited to matches our DB still holds open) so a long outage of the whole stack heals without manual steps. **Bifrost states a `handicap` specifier from the OPPOSITE side to the AMQP feed, so `bifrost.NormalizeSpecifiers` flips its sign in `parseMarketSegment`** — the one place a market key is built, which is why the correction covers odds and settlement together and the storefront needs no change (it renders the line from the specifier: home as-is, away negated). Measured 2026-09-04: every backup-fed market keyed `handicap=-1.5` priced the HOME outcome SHORTER than that team's own moneyline (CS2 1163395 — moneyline 1.55, "-1.5" 1.15, "+1.5" 2.70), which a real -1.5 line can never be; correct score agreed to two decimals. Systematic across all nine handicap-bearing market ids (100% inverted on backup vs 100% standard on AMQP, flipping the day the source switched). **This had to be fixed at key level, not in the label**: identity is `(match, provider_market_id, specifiers_hash)` and settlement is dual-source, so an inverted key means Oddin's `bet_settlement` grades the OPPOSITE line of the one the backup priced — 19 of the 26 backup-era margin-1 ±1.5 pairs were graded on the AMQP convention, 4 on Bifrost's, 3 contradictory (no money moved: one OZ demo ticket, no handicap leg). Oddin's own AMQP feed carried the same inversion until 2026-06-02, so Bifrost is serving the pre-fix convention — if they ever align, the flip has to come back out, and the moneyline test above is what pins the direction. `handicap` is the only specifier key that ever holds a negative value. Consumers: [`services/feed-ingester/internal/backupstream`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/backupstream/consumer.go) and [`services/settlement/internal/backupstream`](./services/settlement/internal/backupstream/consumer.go) (XREADGROUP, per-service group, routing key `bifrost.backup`; they call the same `Handle` the AMQP path uses and must never bump `lastAmqpMessageUnix`). **Two things about finishing matches, both fixed 2026-09-03 after production stranded them.** (1) Once a match ends, Bifrost answers `match(historic: false)` with a real object (`state: CLOSED`) and an EMPTY `marketGroups`; every settled market is only in the `historic: true` view (measured: 0 vs 532-607 CLOSED markets on three CS2 matches). `Client.FetchMatch` asks both and returns whichever carries markets — returning the first non-nil result fed the results sweep a market-less snapshot forever, so it logged `matches_settled: 0` on every pass while its fetch list grew unbounded. (2) Nothing else closes the match: it leaves the live offer so its subscription goes quiet, and settlement's `MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal` needs EVERY market row we hold to go terminal, so one market Bifrost no longer lists strands it at `live` forever. `translate.OddsChange` therefore emits a lifecycle-only `odds_change` (`sport_event_status status=4`, no `<odds>` block) for a CLOSED match, the results sweep publishes it too, and feed-ingester's `applyLifecycleOnly` applies it — terminal codes only, existing matches only, so a market-less message can never auto-create a fixture. A LIVE match with an all-closed book still says nothing: that is an ordinary between-rounds suspension. Fixture metadata without the REST meta API: [`services/feed-ingester/internal/bifrost`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/bifrost/client.go) reshapes Bifrost's `match` query into `oddinxml.FixtureResponse`; `automap.Resolver.fetchFixtureAny` tries REST then Bifrost and seeds `competitor_profiles` icons. Admin card on `/admin/feed` via `GET /admin/feed/backup-status` (Redis hash `bifrost:feed:status`). `-dry-run` exercises the whole path with no Postgres / Redis. **Unplayed maps are voided (2026-09-06):** on a CLOSED match every open market of ours whose `map` specifier is beyond the last map the snapshot proves was played (highest map on any listed market, or the number of scored periods — [`translate.UnplayedMapCancels`](./services/bifrost-feed/internal/translate/cancel.go)) is voiced as a windowless `bet_cancel` on both the live path and the results sweep; Oddin's own rule for an unplayed map, so no settlement risk, and the shape of 2 231 of the 5 737 open Oddin markets on the 09-05 fixtures (a BO5 that ended 3-0 kept 228 markets open on maps 4 and 5). Dropped ladder lines are NEVER voided here — a played market has a real result — and are inferred from settled siblings in `services/settlement` instead (`settler.ReconcileLadderLines`). **Deliberate gaps (2026-09-03):** no whole-event `bet_cancel` and no `rollback_*` synthesis (Bifrost has no cancel state; a cancelled event shows as CLOSED with markets removed — deferred until Oddin confirms the representation; the operator Void button on `/admin/unsettled` is the fallback), probabilities derived from odds, risk tier assigned manually (migration 0094). Full protocol notes, enum evidence, recovery interplay and runbook: [`docs/BIFROST_BACKUP_FEED.md`](./docs/BIFROST_BACKUP_FEED.md); operation catalogue: [`docs/fixtures/bifrost-operations.graphql`](./docs/fixtures/bifrost-operations.graphql). |
| Tournament risk tier (manual assignment + lock) | [`services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts) — `PATCH /admin/tournaments/:id` accepts `riskTier: 1..10` (sets `risk_tier` AND `risk_tier_locked=true`) or `riskTier: null` (unlocks; value stays until the next REST refresh replaces it), audit-logged; list returns `riskTierLocked`. **The list is filterable and sortable** (2026-09-05): `?sportId`, `?categoryId` (scoped to the sport — football alone carries ~200 country buckets, so an unscoped dropdown would be unusable), `?tier` (1..10 or `unset`, which is a first-class choice since an untiered row is the one an operator most wants to find), `?source` (`auto` / `zagi` / `manual`), plus `?sort=` name / sport / category / tier / source with `?dir=`. Sort keys are an allowlist mapped to columns, never a column name off the query string; every sorted query carries a `name, id` tiebreak so paging cannot repeat or skip a row, and an untiered row sorts to the END whichever way the tier column points, because "no tier" is neither a low tier nor a high one. Clicking an already-descending column returns to the DEFAULT pin order — otherwise that arrangement, the only one in which the reorder arrows mean anything, would be unreachable without clearing every filter. One shared `filtersToQuery` builds every link, because the pager previously assembled its own params and would have silently dropped the new filters on page 2. Since 0106 a manual assignment also stamps `risk_tier_source='manual'`, and reverting to Auto restores the label the VALUE deserves — `zagi` when a review timestamp exists, `auto` otherwise, because crediting the feed with a decision it never made is how the "auto" bucket became unreadable in the first place. **A green check beside an `auto` / `ZAGI` tier CONFIRMS it** (2026-09-07, `ConfirmTierButton`): one click posts the tier that is already stored, so the number never moves and only its provenance does — `manual` + locked, which is precisely what takes the row out of reach of the REST refresh (`AND NOT risk_tier_locked`) and of every future ZillaAGI pass (its selector takes only untiered, unlocked rows). It needs no endpoint of its own and gets no confirm dialog, because a write that cannot change the value has no bad outcome worse than pinning a tier that was already in force; reverse it with the edit form's "Auto / ZAGI" option. Both automatic sources are provisional in exactly the way that makes this worth one click rather than a trip through a ten-item dropdown — Oddin's number can be replaced by its own next refresh, and a ZAGI verdict carries the standing +1 margin *because* nobody has looked at it. The one row that does NOT get the button is an untiered one: there is no number to agree with, and locking NULL would fix the tournament at the strictest tier while hiding it from the reviewer that would otherwise price it. **The ZillaAGI note now survives a confirmation** and is cleared only when the tier actually CHANGES: the note explains one particular number, so replacing the number makes it a lie, but endorsing the number leaves it describing the tier that is still there — and it is the only record anywhere of why that tier is what it is. The `manual` chip's tooltip carries it through as "Original reasoning: …". UI: Risk tier column on [`/admin/tournaments`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/tournaments/tournaments-editor.tsx) (`T<n> manual` / `ZAGI` / `Auto`); a tournament with NO tier renders a highlighted `unset` chip there and a highlighted `T?` via `TierBadge` on the ZillaBoost board, because that row is actionable rather than merely unknown. feed-ingester's `store.UpdateTournamentRiskTier` adds `AND NOT risk_tier_locked`, so neither the per-fixture refresh nor `-backfill-tournament-metadata` overwrites an operator's choice. **An untiered tournament is underwritten at the STRICTEST tier, not the loosest.** `UNTIERED_RISK_TIER = 10` in [`services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/riskzilla/engine.ts) is what `tierFor` resolves a NULL `risk_tier` to (tier 10 = 50 USDC match liability / 25 USDC max payout, versus tier 0's 5000 / 1000). Before 2026-09-03 a NULL fell through to the tier-0 global fallback, which is one of the loosest rows in the table — so a tournament first seen while Oddin's meta API was down was underwritten as if it were a mid-tier event. Bifrost carries no tier at all, which makes that reachable in normal operation, not just in an outage. Fail-safe until an operator assigns a real tier. |
| ZillaAGI (ZAGI) — the in-house LLM | Client [`services/api/src/lib/zagi/client.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/client.ts). One OpenAI-compatible chat endpoint (`https://llm.oddin.gg/v1`, model `glm-5.3-flash`) behind `ZAGI_BASE_URL` / `ZAGI_API_KEY` / `ZAGI_MODEL`, falling back to the `SPORTRADAR_LLM_*` trio that named the same gateway first, so an existing deployment gains ZAGI without an `.env` edit. Three things the client exists to stop each caller rediscovering: the base URL **includes** the `/v1` (it posts to `<base>/chat/completions` and appends nothing); the model must be **pinned**, because `/v1/models` lists more than one and auto-discovery takes an arbitrary first entry; and a reasoning model spends most of `max_tokens` on an internal pass before emitting a character, so `complete()` surfaces `finishReason` + token counts and throws a typed `ZagiEmptyReplyError` rather than returning "" — an exhausted budget and a model with nothing to say need different operator responses. Graceful-idle: no key, no client, and every feature built on it no-ops. |
| ZAGI tournament risk-tier review | Engine [`services/api/src/lib/zagi/risk-tier.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/risk-tier.ts) (prompt, `renderBatch`, `parseTierVerdicts`, `clampTier`, `assignRiskTiers`, `riskTierBacklog`), sweeper [`sweeper.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/sweeper.ts) (30 min, Redis NX lock, boot-delayed, `ZAGI_RISK_TIER_DISABLED=1` skips; a sweep takes 200-row chunks until the queue is empty or a **10-minute budget** is spent, because a fixed rows-per-sweep cap is the wrong shape for a backlog — 200 every 30 min would take three and a half hours to clear a queue the model works through in ten minutes, while the steady state of a few new tournaments finishes on the first chunk. It stops early on no progress, since a transport failure burns no attempt and looping would just repeat it. The Redis lock's TTL floors at the budget plus slack rather than a fraction of the interval, or a second replica would take over mid-sweep at the 5-minute minimum interval), unit tests [`risk-tier.test.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/zagi/risk-tier.test.ts). The prompt tells the model the ceilings AND explicitly NOT to make the safety or outright adjustments itself, so the two halves compose rather than doubling. Admin `GET /admin/tournaments/zagi-status` + `POST /admin/tournaments/zagi-review` (`dryRun` previews, `sportId` scopes, 20/hour, audit-logged as `tournament.zagi_review`, busts `catalog:tournaments:v1:<sportId>`), UI panel at the top of [`/admin/tournaments`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/tournaments/tournaments-editor.tsx). **The model proposes and code disposes** — `resolveTier` applies three tightenings, and every one of them can only RAISE the tier number (lower the liability), never lower it. (1) **+1 always** (`SAFETY_MARGIN_STEPS`, operator decision 2026-09-05): a ZAGI verdict is not reviewed by a person before it takes effect, so it is not taken at face value — if the model says tier 2, the book underwrites tier 3. The consequence worth stating out loud is that **ZAGI can never assign T1**; the loosest tier in the book, 50 000 USDC on one match, is reachable only by an operator typing it. (2) **+3 for OUTRIGHT markets** (`OUTRIGHT_TIER_STEPS`): an outright resolves over a whole season or phase rather than one fixture, so the book holds the position for months, cannot trade out of it match by match, and prices it off standings that move underneath it. Before this, `England. Premier League. Season 26/27` sat at the SAME tier as the Premier League's match markets. `OUTRIGHT_PATTERNS` was measured against the live catalogue, and the near-misses are the interesting part: bare `head-to-head` is EXCLUDED because in this feed it is dominated by single-event markets (`Vuelta a Espana. Stage 13. Head-to-head`, `Formula-1. Grand Prix. Italy. Race. Head-to-head` — 12 of 31 rows), `champion` is excluded because it matches `Scotland. Championship` and `Gaelic football. Galway Championship`, which are ordinary leagues, and **`season NN/NN` was excluded the hard way**: it shipped in 0107, and it is how Fonbet names the CURRENT SEASON OF A LEAGUE, so `England. Premier League. Season 26/27` (12 fixtures, 812 active markets) and `Italy. Serie A. Season 26/27` (16, 1 102) were stepped as if they were season bets. That cut the per-match budget on the biggest football markets by three tiers and pushed the Premier League below the Championship in the sidebar, which is how it was caught; migration 0109 reverted the eight leagues involved. **The lesson is in the method, not the regex** — the pattern set had been checked against NAMES and never against what the rows CONTAINED, and a genuine outright is trivially distinguishable: 0-2 active markets and no fixtures under it, against a league's hundreds. (3) The per-sport ceiling: `SPORT_TIER_CEILING` is the operator's own rule made mechanical — the tier measures a competition's standing in WORLD sport, not inside its own sport, so only football / basketball / tennis / american-football may reach T1, handball and the other mid-size sports stop at T2, real-but-narrow circuits (cycling, darts, snooker, table tennis, badminton, futsal, chess) at T3, and anything unlisted at a cautious T4. `clampTier` can only ever RAISE a number, never lower one, so a model that proposes something stricter than the ceiling is taken at its word. A second floor at T9 catches simulations: Fonbet files FC 26 under Football and NBA 2K26 under Basketball, so the sport ceiling alone would let a machine playing itself on a 2x4-minute clock through at T1 — `SIMULATED_PATTERNS` anchors on game titles, known simulated-league brands and the short clock (accepting Cyrillic `х`, which the English catalogue mixes in), and deliberately omits bare "Liga Pro", a real table-tennis circuit as well as a Fonbet FC bracket. Eligibility is narrow — no tier, not locked, active, under `MAX_REVIEW_ATTEMPTS` — and every UPDATE re-asserts it, so an operator who assigns a tier while the model is thinking wins. **The sweeper therefore never revisits a tier of any kind, manual or not**: `risk_tier IS NULL` is the first condition, so a row that HAS a number is invisible to it forever, and re-review after a bad verdict means clearing the tier first. Worth stating because the reverse gets suspected — a `manual` T1 that later reads `ZAGI T2` is not the sweeper; it is migrations 0107 (+1, then +3 on the season-named leagues) and 0109 (the revert) moving a row that was `zagi` all along, on the day the safety margin shipped. Checked against production 2026-09-07 after exactly that report on `England. Premier League. Season 26/27`: `admin_audit_log` held ONE `tournament.update` for that tournament, and it was the operator's fix (`2/zagi/unlocked → 1/manual/locked`) — the remembered earlier T1 was ZAGI's own raw first-run verdict at 20:10Z on 09-05, two and a half hours before 0107 added the margin that makes T1 unreachable. Two manual tier assignments existed on the whole estate. Any future instance of this question is one `admin_audit_log` query away from an answer, and the audit chain (0026) makes that record authoritative. A transport failure burns no attempt (it is our problem, not the row's); only a reply that ignored the row does. Measured on the real backlog before shipping: 45/45 decided in 14 s, ceilings respected, simulated caught, and a prompt-injection probe planted in a tournament name was ignored and rated T9. |
| Settlement payout math + tests | `services/settlement/internal/settler/payout.go` |
| Settlement workflows (settle + cancel + rollbacks + cancel-after-settle + per-generation ledger refs) | `services/settlement/internal/settler/settler.go`; `services/settlement/internal/store/store.go` (`nextPayoutRefID`, `LatestUnreversedPayoutRefID`) |
| Stranded-ticket reconciler (settle path missed a leg-result write) | `settler.ReconcileStranded` + `store.HealStrandedSelections` / `store.SettleReadyAcceptedTickets`; periodic sweeper `runReconcileSweeper` in `services/settlement/cmd/settlement/main.go` (`SETTLEMENT_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 300, <=0 disables). The per-message cascade (`ApplyOutcomeToSelections`) only writes a leg's `result` on the FRESH settle (`InsertIfNew inserted=true`); a replay skips it, so a selection that became settle-eligible after that pass (ticket still in bet-delay, recovery message reorder) stayed `result IS NULL` on an already-terminal market → ticket stuck `accepted` forever (diagnosed 2026-06-01). The reconciler heals NULL legs from the authoritative current `market_outcomes.result`/`void_factor` on a terminal (`status ∈ {-3,-4}`) market for still-`accepted` tickets, then settles any now-fully-resolved ticket via the normal phase-2 path. Reads current DB truth, never message replays, so a rolled-back market (`status=1`, NULL result) is never touched and the replay-gated rollback path can't be defeated. The `applyMarketSettle` `inserted` gate is intentionally left unchanged for exactly this reason. **The heal UPDATE had never actually run until 2026-09-04**: it joined `tickets` in the FROM clause on `t.id = ts.ticket_id`, and Postgres forbids referencing the UPDATE target from a FROM-clause join condition, so every sweep failed with `42P01 invalid reference to FROM-clause entry for table "ts"` and logged `reconcile sweep failed`. The ticket-status check is an EXISTS subquery now. Anything that writes to a table it is also filtering through a join needs the same care. |
| Bet-delay evaluator + tests | `services/bet-delay/internal/worker/worker.go` |
| Chain scanners (Go) | `services/wallet-watcher/internal/{ethereum,tron}/`; shared confirmation tick in `internal/deposits/` |
| Frontend live-odds + ticket WS | [`apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-live-odds.ts), [`use-ticket-stream.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-ticket-stream.ts) |
| WS socket identity vs session | [`apps/web/src/lib/ws-session-sync.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/ws-session-sync.tsx) — mounted in the `(main)` layout, publishes the SSR-resolved user id into the shared socket via `setExpectedSessionUser()`. The gateway authenticates ONCE at upgrade (invariant 7), so a socket opened logged-out stays anonymous through a login and never receives `user:{id}` ticket frames; the same happens when a tab reconnects after a gateway restart with an already-expired 15-minute access cookie. On `hello.userId !== expectedUserId` the connection closes itself and the reconnect re-reads the cookie, capped at `MAX_AUTH_RECONNECTS` so an expired cookie (only a navigation through the Next.js middleware refreshes it) can't spin. |
| Ticket resolution during the bet-delay window | [`bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) — `applyTicketResolution()` is fed by TWO transports: the `user:{id}` WS frame (fast path) and an HTTP poll of `GET /bets/:id` starting just after `notBeforeTs`.
