The poll is not redundancy for its own sake: when the frame never arrived the button sat on "Placing…", timed out, and reverted to "Place bet" **with the slip still populated** while the bet was already accepted and the stake debited. Nothing upstream catches the resulting second click — every submit mints a fresh `idempotencyKey`, so the retry is a genuinely new ticket. Observed in production 2026-09-03. |
| Frontend bet slip store | [`apps/web/src/lib/bet-slip.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/bet-slip.tsx) |
| Bet slip right-rail UI | [`apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx) |
| Shell components | [`apps/web/src/components/shell/`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/) — `top-bar.tsx`, `top-bar-search.tsx` (debounced global search popover), `sidebar.tsx` (auto-expands tournament sub-tree under the active sport), `bet-slip-rail.tsx`, `theme-toggle.tsx`, `zillapass-indicator.tsx` (signed-in-only progress chip beside the search bar — polls `GET /zillapass/me` every 30 s, renders `completed/total` + a mini progress bar; click opens a popover with per-task progress and an "Open ZillaPass" link to the full page) |
| ZillaPass (TS, scaffold) | API: [`services/api/src/modules/zillapass/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/zillapass/routes.ts) (`GET /zillapass/me` — bundles active tasks + per-period progress + level / xp / streak) + [`services/api/src/modules/admin/zillapass.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/zillapass.ts) (admin task CRUD; audit-logged). Schema: [`packages/db/src/schema/zillapass.ts`](./packages/db/src/schema/zillapass.ts), migration `0060_zillapass`. Storefront: [`apps/web/src/components/shell/zillapass-indicator.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/zillapass-indicator.tsx) + [`apps/web/src/app/(main)/zillapass/`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/zillapass/). Admin: [`apps/web/src/app/admin/zillapass/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/zillapass/) (linked under **Storefront → ZillaPass**). Shared types: [`packages/types/src/zillapass.ts`](./packages/types/src/zillapass.ts). **Storefront i18n**: task titles / descriptions / CTA labels are DB rows (seeded in English), so the storefront translates them per-slug via `zillapass.taskCatalog` in [`apps/web/messages/*.json`](./apps/web/messages/) through the [`useZillapassTaskText`](./apps/web/src/lib/zillapass-i18n.ts) helper — slugs without a catalog entry (admin-created tasks) fall back to the DB English text, and operator edits to seeded wording must be mirrored into the message files or every locale keeps showing the catalog copy. No service writes progress today — predicate hooks land in a follow-up once the product team finalises task kinds. |
| Tournament logo sourcing (Wikidata + Wikipedia + Liquipedia) | [`services/api/src/lib/tournament-logos/`](./services/api/src/lib/tournament-logos/) — `resolver.ts` (ZAGI canonical-name prompt + strict parser + ZAGI pair adjudication + orchestration + byte download), `wikidata.ts`, `wikipedia.ts`, `liquipedia.ts`, `sweeper.ts` (hourly, Redis-locked, **time**-budgeted at 10 min because Liquipedia asks 2 s between calls), guards unit-tested in `wikidata.test.ts` + `wikipedia.test.ts`. Admin `GET /admin/tournaments/logo-status` + `POST /admin/tournaments/logo-fetch` (`dryRun` previews, 10/hour, audit-logged). Migration 0108. **Why third parties at all:** Fonbet's `line/logos` catalogue is fully consumed at 462 rows — that is everything they have — and the Oddin esports half has no first-party source, because the Oddin REST token 403s and the stack has run on the Bifrost backup feed since 2026-09-03, whose `Tournament` type exposes `sport { icon }` and no tournament icon. **The split of labour is the design:** ZAGI supplies the canonical competition name plus up to two aliases (nothing local knows `Spain. Primera Division` is La Liga, that `ESL Challenger League Season 52: Europe - Cup #6` is an instance whose logo belongs to the series, or that LCK is also `League of Legends Champions Korea`), and code then does an EXACT lookup and refuses anything whose page title or entity label does not match the name asked for. That guard is the whole safety story, and every case in it is a real observed failure: `EuroLeague` → `EuroLeagueWomen.png`, Finland's `Liiga` → `Naisten Liiga logo.png` (the men's entities carry no logo claim, so a sport-only filter falls through to the women's competition), `Eredivisie` → the Dutch ICE HOCKEY league, and Liquipedia's *search* for `PGL Wallachia` → the page **Team Falcons**, whose infobox image is a team crest — which is why pages are looked up by title and never searched. Bytes are downloaded and stored in `logo_data` rather than hot-linked (Commons and Liquipedia are not our CDN; the storefront already serves `/api/tournaments/:id/logo`, and the api's `default-src 'none'` CSP is what makes serving third-party SVG safe). Liquipedia leads for the esports slugs that have a wiki and Wikidata for everything else, with Wikipedia's infobox as the third pass — the highest-coverage source for traditional sport and the least free, so `extractInfoboxLogo` prefers `logo`/`badge`, accepts `image` only when the FILENAME names a mark, and rejects JPEGs outright (the real miss it exists for: `Test cricket` has no logo field and its `image` is a match photograph). **Liquipedia is gated behind `LIQUIPEDIA_API_KEY`** and idles without one: an unkeyed run took `429` on 75 of 76 lookups despite correct 2 s pacing, and a rate-limit answer now aborts the sweep rather than burning attempts. That gate is what caps esports coverage — league marks are non-free, so Wikidata and Commons largely do not carry them, and a split-scoped name (`LEC 2026 Summer Playoffs`, `VCT 2026: Pacific Stage 2`) has no entity of its own even when the series does. **`maxTokens` on the ZAGI calls is a ceiling, not a cost** — the pair-adjudication call ran at `4_000` on the reasoning that a one-pair yes/no is cheap, and every error in the first production sweep (9 of 9) was `zagi returned an empty reply after exhausting max_tokens on reasoning`: this model runs its reasoning pass FIRST and emits text only afterwards, so a tight ceiling returns an EMPTY reply, which reads as `unsure`, which is silent. Same trap the support bot and the Sportradar adjudicator hit; 16 000 everywhere. **Measured, not estimated (2026-09-06, production):** 321 of the 610 tournaments carrying a bookable match — Fonbet 464 across the whole table, plus 81 from Wikidata and 55 from Wikipedia that no feed had. Per sport, the shape is the argument for the third-party pass: tennis 58/58, Gaelic 17/17, badminton 10/10, football 129/292, ice hockey 13/18 — and the esports slugs at or near zero for the Liquipedia reason above. Nothing has exhausted its three attempts yet, so the hourly sweep is still closing the gap; the residue is county championships, third divisions and simulated fixtures that have a logo nowhere, which ZAGI correctly declines rather than guessing at. Historic figure for reference: 60% of the tournaments that could plausibly have a logo, landing the marquee events (The International, IEM, DreamLeague, LEC, Esports World Cup, MLB, Premier League, La Liga); the misses are county championships, third divisions and simulated fixtures that have a logo nowhere, which ZAGI correctly declines rather than guessing at. |
| Match list ordering (prominence, then time) | `matchListOrder()` in [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts), applied by `/catalog/sports/:slug` and `/catalog/matches` (lobby, `/live`, `/upcoming`). Until 2026-09-05 the sport page sorted live-before-upcoming then purely by kickoff, and `/catalog/matches` sorted the live tab by `matches.id DESC` — which is insertion order. On a line as broad as Fonbet's that means the top of Football's live list was `Venezuela. Division 2` and `Brazil. Women. Series A1` while Brazil's Serie A sat below the fold: the ordering carried no notion of which match anyone wants. Two operator rules now: **(1)** among matches competing for the top, LOWER `risk_tier` ranks higher — and that comparison **does not care whether a match is live**, so a T2 fixture an hour from kickoff outranks every live T3-and-worse game, and a live T1 game outranks it in turn; the tier is already our best statement of how big a competition is, and it finally exists for the traditional line too (ZillaAGI, migration 0106); **(2)** a prematch match joins that competition inside **its own tier's window** — `PREMATCH_HOIST_WINDOWS`, operator's numbers 2026-09-06: **T1 12 h, T2 6 h, T3 3 h, T4 1 h**, everything else never. Prominence and imminence trade off against each other, which is why the window narrows with the tier: a Champions League tie is worth showing half a day out, a T4 fixture only once it is about to start. Everything else stays chronological — a "what's on soon" list sorted by prestige would be actively worse — with the tier only breaking ties. Untiered rows sort as 99: last within their group, never promoted by missing information. **The window map's `ELSE NULL` is load-bearing twice over**: it is what excludes T5+ and untiered rows (`scheduled_at <= now() + NULL` is NULL, not true), and an `interval '0'` default would instead hoist every wedged `not_started` match whose kickoff has already passed. That NULL propagates out of the OR, so the expression is three-valued and the `COALESCE(..., false)` is what stops the `featured` column serialising as `null` for exactly the rows it must report `false` for. **The storefront must group on `featured`, never on status.** The API returns the predicate's own answer as a column on both list endpoints precisely so the client does not carry a second copy of the windows; the sport page had been re-splitting the tier-sorted region by `status === "live"`, which put the nine Premier League / La Liga / Ligue 1 fixtures the rule had promoted to positions 1-9 underneath 91 live youth and women's games — the exact inversion the ordering exists to prevent (measured on production 2026-09-06). Its top section is named after what is in it (`Live` / `Starting soon` / `Live & starting soon`), because a header claiming "Live" over a list led by kickoff times is worse than no header. Verified against production before shipping — Football's live list led with Serie A (T4) instead of Venezuela Division 2, five upcoming US Open (T2) fixtures hoisted above live T3 MLB, and under the per-tier windows four T2 fixtures sat above live T3 Serie A. **Still status-split**: the lobby, `/live` and `/upcoming` each fetch one status per request, so the promotion cannot show there without merging those fetches; within a status they are correctly tier-ordered. |
| UI primitives | [`apps/web/src/components/ui/`](./apps/web/src/components/ui/) — `primitives.tsx` (Button, Pill, LiveDot, Tabs, OddButton, TeamMark, which renders the crest when `logoUrl` is set and NOTHING otherwise or on `<img onError>` — the monogram-of-initials fallback was removed 2026-09-05 because "KY/" beside a tennis doubles pair read as a fake badge; two-row surfaces (match list, scoreboard) hold an empty same-sized slot when only one side has a picture so the names stay flush, Divider), `icons.tsx`, `monogram.tsx`, `sport-glyph.tsx`, `tier-mark.tsx` (gold "Top" star + `isFeaturedTier()` predicate, **`risk_tier` 1..3 since 2026-09-05** — widened from `{1,2}` because ZillaAGI now tiers the traditional line too and its standing +1 safety margin means a machine verdict can never produce a T1, which left the whole Fonbet offer with four featured tournaments between them; three tiers restores a meaningful Top set on both halves of the catalogue without an operator having to raise a competition's liability budget just to put a star on it; rendered next to the tournament name on match cards, the sidebar tournament tree, top-bar search hits, and the match-detail header. Featured cards also pick up a 2px gold left-edge accent and a "TOP" pill on the detail page; both tiers share one visual treatment per the user's "просто Top для обоих этих тиров" call) |
| Market names carry their sub-event (all sports) | [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) — Fonbet ships a sub-event market as `"<sub-event>: <market>"` (`"3rd set aces: Match result"`). The route splits that prefix into the match page's scope TAB, and until 2026-09-05 it also stripped it from `market.name`, on the reasoning that the tab already says it. **That reasoning only holds where the tab is on screen.** `market.name` is what the bet slip stores as `marketLabel` (see the `add()` call in [`live-markets.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-markets.tsx)), what bet history renders, and what a copied community ticket shows — none of which carry a tab. Observed on production: a leg on the 3rd-set-ACES three-way rendered in the slip as `MATCH RESULT / 1 / Michelsen A vs Merida Aguilar D` at 2.45, indistinguishable from a match-winner bet and priced nothing like one. The prefix now stays on the name; only `baseName` (the small market-kind tag, and the line-grouping key) is still stripped. Repetition inside one tab is a cosmetic cost, an unidentifiable leg on a money surface is not. Oddin markets were never affected — their templates already carry the map (`"Total kills {threshold} - map {map}"`). **A second stripping path survived that pass and was closed on 2026-09-07**: `familyTitle` in [`live-markets.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-markets.tsx) titles a LADDER card (a handicap or total family, which has no single `market.name` to show) from `baseName`, and dropped the sub-event whenever the market's own tab was the one on screen — the same "the tab says it" reasoning, in the other codebase. It failed the same way and one step more visibly, because a SINGLE market on that very tab renders `m.name` and never dropped it: a football fixture showed "To hit the woodwork 1st half hit the woodwork: Match result" directly above a ladder headed "Handicap", while the leg that ladder recorded said "…woodwork…: Handicap 0". The prefix is now unconditional for sub-event scopes (map scopes still skip it — the map is already in the name). |
| Market names name the TEAM, not a number (all sports) | [`applyTeamNumberLabel`](./services/api/src/lib/market-naming.ts), applied by `substituteTemplate` — which every market-name render goes through, so one rule covers the match page, list cards, the bet-slip leg label, bet history, ZillaFlash / ZillaBoost banners, push copy and the RiskZilla betticker. Fonbet names a per-team market by NUMBER, differently in each language — "Team 1 totals {threshold}", "Team Totals-1", "1 to win", "Инд. тоталы-1", "Победа 1" — and in a couple of captions its own `%1` / `%2` team placeholder leaks into the market name. Rendered verbatim that reads **"Team 1 totals 2.5" on a page showing Swansea vs Wrexham**: a market about exactly one of them that does not say which, and the same string is what the slip stores as the leg label, so the bettor cannot tell afterwards either. Number 1 is the home side and 2 the away side — the convention Fonbet's own factor ids use (`1` home / `2` away / `3` draw). Six anchored patterns, verified by rendering the entire live catalogue (2026-09-05): 198 distinct market-name shapes gain the team, and the near misses stay untouched — "1x2", "Score after 2 goals scored", "Score after 2 maps", "Score after 2 sets", "score in the series after 2 matches". Anything matching nothing is returned unchanged, so a caption Fonbet adds later degrades to today's wording rather than to a WRONG team. **Market names only**: `renderOutcomeLabel` deliberately calls the inner `substituteCore` instead, because an outcome cell sits under a header that already names the team and Fonbet writes some outcome captions mid-sentence ("Фрейм %P: инд. тотал-2 ударов Больше"), where swapping the number in reads as broken grammar — measured 0 of 25 096 outcome templates touched. A caller with no match in hand (the admin feed log, the backoffice market pickers) keeps the generic caption, which is the right label there. Esports never had this problem: Oddin marks the side structurally (`{side}` specifier, bare `home` / `away` outcome templates) and that path is unchanged — this is the same fix for a catalogue that numbers its teams instead of naming them. **The outcome side of it was fixed at the SOURCE instead, on 2026-09-07** ([`descriptions.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/internal/mapper/descriptions.go)): a line table captions its two TEAM columns "1" and "2" — literally, which is exactly how `sideCaptions` already reads them when it keys the outcomes `h1` / `h2` — so every Fonbet handicap ladder on the storefront was headed by a pair of bare numbers, and the leg it recorded said "1" for a team. `OutcomeTemplate` now emits the bare `home` / `away` word for those columns, the same thing the `%1` / `%2` captions beside it already produced, so `renderOutcomeLabel` resolves the team in every locale with no rewrite rule and nothing on the general outcome path loosened. Guarded on the caption being ONLY the number: two multi-row tables prefix their row ("1st substitution 1", "Handicap from 1 to 6 min 2") and there the row IS the outcome's identity, so those keep it (measured 2026-09-07: 16 of the 18 tables carrying `h1`/`h2` caption them bare). The match-winner factor ids got the same treatment in the same pass — a sub-event market is keyed by factor id rather than the canonical 1/2/3, so "1st half: Match result" read "1 / X / 2" where the match tab read the team names; double-chance cells keep their caption, since no single team names "1X". Two consequences elsewhere, both display-only: `outcomeSortWeight` now weights `h1`/`h2` as 1/2 (they parse as non-numeric, so the ladder's two columns had been falling back to Postgres' undefined row order — harmless while both read "1"/"2", not once each carries a team name), and `isAwayHandicapSide` recognises them, which fixes a separate standing bug where EVERY Fonbet handicap printed the home line on both sides (a home −0.5 showed as "−0.5" on the away column, where the feed itself says "+0.5"). |
| Collapsible market cards (match page) | [`apps/web/src/lib/use-collapsed-markets.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/use-collapsed-markets.ts) + `CollapseToggle` / `entryKindKey` in [`live-markets.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-markets.tsx) (2026-09-06, operator request). Every market card — singles and line families alike — folds down to its title: a chevron button at the far right of the card header is the accessible control (`aria-expanded`), the title text toggles too, and a "Collapse all / Expand all" text button sits in the scope-tab row beside the BetBuilder pill (that row now always renders). **State is keyed by market KIND, not by row**: `marketKindKey(providerMarketId, variant)` — so folding "Handicap" on one football match keeps it folded on the next, while Fonbet's "Total" under Match, Corners and 1st half (same table id, different `variant`) fold independently. Persisted in `localStorage["oz:collapsed-markets"]`, read in an effect (never the initializer, or SSR and hydration disagree), every access guarded. A collapsed line family's title-only suspended branch is unchanged — it already has no body. |
| Market scopes (the match page's tabs) | Grammar + derivation: [`packages/types/src/market-scope.ts`](./packages/types/src/market-scope.ts). Discovery for the backoffice: [`services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts). A scope is one tab on `/match/:id` AND the string stored in `fe_market_display_order.scope` / `fe_market_groups.scope`: `match`, `map_<N>`, `fb_<kinds>` (a Fonbet sub-event — `fb:400100/10100201` → `fb_400100_10100201`, with every per-player variant collapsed into `fb_players`), `top`, `custom_<key>`. **The label is not in the id**: Fonbet ships it as a prefix on the market-name template ("1st half corners: Match result"), so deriving a scope needs the template as well as the specifiers — which is why `deriveScope(specs)` (Match / Map N only) survives alongside `deriveMarketScope({specifiers, template})` for the callers that only ever see Oddin markets. Three consumers must agree byte-for-byte or the backoffice configures a tab the storefront never renders: the api's match-detail route, the web admin (which validates the scope in the URL and labels the tabs), and the DB CHECK constraints that mirror the regexes (migrations 0084, 0106). It lives in `@oddzilla/types` rather than `@oddzilla/db` for exactly one reason — apps/web needs it and must not depend on the db package; import it by subpath (`@oddzilla/types/market-scope`), never through the barrel, per the `oddzilla-types-barrel-imports` footgun. `@oddzilla/db` re-exports the predicates so schema consumers keep importing them from there. |
| Live / Pre-match section tabs | [`apps/web/src/components/lobby/section-tabs.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/lobby/section-tabs.tsx), rendered as the `MatchListTabs` first-group label on the lobby, [`/live`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/live/page.tsx), [`/upcoming`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/upcoming/page.tsx) and, since 2026-09-06, the sport page [`/sport/:slug`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/sport/[slug]/page.tsx), where it takes the slot the page's own counted kicker held (`SectionLabel`, "Live and soon · 30") and carries `sport={slug}` so each tab lands on its section page already filtered to that sport. It labels the FEATURED group — #657's tier-hoist partition is untouched, only its header changed. The sport page's "100 matches" subtitle went in the same change; like the kickers' counts it printed a slice of that page's own 100-row fetch, not a total. **Both tabs render on all four surfaces.** Until 2026-09-06 the strip existed only on the lobby: `/live` and `/upcoming` each printed their OWN heading, so picking a section removed the other tab from the page — the two sections were dead ends reachable from each other only through the browser's back button or the lobby. The selected tab is now MARKED (full-strength label, 2px underline, `aria-current="page"`) rather than being the only one on screen, and the unselected one dims and drops to `<span>` so the page keeps exactly one `<h1>`. Underline machinery is gated on `[data-selectable="true"]` so the lobby — which shows both sections at once and therefore selects neither — keeps its original flat spacing. **The sport chip rides along**: on `/live?sport=football` the Pre-match tab links to `/upcoming?sport=football`. **The tabs carry NO count** (removed 2026-09-06). They did for one day, and the number was the length of the page's own fetch — `/catalog/matches?…&limit=60` rendered as "Pre-match 60" on a line carrying thousands of prematch fixtures, and "Live 120" beside a sidebar badge reading 126 — because `/catalog/matches` returns a page, not a total, and there is no cheap true prematch count to put there. `/live` and `/upcoming` stopped fetching the sibling list they had been pulling only to print that number. The sidebar's Live badge (`/catalog/live-counts`) remains the one true live total. The strip also renders in the empty-list branch, where it matters most: a bettor on an empty Live list is exactly the one who needs the way over to Pre-match. |
| Ended matches leave an open list | `endedIds` + `shownOf` in [`apps/web/src/components/match/match-list-tabs.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-list-tabs.tsx) (2026-09-07). **A list page is a one-shot SSR snapshot** — the lobby, `/live`, `/upcoming` and `/sport/:slug` each fetch `/catalog/matches` once per render, and nothing re-fetches it: no `revalidate`, no poll, no `router.refresh()`, and the groups are fixed arrays the server computed. So a tab left open on the lobby used to fill its **Live** section with finished games. The `matchStatus` frame did arrive (settlement publishes it from `MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal` — [`settler.go`](./services/settlement/internal/settler/settler.go), and the provider-neutral twin in [`external.go`](./services/settlement/internal/settler/external.go)) and `mergeMatchWithLive` flipped `match.status`, so the row DEGRADED correctly — LIVE dot gone, scoreboard highlight gone, prices nulled by the market's own `-3` / `-4` frame — but it stayed exactly where the server put it, under a header reading Live. It is now dropped from the rendered list instead. **Terminal only, deliberately not `suspended`**: every AMQP reconnect runs `FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog`, which moves every live match to `suspended` for the seconds until Oddin's replay re-activates it, so dropping those would blank the whole visible list on a routine feed blip — and since nothing re-ADDS a row, it would stay blank until a reload, which is far worse than a row marked non-live and priced at an em dash. **The fix is one-sided and that is the known limit**: a match that goes live after page load still does not appear, and one that kicks off stays in the Pre-match group with a LIVE pill. Both need a periodic re-fetch of the list, which would also want the list extracted from the lobby server component so a poll does not re-render ComboZilla and the banner rows. Filtering is by ID, not object identity — `/live` and `/upcoming` pass the same rows through two separate `.map(enrich)` calls, so `matches` and the group hold different objects for the same match (the same reason `renderRow` looks the merged row up by id). The `emptyMessage` prop exists because each page's own "no matches" copy is derived from the SSR list and cannot see this case: on a narrow list — one live tennis match on `/live?sport=tennis` finishing — the page would otherwise render its section header over a void. **This is NOT the removed storefront time-gate** and must not be confused with it (see that row below): the gate hid a *wedged, still-bettable* `not_started` match from lists while placement went on accepting it, which is why it was removed. This drops a match the book has already closed and settled, whose markets are terminal and whose prices are already locked — hiding nothing that is bettable, and suppressing no evidence of a bug. |
| Match-list layout — Default / Pro | Switch + persistence in [`match-list-tabs.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-list-tabs.tsx) (`localStorage["oz:match-list-layout"]`, namespaced per signed-in bettor like the existing column count; unreadable or absent falls back to `default`, so nobody is put in a layout they never picked). **The preference is ALSO mirrored into a host-scoped cookie, `oz_list_layout`, so the server renders the chosen layout on the first paint** ([`lib/list-layout-cookie.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/list-layout-cookie.ts) + [`lib/list-layout.tsx`](./apps/web/src/lib/list-layout.tsx)): the `(main)` layout reads it with `cookies()` and provides it through `ListLayoutProvider`, `MatchListTabs` seeds its state from `useInitialListLayout()`, and the localStorage effect only ever CORRECTS — it overrides solely when storage holds a real value, so a bettor who toggled under one account and now views under another (or logged out) is not flipped back to Default by an absent key. Without the cookie SSR always rendered the cards and a Pro user watched three cards flip into eleven rows on every navigation; the column toggle has the same shape and the codebase accepted it there because that control is invisible below 2000px, where a card-to-table flip is visible on every screen. Same reason the theme has a pre-hydration boot script — a cookie is the SSR-side equivalent for state that changes the component TREE rather than a stylesheet. Proven on the raw HTML, not the hydrated DOM: `curl -H 'Cookie: oz_list_layout=pro' /live` returns one `oz-pro-table` and no `oz-match-list-grid`, no cookie the reverse, a garbage value the reverse. **The split into two files is load-bearing**: the first cut exported the parser from the `"use client"` module and called it from the server layout, which typecheck, lint AND `next build` accept — it threw only on the first real request (`Attempted to call parseListLayoutCookie() from the server but parseListLayoutCookie is on the client`), rendering every list page empty. That is the `nextjs-client-boundary-values` footgun; anything a server component needs lives in the plain module, the provider / hook / cookie writer in the client one. **Default** is the card stack ([`match-row.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-row.tsx)); **Pro** is the dense tournament-grouped table ([`match-table.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-table.tsx)), modelled on fon.bet's own list and added 2026-09-07. A card spends ~140px of page per fixture — right for a handful of esports matches, wrong for the Fonbet line, where Football alone puts nineteen fixtures in front of a bettor; measured on one 1500px viewport, Default showed 3 fixtures and Pro 11 across 7 tournament groups (34px per row, ~44px stacked below 600px). **The alignment is the feature, and one CSS variable carries it**: `--oz-pro-cell-w` is declared on `.oz-pro-group` and read by BOTH the header captions and every row's price cells, so the columns line up down the whole group — a per-row width would reintroduce the ragged edge the cards already have. The column SET is derived once per PAGE — `resultColumnsFor` / `marketGroupsFor` over every fixture on the list — not per tournament group. It was per group for a day, and the operator caught why that is wrong: the clock, score and names sit LEFT of the odds track, so a group one cell narrower (a football 1X2 that came back two-way at half time with its draw suspended) slid its clock 46px off the rows above and below. Columns are now the widest any row on the page needs, dashed where a fixture lacks the market — fon.bet's rule as well; derived from the SSR market STRUCTURE rather than from prices, so a live tick can never add or remove a column mid-session. Captions are the literal `1` / `X` / `2` and are NOT translated — they are the same three characters on every book in every language. The row is a **stretched link**: the anchor holds the team names and its `::after` covers the row, so the whole strip navigates without nesting the price buttons inside an `<a>` (which is what the Default card does, and is invalid HTML); the chevron needs `pointer-events: none` or it paints above that `::after` and punches a dead 16px hole in the row. The clock/score block is fixed-width and **left**-aligned so the monospaced clock starts at the same x on every row and the score lands in a column behind it — right-aligning gave a clean right edge and a ragged score, because Fonbet's `comment` varies in length ("(0-0)" against "(0-1) +4 min"); the comment is the only part allowed to shrink. **One segmented control, three options — cards, two-column cards, table** (`ListControls`, 2026-09-07; it was a layout switch plus a separate column toggle for a day, two groups for one question). The two-column button keeps the ≥2000px gate the two-column GRID has always had, now on the button itself (`.oz-match-list-opt-2col`): below that width two cards per row would each be under ~450px, the grid rule inside the same media query renders a saved "2" as one column anyway, and a visible button whose effect the stylesheet undoes reads as a bug where a hidden one reads as "not at this width". When a saved two-column view is being shown as one column, the cards button takes the active state via `:has()` so the control never shows no selection. **Source order is load-bearing**: the button's base `display: none` must come BEFORE the ≥2000px rule that shows it — equal specificity, so the later one wins, and the first cut had it after and the button appeared at no width (measured at 2100: still `none`). The stored model is unchanged (`oz:match-list-cols` + `oz:match-list-layout` + the cookie), so nobody's saved choice moved; picking table leaves the column count alone, so switching back returns to it. **One tournament can head two groups on the same page and that is not a grouping bug** — MatchListTabs renders one table per section, and a league with one fixture inside its tier's hoist window and one outside is split across both, exactly as its cards already are. **Three market groups, as fon.bet has: Result, Handicap, Total.** The captions are one row rather than fon.bet's two tiers (they put the group names in a per-SPORT bar; ours is per-tournament, so repeating them on every group would be noise), and the sub-captions are made self-describing instead — `1 X 2` / `H1 H2` / `Total O U`, translated except for the three betting symbols. A handicap cell carries its own line beside its price, so two identically-shaped columns stay readable; a total's line is shared by its two prices, so it sits in a chip that is deliberately not a button. **Column groups are progressive, keyed on the LIST's width via a container query rather than the viewport's** — the shell spends 240px on the sidebar and 380px on the rail, so a 1500px window leaves the list ~836px while a 900px tablet with neither leaves ~880: a viewport media query would hide columns that fit and show columns that do not. Result below 560px, +Total from 560, +Handicap from 700, roomier cells from 920. Header captions and row cells carry the same `data-group`, so a group can never half-appear and there is no SSR/client mismatch; where `@container` is unsupported the base rules apply and the list shows Result only, a functional degrade rather than a broken one. Cell widths are **fixed px, not a vw clamp** — the first cut clamped against `vw`, grew every cell to its maximum on a 1500px desktop and left two team names sharing 124px (measured, which is why these are numbers rather than a formula). A PAGE on which no row quotes a handicap (an esports list) gets no handicap column; a page where any row does gets it on every row, dashed where a fixture lacks it — that is what the alignment is for. **An operator-authored QUESTION event (`inlineMarkets` set) renders as a title-only row with no price cells** and is left out of the column derivation — the Default card replaces its match-up with the event's markets for exactly the reason the Pro row must not print `Home — Away` for it: its two "sides" are answers, and a `1` / `2` caption over their prices would tell a bettor which answer is playing at home; the answers live on the match page, one click away, where they can be labelled as what they are. **Two memo details are load-bearing, not tidiness**: `marketGroupsFor` returns one of four module-constant arrays (a fresh array per call failed `ProRow`'s shallow compare on every row on every tick, which the first cut did), and the ladder tick-merge runs ONLY in Pro — under the card layout it would mint a new `match` object for every handicap tick on a row that shows no handicap, re-rendering cards the tick never touched, so Default keeps its pre-Pro render behaviour byte-for-byte. **Both layouts show the same live headline** — clock, score, the feed's parenthetical (`45:57 0:0 +2 min`) — through one component, [`live-meta.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/live-meta.tsx): the Pro row's meta column and, since 2026-09-07, the Default card's header strip beside the LIVE pill. It shipped on the Pro row first and the card, with far more room, had none of it: the card's scoreboard shows per-map cells and hides its Σ column until a second map exists, so a first-half football match showed a single `0 / 0` column and no clock (operator). **Pro rows carry the team crests** (`TeamMark` at 16px before each name, operator 2026-09-07): fon.bet's list has none, ours does. A side with no logo is its name alone — `TeamMark` renders nothing without a picture, no slot and no monogram — and the name sits in its own `.oz-pro-teamname` span so it is the only part of a side that truncates; the crest and the serve mark keep their size. |
| Pro-row line picker (fon.bet's ⇅) | `LinePicker` in [`match-table.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-table.tsx) + `GET /catalog/matches/:id/ladders` in [`catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) (2026-09-07, operator: "value for handicap and total should be selectable as at Fonbet, out of available selections"). The list carries ONE rung per kind — the balanced main line — and the picker lets a bettor choose another from the match's ladder. For a total the control IS the line chip (one line shared by two prices); for a handicap it is a narrow stepper between the two sides, where fon.bet draws it, since it is one line for both and belongs to neither cell. **On demand, not in the list payload**: a football fixture carries ~12 rungs × 2 outcomes and a 100-row page would ship 2 400 outcome objects for a control most rows never open, so opening it fetches the match's whole ladder — every full-match rung, priced for THIS viewer (adjustment or ZillaBoost, through the same `priceLadder` the list uses), in the row's own wire shape — sorted by numeric line, current rung marked by market id. Anonymous responses cache 3 s like the lists; signed-in ones are per viewer and do not. Fetched on every open rather than cached client-side: a ladder shown a minute stale on a live match would quote rungs the book has moved off. **The chosen rung lives in `MatchListTabs`, ABOVE the merge** (`lineOverrides` + `withChosenLines`), so it goes through `mergeLadder` exactly like the server's pick — ticks re-price it, its boost re-applies, a suspension nulls it. Session-only, in memory, as on fon.bet: a chosen line is a look at this match now, not a preference. Cost: an overridden row gets a fresh `match` object per tick and its memo does not short-circuit, bounded by the handful of rows a bettor has actually stepped. **The popover is portalled onto `<body>`**: the tournament group clips overflow for its rounded corners, so a panel inside the row would be cut at the group's edge; fixed under the trigger, closed by Escape, outside pointer-down (window listener, capture), scroll or resize — Bet Assist's discipline. Inside it a row SELECTS; betting stays on the main row's cells, so there is exactly one place a price is clicked. `collectLadderRungs` is the shared collector — the list's `loadLadderMarketsForMatches` picks on top of it, the route returns all of it — so the two can never disagree about which rungs exist. | [`packages/types/src/list-markets.ts`](./packages/types/src/list-markets.ts) (pure, unit-tested in [`list-markets.test.ts`](./packages/types/src/list-markets.test.ts)), read by the API loader, the storefront and the match page. Two questions, answered once. **(1) Which market is "the handicap"** is a TABLE, for the same reason `bet-assist.ts` is one: `provider_market_id` is the feed's market TYPE and no shape separates a match handicap from a match ROUND handicap or a first-half round handicap — Oddin quotes all three, all keyed by a lone `handicap` specifier. Read off the live catalogue on 2026-09-07: Oddin `2` (match handicap, stated in maps) and `3` (number of maps) — deliberately NOT `136` / `156`, whose round counts (61.5) would read as nonsense in a column captioned "Total" beside a map handicap — plus Fonbet `1000304` / `1000305`. Outcome ids are per feed AND per kind (`1`/`2` against `h1`/`h2`; over-first `5`/`4` against `over`/`under`), which is why the loader buckets rungs per provider before picking: a bucket holding both feeds would read one's rungs through the other's ids and quote an empty pair. Sub-events and per-map copies are excluded by requiring **exactly one specifier key, the line key** — stricter than a list of keys to exclude, and it stays right when a feed invents a new qualifier. **(2) Which rung is the main line** is a RULE: the BALANCED one, scored `|o2 − o1| / (o1 + o2)`. The normalisation is the load-bearing half and the first cut lacked it — a raw gap in odds is scale-dependent (1.90/1.95 and 5.00/5.05 tie) and a raw gap in PROBABILITY favours the longer pair outright (0.0020 against 0.0135), so a rung deep in the tail could win the column by being far out rather than by being level; the unit test that pinned the wrong expectation is what surfaced it. **It is a rule by DECISION, not for lack of a flag.** Fonbet marks its own main table (`isMain`) and fon.bet prints that rung — for football totals often the conventional `2.5` even when `2` is the level line; fonbet-ingester reads the flag and never persists it, and Oddin sends no equivalent. Persisting it was offered to the operator on 2026-09-07 as the lever for exact fon.bet parity and REFUSED: "our logic is better than Fonbet". So the balanced rung is the product's own line. Measured that day on production: Cagliari vs Lecce picked total `2` (1.65/2.17) where fon.bet would show `2.5`, and that difference is intended. Do not add an `is_main` column or an ingester change for this, and do not describe the rule as provisional. Verified against the live Getafe vs Celta ladders — handicap picks `0` (1.70/2.17) out of five rungs, total picks `1.5` (1.70/2.15) out of seven — and, after deploy, the same two rungs came back from the real database. A rung needs BOTH sides priced and bettable or it is skipped; no rung qualifying leaves an empty column, the same affordance a suspended price gets. `handicapLineForSide` (the sign — home as stated, away negated) lives here too and the match page's `handicapForSide` now delegates to it, because the sign is the part that silently prints a whole column's line wrongly when it drifts, as it did on every Fonbet handicap until 2026-09-07. |
| List handicap / total loader (API) | `loadLadderMarketsForMatches` + `serializeLadders` in [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts), returned as `ladders: {handicap?, total?}` on `/catalog/sports/:slug` and `/catalog/matches`. ONE query, and the only one this feature adds to a list response — `loadTopMarketsForMatches` beside it early-returns when no sport has a curated Top list, which is every sport on production today. **The loader PICKS and the serializer PRICES**, the same split `loadMatchWinnerOdds` has with its serializer, and for the same reason: ZillaBoost is computed from the RAW published odds and replaces the per-bettor adjustment outright, so prices have to leave the loader raw. The balance score is taken on raw odds too — the main line is a property of the book, not of one bettor's adjustment, so every bettor sees the same rung. **Every priced market on a card goes through ONE quoter**, `quoteCardMarketBoost` (the match-winner row's `quoteMatchWinnerBoost` is now a thin call into it): the match page boosts every market a rule covers, so a card that boosted only its match winner would have shown a different price for the same handicap the match page had already marked up — which is what the first cut did, on the reasoning that quoting raw was "safe" because placement prices a rule-less leg raw. Safe, and wrong: the same market must show the same price on every surface. The ladder market ids are fed to `loadBoostRulesForMatches` alongside the match-winner ids so a market-scope rule pinned to a handicap resolves, and the leg carries `customBoostRuleId` into the slip exactly as the match-winner cell does — without it POST /bets would price the leg from the raw book and silently accept it at the lower price, since a typical boost sits inside the 5% drift tolerance. The client re-applies the boost over WS ticks (`mergeLadder` in match-list-tabs.tsx, the match-winner merge on a two-outcome market, with the no-tick fallback reaching for the pre-boost original so the boost cannot compound) but never re-picks the rung: the line is fixed for the life of the SSR payload, because a row whose line jumped between rungs mid-read would be worse than a stale one and the other rungs are not even subscribed. A ladder leg carries the line in BOTH halves of its slip label (`Handicap -1` / `Getafe -1`), because none of the surfaces that read it back — slip, bet history, a copied community ticket — shows the column caption that would otherwise say which rung it was; same lesson as the sub-event prefix on `market.name`. |
| Bet-slip selection for an inline list price | [`apps/web/src/lib/match-winner-selection.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/match-winner-selection.ts) — built by BOTH list layouts, extracted for one field: `customBoostRuleId`. Without it `POST /bets` falls back to the raw published price, and since a typical ZillaBoost sits INSIDE the 5% drift tolerance the bet is silently ACCEPTED at the lower raw price rather than rejected — a layout that forgot it would quote one number and charge another with nothing failing anywhere. The input type is declared structurally rather than imported from `match-row.tsx`, which is `"use client"` and imports this module back. |
| Which market is "the match winner" | [`services/api/src/lib/match-winner-market.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/match-winner-market.ts) (pure, unit-tested in [`match-winner-market.test.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/match-winner-market.test.ts)), read by `loadMatchWinnerOdds` in [`catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts) and by the ZillaBoost match banner. **It is a rule, not a column, and the discriminator is the OUTCOME IDS rather than `provider_market_id`.** Oddin says market 1; Fonbet has no market 1 at all — its id is the catalogue TABLE (`1_000_000 + num`), and it reuses one table across every sub-event, so "Match result", "1st half: Match result" and "Corners: Match result" are all `1000120`. What separates the full match is that the ingester rewrites the outcome ids to the canonical `1` / `2` / `3` only on tables Fonbet flags `isMain` whose columns are captioned literally "1" / "2"; a sub-event copy keeps its raw factor ids (921/922/923, plus 924/925/1571 for the double chance). So the canonical ids do double duty — they pick the full match out of a table's sub-event copies AND the three-way result out of a row that also carries double chance — which is why the test is stated once here instead of per surface. Custom markets (2 000 000) are inside the range on purpose: a 2- or 3-way operator market is given exactly these ids so it prices on cards, and a wider one gets `o1..oN` precisely so it falls out. Oddin's map winner (4) is deliberately EXCLUDED — it wins a map, not the match, so a surface wanting it as a fallback has to say so. Extracted 2026-09-07 from the catalog route, which had it inline, when the banner needed the same answer; the third copy is what the hard-coded `1` in `quoteMatchWinnerBoost` already cost once. Measured on production the same day: of 3 107 open matches, **2 985 have exactly one** market satisfying it and 122 none (deep-live, or head-to-head-only fixtures whose ids are factor ids by design) — never two, so no tie-break is load-bearing. |
| Match-row component | [`apps/web/src/components/match/match-row.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-row.tsx). **The draw button is half height** (2026-09-06, operator call): on a 1X2 card the "X" row carries nothing but its odds button, so at the team rows' 30px it added a whole extra row of card height for the least-picked cell. `RowOddBtn`'s `compact` variant renders it at 16px (half, rounded up one so the 1px borders and the 9.5px label centre on whole pixels) with smaller type; the TAP target is grown back to 24px by a `::before` on `.oz-row-odd[data-compact]` in globals.css reaching 4px above and below, into the clearance the row leaves either side — WCAG 2.5.8 without the card paying for it. **The first cut of that shrank the button without shrinking the CARD as much as it looks like it should have** (fixed 2026-09-07, measured on production rather than reasoned about): the 16px button sat in a 20.3px grid row, because `RowOddBtn` is inline-flex and a plain block wrapper is at least one line box tall (the card's 14px/1.45), and the grid's 6px gaps then applied on top — 26.3px of card for a 16px control. `DrawScoreRow`'s trailing cell is `display: flex` (no strut) and carries `DRAW_ROW_PULL` (2px) negative margins that pull it into those gaps, leaving exactly 4px of clearance. 4 is the floor, not taste: it is what the tap pseudo-element needs to reach 24px without covering the "1" / "2" buttons, and a mis-tap there puts the wrong leg in the slip. Measured before and after on the same production fixture: 1X2 card **167px → 158.7px**, two-way card unchanged at 140.7, win buttons unchanged at 30px both times — the draw costs 18px now instead of 26.3. Below this, height can only come from something other than the draw. |
| Who is serving (tennis / table tennis / volleyball) | [`ServeMark`](./apps/web/src/components/match/serve-mark.tsx) + `servingSide()` in [`live-score.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/live-score.ts), rendered beside the name on the list card ([`match-row.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/match/match-row.tsx)) and the match-page scoreboard ([`live-scoreboard.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/match/[id]/live-scoreboard.tsx)). Fonbet has carried the signal all along and we were dropping it: `serve` (1 = team1, 2 = team2) sits on the innermost `liveEventInfos.scores` cell, and **which group that is differs per sport** — the current game in tennis, the current set in table tennis and volleyball — so `buildScore` takes the last cell that carries it rather than a fixed index, and ignores any value that is not a side. Verified against a live fon.bet snapshot (2026-09-06) where Fonbet's own score comment marks the server with an asterisk (`7*-10`), which is what pins 1 to team1. Rides `matches.live_score` (no schema change — it is jsonb, and the api passes the column through), so a serve change is an ordinary live_score diff and publishes on the channel the score already uses. Oddin's esports payload has no equivalent and the field is simply absent there. **The marker is a tennis ball in the ball's own colour** (`--serve-ball` / `--serve-ball-ring`), not a coloured dot. It shipped for a day as a plain `var(--accent)` dot, which resolves to near-black in the light theme and read as a stray bullet point beside the player's name rather than as a serve — operator's call, 2026-09-07. Optic yellow needs no legend because it is the actual colour of the object, but it is a light colour on a light page, so the ring is what makes it legible and doubles as the ball's outline; the single curved seam is drawn only from `size >= 8`, below which it is a smudge. On dark the ball separates itself, so the ring stops doing contrast work and instead reads as the seam drawn ON the ball. Never `--live`: a red dot is this design's live signal and a second one on the same row would read as a second state. Table tennis and volleyball share the mark even though their balls are not this colour — the shape is the message, and one recognisable ball beats three near-identical pale circles. Gated on the match being live — the feed keeps the last value after a match ends. |
| Cookie notice banner + privacy policy | Store [`apps/web/src/lib/cookie-consent.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/cookie-consent.ts) (localStorage `oz:cookie-consent` v2 — records only that the notice was dismissed; nothing is gated on it). Banner [`apps/web/src/components/shell/cookie-banner.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/cookie-banner.tsx) — **purely informational** (operator decision 2026-07-02 for the UZ market): cookies, third-party embeds, and fe-analytics are ON BY DEFAULT; the banner's two "Accept all" buttons acknowledge and dismiss. Banner body + `/privacy` describe the on-by-default behaviour — keep them in sync with reality if the regime changes. **Guardrail: if a Reject / "Necessary only" control is ever reintroduced, it must genuinely disable the categories — a reject-labelled control that accepts is deceptive design; do not ship one.** Policy page [`apps/web/src/app/(main)/privacy/page.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/privacy/page.tsx) (Privacy + Cookies Policy, English-only, points to browser-level cookie controls; operator-entity placeholders still to fill). Links: shell footer [`shell-footer.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/shell-footer.tsx), sidebar bottom notice, auth layout. i18n namespace `cookieConsent` in all 6 locales. |
| Server-side fetch (cookie-forwarded) | [`apps/web/src/lib/server-fetch.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/server-fetch.ts), [`lib/auth.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/auth.ts) |

The root layout pins `metadataBase` to `https://oddzilla.cc` so the emitted `og:image` / `twitter:image` URLs come out absolute; without it Next resolves them against `http://localhost:3000`, which is what production shipped until 2026-09-04 and why every link preview was broken), [`apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/res/drawable-xxxhdpi/{ic_launcher_foreground,ic_splash_logo}.png`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/res/drawable-xxxhdpi/) (adaptive-icon foreground + splash bitmap; the `mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` and the splash theme entry in `values/themes.xml` resolve to these by name). Wordmark aspect (`WORDMARK_ASPECT` in `monogram.tsx`) must match the master's `width/height`; mobile top-bar width overrides in `globals.css` are derived from it. |

Inbound IDs are clamped to `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$` at every layer so a hostile client can't poison log lines. |

Operator visibility for whatever the suspend-before-recover flush didn't catch), `unsettled` (see the Unsettled monitor row below), `fe-settings` (storefront-display knobs; first sub-section: `markets-order` per-sport ordering of market types), `competitors` (team logos + brand colours; sport-filterable list with inline edit, missing-logo toggle, and a bulk-seed endpoint). Not yet reskinned; uses legacy `--color-*` tokens mapped in `@theme`. |

[`web/WebViewHost.kt`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/web/WebViewHost.kt) hosts a single `WebView` pointing at `https://oddzilla.cc`; the web app is already responsive (`MobileBetSlipBar`, `MobileDrawersProvider`, `MobileShellOverlay`, mobile-first breakpoints in `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`) so wrapping it gives pixel parity with mobile Chrome. Native chrome that stays native: system splash, `UpdateGate` modal (overlays the WebView so a mandatory update can't be tapped past), and the FCM scaffolding in `fcm/`. WebView config: `domStorageEnabled`, third-party cookies on (Disir / Twitch / YouTube embeds), strict mixed-content, UA-stamped with `Oddzilla-Android/<ver>` for log attribution, in-app for `oddzilla.cc` + `*.oddin.gg` + Twitch / YouTube / Kick / Gjirafa hosts and `Intent.ACTION_VIEW` for everything else. Cookies mirror from the WebView's `CookieManager` into [`PersistentCookieJar`](./apps/mobile-android/app/src/main/java/cc/oddzilla/app/data/api/CookieStore.kt) on every page-finished so the OkHttp client used by `DevicesRepository` (FCM device-register) authenticates against the same session. REST surface trimmed to the three `/devices/*` endpoints — auth, catalog, wallet, bets, community, cashout flows now happen entirely inside the WebView. Self-update flow lives in `update/`: `UpdateController` polls `https://oddzilla.cc/app/version.json` on cold start, downloads the APK via OkHttp into `externalCacheDir/updates/`, verifies SHA-256 against the manifest, and hands off to `Intent.ACTION_VIEW + application/vnd.android.package-archive` via `FileProvider`. Version source-of-truth is `version.properties` at the project root; `scripts/release.ps1` (and the bash twin) builds a signed APK, hashes it, scps both the APK + new `version.json` to `team@178.104.174.24:/srv/oddzilla-apk/`. |
