## Deep references

- [`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`](./docs/ARCHITECTURE.md) — service interaction, end-to-end flow walkthroughs (odds, bet placement, settlement, deposit, withdrawal), scale path, security boundaries
- [`docs/SCHEMA.md`](./docs/SCHEMA.md) — every table with columns, indexes, rationale, common queries
- [`docs/ODDIN.md`](./docs/ODDIN.md) — AMQP routing keys, XML shapes, REST endpoints, market IDs, recovery protocol
- [`docs/PHASES.md`](./docs/PHASES.md) — phase-by-phase roadmap with delivered detail per phase
- [`docs/OPERATIONS.md`](./docs/OPERATIONS.md) — deploy, env vars, backup, withdrawal admin runbook, incident playbook
- [`docs/SETTLEMENT_COVERAGE_PLAN.md`](./docs/SETTLEMENT_COVERAGE_PLAN.md) — why markets stay open on finished matches (measured 2026-09-05), the operator's decisions (no auto-void of played markets, denylist unsettleable shapes, English-only grader), and the phased plan with what shipped

## Conventions

- **One domain per file** in `packages/db/src/schema/`. No circular imports
  across packages (enforced by tsconfig project references).
- **Each Go service is its own module** (`services/<name>/go.mod`). Shared Go
  code goes in `services/<name>/internal/`. Do not create a shared Go module
  — duplicate small helpers to keep services independently deployable.
  Examples already duplicated: `oddinxml` (feed-ingester ↔ settlement),
  `amqp` consumer pattern (feed-ingester ↔ settlement).
- **Logs are JSON, structured.** TS uses `pino`, Go uses `zerolog`. Always
  include `service` and `event`/`component` fields.
- **Errors return early, never swallowed.** Go: wrap with
  `fmt.Errorf("context: %w", err)`. TS: throw typed errors from
  `services/api/src/lib/errors.ts`; Fastify maps them to HTTP status codes
  via the `setErrorHandler` in `server.ts`.
- **Tests live next to code.** `*.test.ts` and `*_test.go`. Integration
  tests hit the real Postgres container — never mock the database.
- **Commits:** imperative mood, lowercase, prefix by area
  (`db:`, `api:`, `feed:`, `web:`, `docs:`, `infra:`). No emojis.
- **Graceful idle.** Every service that needs external creds (Oddin AMQP,
  chain RPC) must boot cleanly when those creds are absent — log a warning
  and serve health only. Pattern is well-established in feed-ingester,
  settlement, wallet-watcher.
- **Docs stay in sync on every commit and deploy.** Before ANY commit
  lands (not just PR merges — direct commits too), check whether
  CLAUDE.md, the relevant `docs/*.md`, the root `README.md`, and any
  affected `services/*/README.md` still describe reality, and update
  them in the *same* commit or PR — never "in a follow-up". And before
  running `make deploy`, confirm the docs for everything being shipped
  are already up to date — a deploy of code whose docs are stale is an
  incomplete deploy. Stale docs are worse than missing ones: they
  mislead the next agent. Trigger map:
  - New service, data flow, or invariant → CLAUDE.md (`## Architecture
    map`, `## Invariants`, `## Where things live`) + `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
  - Schema migration → CLAUDE.md migration list under `## Where things
    live` + `docs/SCHEMA.md`.
  - Env var added / renamed / removed → `.env.example` + CLAUDE.md
    `## Local secrets that exist`.
  - Phase progress, new component shipped, acceptance bar reached →
    `docs/PHASES.md` + the CLAUDE.md `## Live phase status` table.
  - Deploy / backup / runbook / incident step changed →
    `docs/OPERATIONS.md`.
  - Oddin protocol detail changed → `docs/ODDIN.md`.
  - Service-internal change (new module, renamed binary, added flag) →
    that service's `services/<name>/README.md`.
  - File or symbol renamed / removed → grep `CLAUDE.md README.md docs/`
    and the service READMEs for the old name and either update or
    delete the references.

  If a doc claim is no longer true, fix it in the same merge. When
  reviewing, treat "diff touches code but no doc" as a smell — ask
  whether one of the triggers above applies before approving. The same
  smell applies at deploy time: if `make deploy-status` shows commits
  whose docs were never checked, do the doc pass before deploying.

## Hard limits

- **Don't expose Postgres or Redis publicly.** Compose binds them to
  `127.0.0.1` only; Caddy never fronts them.
- **Don't bypass bet-delay** when a user has `bet_delay_seconds > 0`. The
  admin toggle is the only legitimate way to skip it.
- **Don't return secrets in API responses** — including internal user fields
  like `password_hash`, `refresh_token_hash`, KYC raw documents, and
  `derivation_path` from `deposit_addresses`.
- **Don't run SQL migrations manually on prod.** Use `make migrate`; it's
  transactional and records history in `_migrations`.
- **Don't add a service without adding a healthcheck to compose.** A service
  with no `/healthz` will fail silently.
- **The signer container is the only place `HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC` lives.**
  PR #132 moved derivation out of the API into [`services/signer/`](./services/signer/).
  The API talks to it over a Unix socket on a tmpfs volume; no
  other container mounts it. Putting `HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC` back into
  the API's env (or compose service entry) defeats the entire isolation
  layer — if you find yourself needing to, talk to the signer instead.
  The signer also calls `os.Unsetenv("HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC")` at boot so
  `/proc/<pid>/environ` is uninteresting after startup.
- **The web tier carries no server secrets.** The docker-compose `*web-base`
  anchor intentionally has NO `env_file: .env`; each web replica lists only
  the non-secret runtime vars it reads (grep `apps/web/src` for
  `process.env`). JWTs are HS256 (symmetric), so a web RCE able to read
  `JWT_SECRET` from `process.env` could FORGE admin tokens — the escalation
  `apps/web/src/lib/auth.ts` documents as foreclosed. Never add `env_file:
  .env` (or any secret) to a web service. Verify with `docker compose exec
  web1 env | grep -E 'JWT_SECRET|POSTGRES_PASSWORD|ODDIN_TOKEN'` → nothing.
- **Linking a deposit sending-address requires proof of control.**
  `POST /wallet/addresses` verifies an EIP-191 signature (challenge from
  `GET /wallet/addresses/challenge`, binding userId+address+issuedAt) that
  recovers the claimed address, plus a CEX hot-wallet blocklist. Without it
  a bettor could register an address they don't own — e.g. an exchange hot
  wallet every withdrawal originates from — and the wallet-watcher would
  auto-credit those deposits to them. Don't drop the signature check.
- **Don't disable the audit-log trigger.** `admin_audit_log_chain_trg`
  computes `prev_hash` + `row_hash` on every INSERT under an advisory
  lock. Bypassing it (e.g. via `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE TRIGGER`) breaks
  the chain at every later row, and the verifier (`admin_audit_chain_check`)
  will flag every subsequent insert as tampered.
- **Don't bypass the CSRF plugin.** Every state-changing API request
  (POST / PUT / PATCH / DELETE) must carry an `Origin` or `Referer` in
  `CORS_ORIGINS`. Server-to-server callers (the Next.js middleware's
  `/auth/refresh` fetch, the web container's `/auth/me` lookup) set
  `origin` explicitly. Browsers always set Origin on POST. If you find
  yourself adding a route that needs to skip the gate, surface the use
  case first — almost certainly you want a webhook signature instead.

## Live phase status

What's actually wired up vs scaffolded vs deferred. Last updated after the
post-Phase-8 Oddin-workflow hardening pass; production stack is live at
`team@178.104.174.24` consuming the integration AMQPS feed.

| Component | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fonbet sports feed (`fonbet-ingester`) | Local stack verified (2026-09-03); review fixes 2026-09-04; moved to fon.bet in English 2026-09-04. **Feed off by default (`FONBET_ENABLED`), and settlement off by default INDEPENDENTLY (`FONBET_SETTLE_ENABLED=false`) — run the feed alone first, soak the grader against Fonbet's own results on staging, then enable settlement** | Polls `events/list` (`fon.bet`, `lang=en`, scopeMarket 1600) every 5 s, diffs against the previous snapshot and writes only deltas into `markets` / `market_outcomes` / `odds_history` + `odds.raw`; sub-events (halves, corners, player props) ride the `variant` specifier, esports maps `map=N`; market / outcome labels come from Fonbet's `factorsCatalog/tables` in the feed language plus every other locale in `descriptionLangs` (en + ru), so the storefront's `/ru` keeps Russian market names off an English feed. Staleness watchdog + SIGTERM suspend the whole Fonbet catalog (`fb:` URNs only). Settlement: the ingester grades closed matches from Fonbet's results feed (1X2 / double chance / handicap / total, halves, periods, statistic rows; conservative sport rules) and hands them to `services/settlement` over the `settlement.external` Redis stream (`internal/extstream` → `Settler.ApplyExternalSettlement`), so tickets on Fonbet markets settle through the same apply-once path as Oddin's. Markets the rules skip (overtime tables, exotic shapes, unsupported sports) stay open for manual settlement. odds-publisher was batched in the same change (~5k ticks/s). **Review fixes (2026-09-04):** `settlement.external` consumer recreates its group on `NOGROUP` and creates it from `0` (invariant 7); `odds.raw` stays at the shared 100k MAXLEN and the ingester paces its cold-start republish on odds-publisher's group lag instead (`OddsBacklog`, high-water 50k); `settlement.external` capped at 20k; `FONBET_SETTLE_ENABLED` defaults to false; the results-feed fallback match requires home-before-away (an order-blind substring match bound mirrored fixtures and inverted every grade); overtime is folded into a two-way score exactly once (`scoreFor` reports `otApplied`, `breakTie` honours it); `emitted` is stamped only after the settle message is on the stream; urls.json hosts are adopted only when `https` and under a domain from the operator's static lists; `EnsureSport` retries on a `sports_slug_key` collision with an `-fb-<id>` suffix; `FONBET_MAX_MATCHES` no longer neuters the shrink guard (`Snapshot.TotalMatches`) nor closes capped matches (`Snapshot.Capped`); compose gives the service `mem_limit: 768m` / `cpus: 1.0` / `GOMEMLIMIT=640MiB` (it holds the whole line plus a fresh snapshot in RAM — the anchor's 320m was never exercised under a cgroup); `ODDS_HISTORY_SKIP_PMID_MIN` is the operator brake on `odds_history` growth. **fon.bet in English (2026-09-04):** the feed read the Kazakhstan site in Russian, which is why traditional-sport market names arrived in Russian on the storefront. Now `fon.bet` + `FONBET_LANG=en` by default, with `FONBET_SITE_ORIGIN` / `FONBET_LOGO_CDN` added so the request `Origin` and the asset CDN follow the site instead of being hard-coded. Identity-neutral (same event / market / outcome ids — see the Where-things-live row); the substantive part was teaching the settlement grader English, since it reads Fonbet's own text. **Runtime switch (migration 0099, 2026-09-04):** the **Fonbet feed** card on `/admin/feed` turns the feed on/off without a restart — `feed_control.fonbet_enabled` (NULL = follow `FONBET_ENABLED`), read every 2 s by the ingester's switch watcher in [`main.go`](./services/fonbet-ingester/cmd/fonbet-ingester/main.go) (`switchWatcher` / `runFeed`); OFF = `SuspendAll` + stop polling + settlement worker paused, ON = boot the feed in place; a boot failure while ON retries every 30 s instead of `log.Fatal` (which would crashloop under the switch). The card reads `GET /admin/feed/fonbet-status` (switch + `fonbet_applied_*` ack from Postgres, live counters from the Redis hash `fonbet:feed:status` the service refreshes every 5 s). |
| DB schema + migrations | Live | 30 migrations 0000–0029. See "SQL migrations" row above for the full list. Recent additions: `0014_multi_currency`, `0015_cashout`, `0017_tiple_tippot`, `0018_bet_product_per_leg_margin`, `0019_fe_market_display_order`, `0020_fe_market_order_scope`, `0021_competitor_logos`, `0022_match_tv_channels`, `0023_settlements_market_id_idx`, `0024_community_profiles` (Phase 10.1), `0025_community_tickets` (Phase 10.2), `0026_audit_hardening` (4-eyes withdrawals + audit-log SHA-256 hash chain), `0027_deposit_reorg_safety` (`deposits.block_hash`), `0028_session_family` (refresh-token family + replay detection), `0029_community_achievements` (Phase 10.4 — `achievement_definitions` + `user_achievements`; 5 starter badges seeded). |
| Auth (signup/login/refresh/me + password change) | Live | argon2id (m=46 MiB / t=1) + JOSE JWT (`alg: HS256` pinned, `aud=oddzilla-api`) + refresh-rotation with **family-based replay detection** (presenting a refresh token whose session is already revoked → revoke the whole family); helmet CSP `default-src 'none'` on `/api/*`; rate-limited login (5/min, with timing-equalised dummy hash on unknown email) + signup (10/min) + password change (5/5min); **host-scoped auth cookies** so the apex storefront and the admin subdomain hold independent sessions (was `Domain=.oddzilla.cc` pre-2026-05-18; the legacy shared-domain cookie is killed alongside every new set in [`services/api/src/lib/cookies.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/cookies.ts) so existing sessions migrate cleanly); **Origin/Referer-based CSRF** on every state-changing method via [`plugins/csrf.ts`](./services/api/src/plugins/csrf.ts); **per-request session-revocation check** with Redis-cached `session:status:{sid}` (60s TTL) so logout / password change / refresh-replay invalidate the access JWT immediately instead of waiting up to 15 min. Web container no longer carries `JWT_SECRET` — `apps/web/src/lib/auth.ts` calls `/auth/me` for every check; enforced in `docker-compose.yml` by dropping `env_file: .env` from the `*web-base` anchor and listing only non-secret runtime vars per web replica (2026-05-29 audit). Browser uses same-origin `/api/*` through Caddy — `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` is baked **empty** so api-client falls back to `/api`. Signup also creates a USDT wallet (zero) + OZ wallet (1000 OZ demo bonus) atomically, with an idempotent `(adjustment, signup_bonus, user_id)` ledger row. |
| Catalog API + sport/match SSR pages | Live | `/catalog/sports` filters `active=true` (hides `unclassified`); `/catalog/sports/:slug` returns inline match-winner odds per row (paired by Oddin outcome_id: `"1"`=home, `"2"`=away) and accepts `?tournament=N` and/or `?team=N` (competitor id; scoped to this sport, returns the team name as `filteredTeam` so the storefront chip can render even when the team has zero current matches) to filter the list; `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments` returns tournaments under a sport with live+upcoming match counts; `/catalog/matches/:id` returns all active (`status=1`) markets — provider-id whitelist removed. Odds are quoted on the 0.01 ladder (coarsening in the tail) with full 4dp precision kept below 1.01 — see the "Odds display formatting + the quote ladder" row above for the ladder, the six places that apply it, and why the two halves of the range are treated differently. **The storefront must render through [`formatOddsDisplay`](./packages/types/src/odds.ts)**: the API payload has carried 4dp since 2026-05-25, but every price *display* still did `toFixed(2)` until 2026-08-27, so a genuine 1.003 reached the user as "1.00" — a price that doesn't exist, and indistinguishable from a real 1.0000. Keep every copy of the ladder-and-trim algorithm identical or the displayed price and the drift reference diverge and bets get rejected for phantom drift. List/count endpoints (`/catalog/matches`, `/catalog/sports/:slug`, `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments`, `/catalog/search`, `/catalog/live-counts`) gate on the shared `hasActiveMarket` predicate — at least one `markets.status=1` row AND `matches.status IN ('not_started','live')` — so matches with nothing to bet on (or that already went terminal) are dropped. The match-winner can be briefly suspended (mid-round, post-goal) while secondary markets stay active, and the row still surfaces. Defense in depth on the storefront side complements the upstream lifecycle fixes (terminal-status apply on `odds_change`, `bet_stop` blanket suspension, `bet_settlement certainty=2`); phantom-drain remains as the safety net for matches Oddin never sends a terminal signal for. Inline odds are additionally restricted to `variant='way:two'` (`isTwoWayMatchWinner`) so 1X2 sports like eFootball don't render misleading 1/2 buttons; the match still appears in the list and clicking through shows the full 1X2 on `/match/:id`. |
| Top-bar global search | Live | `/catalog/search?q=…` (case-insensitive, 6 hits per facet across active sports / tournaments / teams / matches; matches gated on at least one active market). Frontend popover in `top-bar-search.tsx` debounces 180 ms, supports arrow-key nav, Cmd/Ctrl+K focus, Esc/click-outside close. Picking a team result navigates to `/sport/:slug?team=ID` so the sport page renders only that team's matches with a dismissible chip. |
| Sidebar tournament sub-tree | Live | When the user is on `/sport/:slug`, the sidebar auto-fetches `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments` and renders an indented list under the sport, **grouped by category** (2026-09-04). Each tournament links to `/sport/:slug?tournament=ID`; the sport page reads the param server-side and shows a dismissible filter chip. **Rows keep a shared left edge without inventing a mark** (2026-09-05): Fonbet has a competition logo for only about half its leagues, so a bucket is normally a mix of logo'd and bare rows, and `TournamentLogoMark` used to return `null` — which pulled the bare names ~20px left of their neighbours and made the list read as ragged. `CategoryGroup` now computes `reserveLogoSlot` from the data (any row in the group has a `logoUrl`) and the mark holds an empty 14px square for the rest; a group with no logos at all reserves nothing, keeping the full 240px for names. Derived from the data rather than each `<img>`'s error state, so a logo that 404s doesn't reflow its neighbours. The alternative — importing Fonbet's own country-flag fallback — was rejected because the category header directly above already shows that flag; same reasoning that removed `TeamMark`'s monogram. The endpoint already joined `categories` as a sport filter and now selects it too, returning `category: {id, name, slug}` per tournament — or **`null` when `categories.is_dummy`**, which is what makes the grouping safe to turn on globally: Oddin's auto-mapper files every esports tournament under one synthetic `Auto-mapped` category (153 of them on CS2 alone), so a header there would be pure noise, and the client renders null-category tournaments flat in an unlabelled bucket first — identical to the pre-grouping look. Fonbet's categories are real (country / competition, from the segment-name prefix: `"Spain. Primera Division. Season 26/27"` → `Spain`), which is what the grouping is for — before it, football showed 50-odd dotted tournament names in one flat column with no country structure. `groupTournamentsByCategory` in [`sidebar.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/sidebar.tsx) keeps the endpoint's tournament order inside each bucket. **Navigation rework (2026-09-04)** after the first Fonbet-era look at a real basketball tree (18 tournaments across 12 countries, all flat, all expanded): (1) every sport row carries a **disclosure caret** that opens its tournament tree in place — before this the tree existed only while you were ON `/sport/:slug`, so comparing two sports' offers meant navigating twice; expansion state is per-slug in the sidebar, the active sport still auto-expands, and the fetch effect now pulls the list for every open slug (guarded by a `requestedSlugs` ref, not state, so StrictMode's double-invoke can't double-fetch). (2) Labelled category buckets are **collapsed by default** and sort by **name**, not live count — with the header now a click target, a target that reorders itself as matches go live is worse than one occasionally below the fold. **(2b) A category header shows a live count or NOTHING (2026-09-07, operator call):** when nothing was live the same slot printed the bucket's TOURNAMENT count in grey — a fact about how the offer is FILED rather than about the offer, at the same size and in the same place as the live badge, so the tree read as a column of numbers with one of them occasionally red. Now the red badge is the only thing that ever occupies it, which is what makes it read as an exception. The API still returns the counts (`/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments`) and `TournamentGroup.liveCount` is unchanged; only the fallback render went. Same call as the tournament row below it, which has never printed its `matchCount` for the same reason. **Operator ordering landed 2026-09-05 (migration 0103):** a category pinned on `/admin/categories` carries a `display_order` and heads its sport's tree in that sequence, so Football opens on England rather than Albania; everything unpinned stays alphabetical behind it, which is byte-identical to the previous behaviour for a sport nobody has touched. `groupTournamentsByCategory` sorts pin-then-name, with `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` standing in for an unpinned bucket the same way the tournament sort already does for a null `risk_tier`. The bucket holding the active `?tournament=` filter auto-opens (via an effect that only ever forces `open` true, so the caret can still close it). **The header is two controls, not one (2026-09-04, migration 0102):** the caret collapses, and the LABEL links to `/sport/:slug?category=<id>`, filtering the sport page to the whole bucket. They were a single toggle button until list-excluded categories landed — a bucket the match lists deliberately skip has to be reachable, and "expand it, then pick one of seventeen tournaments" is not reachable in any useful sense. Splitting them pays off on the ordinary country buckets too: one click on `England` is now every English league at once. The hover surface still spans the whole row so the two halves read as one header, the bucket auto-opens when it IS the active category, and a bucket flagged `hidden_from_lists` wears a quiet `EyeOff` mark — without it a bettor has nothing to explain why Football's badge reads 13 while this one bucket reads 10. The unlabelled bucket has no header and stays open, so esports look exactly as they did. **Both carets sit on the RIGHT, and the indent steps are small (2026-09-06).** The category caret used to lead its row while the sport caret trailed its own, so the tree's two expanders were mirror images of each other for the same gesture; it now renders last in the header row, built as the same 24px box with the same 10px glyph, and the row reserves the same 4px to its right — both glyphs measure 31px from the rail's edge, one column. Moving it also returned ~26px to the category label, which had been indenting FURTHER than its own tournaments. The indent steps came down with it (tree container 14+8 → 6+7, category list 10 → 8, tournament row's left padding 6 → 3): measured on the deployed 240px rail, a tournament name had **129px** to live in and 5 of England's 13 truncated, against **143px** and 4 after. The rule that keeps this from going further: the tree's border lands at x=20, just left of the sport row's own icon at x=24, so it still reads as descending from the sport — which is all the indent was ever for. The two carets' coarse-pointer sizes are deliberately NOT copies of each other, because they are built differently — the sport one is absolutely positioned, so its box overlays reserved padding and its HEIGHT is capped by the row pitch or adjacent carets overlap; the category one is a flex item, so its WIDTH comes out of the label (hence 40px, not 48) while its height merely grows the row from 29 to 32, which is a touch improvement rather than a hazard. (3) Category headers get a **country flag** — see the flag-proxy row below — plus normal 12px `--fg` text instead of 9.5px letterspaced uppercase `--fg-dim`, which was failing on contrast. A category that is NOT a country (cycling races, `NBA 2K26`, `Friendly games`) falls back to the bucket's own tournament logo, and to **no mark at all** when there isn't one. The first cut put a two-letter initials disc there so every row kept the same left edge; on the cycling tree that stamped `TO` / `VE` / `ZT` beside Tour of Britain, Vuelta Espana and ZLM Tour — invented monograms abbreviating words already on the row — so it was dropped (2026-09-04). The bucket logo is taken only when every tournament in it that HAS a logo has the SAME one (`TournamentGroup.logoUrl`), which is true of a race and its stages and false of a country holding several leagues; the caret still holds the left edge. (4) Indentation was **reduced**, not added: the tree steps in 14+8px (was 22+10) and tournaments inside a category 10px (was 8 on top of the deeper base), because on a 240px panel every pixel of indent is a pixel off the tournament name. (4b) The **Sports** nav item was removed 2026-09-05: it existed to give the Fonbet traditional line one entry point back when the rail was a single esports-first list, and the Sports / Esports switch a few pixels below is both a better entry point and the one bettors reach for — two controls competing for one job. The `/sports` route still exists; its `shell.sportsNav` message key is now unused in all six locales. (5) The list splits into **Sports** and **Esports** (Sports on the left since 2026-09-05 — render order only; `esport` is still the tab a fresh visitor lands on and the stored preference key is unchanged, so nobody's saved side flipped), switched by a side-by-side segmented control keyed off `sports.kind` rather than stacked sections — stacked, the Fonbet traditional line always sat below ~16 esports, so reaching Football was a scroll on every visit. The tab persists in `localStorage["oz:sport-tab"]` (read in an effect, never the initializer, or SSR and hydration disagree) and follows the page: landing on `/sport/football` while the rail shows Esports would hide the very row the user is looking at. The control's token pair is `--bg-sunken` track / `--surface-2` active pill, the only pair that stays *raised* in both themes — the obvious `--surface-2` / `--bg-elevated` inverts on dark (`#1a1a1c` track, `#131314` pill) and reads as a recess. With only one kind on offer (a feed outage, Fonbet switched off) it degrades to a plain label rather than a toggle with a dead half. Edit mode follows the SAME tab, and keeps the tab strip live so the other half is one click away: it used to render one flat list of every sport, so on a 16-esport estate reordering Football meant scrolling past every esport, and the arrows on that row moved it through sports that are never on screen together. The saved preference is still one global slug list — `move` swaps the two rows at their positions in the full visible order, so the other kind keeps its slots and "up" means "above the next sport you can actually see". The edit-mode universe is visible + hidden sports, so a bettor who hid every traditional sport can still reach that tab to un-hide one. **Expanding a big sport felt slow, and it was three separate things (2026-09-06).** (a) The flags never cached — see the flag-proxy row; that one was the bulk of it and cost ~500 KB on every Football expand. (b) The endpoint's plain 10 s TTL put its own cold load in front of a real bettor nearly every time: measured on production, football (291 tournaments over ~1 900 matches) is ~1.3 s cold against ~85 ms warm, and at this traffic level a 10 s window has almost always expired by the time somebody clicks, so the person expanding the tree was the person paying for the refresh. It runs on `cachedSwr` now (15 s fresh / 10 min stale) — a stale payload returns immediately and the refresh happens behind the response. Live counts are no less current in practice; the sport row's own badge comes from `/catalog/live-counts` at 5 s, so this list is a navigation aid, not a scoreboard. (c) The fetch only started on the click. `ensureTournaments` is now also called from `prefetchHandlers` on each row — mouse hover after a **140 ms dwell** (without the dwell, sweeping the pointer down the rail would request every sport it passed, several of them the expensive ones) and immediately on pointer-down, which is ~100-200 ms of real headroom on touch. Deliberately NOT on focus: React's `onFocus` bubbles, so tabbing the rail would request all of it. Idle-prefetching every sport was rejected for the same reason the dwell exists — most of that work is for a sport nobody opens. `CategoryGroup` also builds its tournament list only when expanded; it used to construct all ~291 rows on every sidebar render and mount a handful. **Touch targets (2026-09-06):** both carets are chevrons sitting next to a link, and on a phone the 24px sport caret was missed often enough that hitting the sport instead was the normal outcome — and the miss is expensive, because the link navigates AND the drawer auto-closes on navigation ([`mobile-drawer-context.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/mobile-drawer-context.tsx)), so a bettor peeking at Football's tree loses the sidebar and reopens it. `.oz-sport-caret` / `.oz-cat-caret` / `.oz-sport-row` in globals.css grow the hit areas under `(pointer: coarse)` — the input device, not the viewport, is what matters, so a touch laptop gets it too. The sport caret goes to **48x36, not 44x44**, and the WIDTH is the half that matters — the control is pinned to the right edge, so the finger lands left of it. Height is capped by the row pitch, **measured at 39px on a 375px viewport, not estimated**: the caret is absolutely positioned and vertically centred, so anything taller spills into the neighbouring sport's caret and two adjacent expanders overlap, converting a 4px band that previously hit nothing into a band that can hit the wrong sport. The first cut shipped at 40px and did exactly that by 1px, caught by measuring `getBoundingClientRect` in a real mobile browser after deploy rather than trusting the estimate. Honouring Apple's 44px would mean growing every row (~6px x 30 sports of extra scrolling) to fix a miss that is horizontal; WCAG 2.5.8's 24x24 is cleared either way. The glyph does not move; it just grows 10px → 12px so the larger target reads as one. **The first nav item is "Home", not "Lobby" (2026-09-06):** operator call — "lobby" is casino vocabulary for what is plainly the home page, and the item has always pointed at `/`. The message key was renamed with it (`shell.lobby` → `shell.home`, all six locales; `home.lobby` was a dead duplicate and went with it), so a stale `tShell("lobby")` fails at typecheck rather than rendering the old word. The `lobby` message NAMESPACE is untouched — it holds the TodayLabel + ComboZilla strings and is an internal name nobody reads. |
| Category visibility (tree-only categories) | Column `categories.hidden_from_lists` (migration 0102) + the `notHiddenCategory` predicate in [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts). **The distinction from `HIDDEN_TOURNAMENT_NAMES` is the point**: that constant hides rows that should never be reachable (Oddin's integration-test tournament), this flag hides rows that shouldn't be the DEFAULT. Applied unconditionally on `/catalog/matches` (lobby / `/live` / `/upcoming`, which take no category filter) and on `/catalog/live-counts` (a "Football 23" badge over a list of 13 is worse than no badge); applied on `/catalog/sports/:slug` only when the request carries no `?category=`, `?tournament=` or `?team=` — an explicit narrowing means the bettor asked, and leaving the predicate on would render a filter chip for a view that then returns nothing. NOT applied to `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments` (the tree is the way in, so it must still list them, and its per-category / per-tournament counts still count hidden rows), NOT to `/catalog/sports` (a sport whose every category is hidden must keep its sidebar row or the tree becomes unreachable), and NOT to `/catalog/search` (typing a team name is explicit intent). Storefront: the sidebar's category header split into a caret that collapses and a **link that filters** (`/sport/:slug?category=<id>`) in [`sidebar.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/sidebar.tsx) — they were one button, and "expand the bucket, then pick one of seventeen tournaments" is not a reachable offer; a hidden bucket wears a quiet `EyeOff` mark so the bettor can tell why Football reads 13 while the bucket reads 10. The sport page ([`page.tsx`](./apps/web/src/app/(main)/sport/[slug]/page.tsx)) renders the category chip and returns `filteredCategory` from the API for its name. **All three sport-page filter chips are resolved server-side and none of them names its own KIND** (2026-09-06): `/catalog/sports/:slug` returns `filteredCategory` / `filteredTeam` / `filteredTournament`, each scoped to the sport so a foreign id yields null and the chip hides rather than silently widening the view. The tournament one used to be read off a match row, which tied the chip to the list having rows — a tournament between rounds rendered a chip with no name — and it carries `logoUrl` because the chip shows the tournament's MARK where the literal word "TOURNAMENT" used to sit. That label told the reader nothing the value did not already say, and on a chip reading "England. Premier League. Season 26/27" it was pure noise; the category and team chips lost theirs in the same pass, and the six `sport.filterKind*` message keys went with them. The mark renders through [`LogoMark`](./apps/web/src/components/ui/logo-mark.tsx), a client component precisely so it can draw NOTHING when the `<img>` errors — a server-rendered `<img>` cannot hide itself, tournament logo URLs come from three sources (our byte-serve, Fonbet's CDN, Wikimedia) so URL rot is normal, and a broken-image icon is worse than an empty slot. Deliberately no monogram stand-in, the same call `TeamMark` and the sidebar's `TournamentLogoMark` make. Admin: [`services/api/src/modules/admin/categories.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/categories.ts) (list / sport options / audit-logged PATCH; the list carries a bookable-match count per row because that number IS the argument for flagging one, and dummy categories are excluded so nobody blanks an entire esport by accident) + [`/admin/categories`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/categories/) under **Catalog** in the admin sidebar. The same page carries the pin ordering — see the row below. |
| Operator pin ordering (sports rail, category buckets, tournaments) | Column `display_order` on BOTH `sports` and `categories` (migration 0103), NULL = unpinned. Transform: [`reorderPinned`](./services/api/src/lib/pin-order.ts) (pure, unit-tested in [`pin-order.test.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/pin-order.test.ts)) — every action is a rewrite of the ordered id list, so the stored sequence comes back dense and duplicate-free whatever it held going in. Endpoints: `POST /admin/sports/:id/order` in [`admin/sports.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/sports.ts) (global scope), `POST /admin/tournaments/:id/order` in [`admin/tournaments.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts) (migration 0104, scoped to the tournament's own CATEGORY — the bucket it renders in; esports tournaments all sit under one synthetic dummy category per sport, which is also how the storefront draws them) and `POST /admin/categories/:id/order` in [`admin/categories.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/categories.ts) (scoped to the category's own sport; dummy categories are refused — the storefront renders that bucket without a header, so there is no position for it to hold). Both are audit-logged and bust the cache their surface reads. Read path: `/catalog/sports` carries `displayOrder` per sport, `/catalog/matches` carries `sport.displayOrder` per MATCH (the cross-sport lobby / Live / Pre-match pages don't fetch the sports tree, so the pin has to ride the row), and `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments` carries `category.displayOrder`. Applied by [`compareSports`](./apps/web/src/lib/sport-order.ts) — the one place the storefront decides which sport comes first, in three tiers: operator pin, then the hard-coded flagship slugs, then name — and by `groupTournamentsByCategory` in [`sidebar.tsx`](./apps/web/src/components/shell/sidebar.tsx) for the tree. **Tournaments sort on the SERVER** (`/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments`), pin first then the old rule — tier, live count, match count, name; the client buckets afterwards and preserves order inside each, which is what makes a pinned tournament head its own category even though the sort runs across the whole sport. **What it does NOT move:** a bettor's own saved sport order (migration 0056), which still wins — this sets the default, not somebody's choice. It DOES drive the lobby's **Top sports** strip as of 2026-09-05: that was a hard-coded seven-slug esports allowlist nobody could change without a deploy, and it now renders the pinned sports in pin order, falling back to the old list only while nothing is pinned so a fresh estate is never blank. The strip also gained a `home.topSports` heading — unlabelled, a chip row reads as a filter bar rather than the recommendation it is. UI: shared [`PinOrderControls`](./apps/web/src/components/admin/pin-order-controls.tsx) on both admin tables (rank chip + ⤴ / ↑ / ↓ / ✕ when pinned, one “Pin to top” button when not), styled inline because the bare `.btn` utility carries no background and the two host tables are written in different idioms. |
| Category flags (sidebar country groupings) | API proxy [`services/api/src/modules/catalog/flags.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/flags.ts) (`GET /catalog/flags/:name`, 600/min) + name table [`apps/web/src/lib/category-flags.ts`](./apps/web/src/lib/category-flags.ts). Fonbet's static CDN carries a circle-flag set keyed by the English country name — `/ContentCommon/NewFlags/Circle/png/<Name>.png` — which is exactly the string our `categories.name` holds for the Fonbet line (the segment-prefix split yields `Spain`, `Brazil`, `Hong Kong`). So flags need no migration and no per-category upload. **Proxied, not hot-linked**: Fonbet is a scraped public line, not a data partner, so pointing every visitor's browser at their CDN would put our whole audience in their logs and leave the flags one referer rule from breaking; the api fetches once and caches the bytes in Redis (30 d, `allkeys-lru` so an eviction just costs a refetch), serving them `immutable`. Misses are negative-cached 24 h, but an upstream *failure* deliberately is not — a transient blip must not blank the flags for a day. CDN host follows `FONBET_LOGO_CDN`, the same var fonbet-ingester uses for team crests, so a fon.bet ↔ fonbet.kz estate move needs no storefront rebuild. **The upstream file names are not a clean transform** (`Costa-Rica` but `Czech_Republic`, `South-Korea-2` for Korea, `Madagaskar` for Madagascar), so the map is an explicit 200-entry table verified by probing the live CDN rather than a string rewrite; anything absent (esports categories, `Other`, `WC 2026`) resolves to null and costs no request — the sidebar then falls back to the bucket's own tournament logo, or to no mark at all. **The route must be exempt from Caddy's blanket `no-store`, and it was not until 2026-09-06** — the `@api_nocache` matcher lists the public image byte-serves that keep their own `public, immutable` header, this route was added after that list and never joined it, so both headers reached the browser and `no-store` won. Nothing looked wrong: the api's header was correct and only a header dump showed which one applied. The cost was the whole point of the proxy undone — every sidebar render re-downloaded every flag, ~105 requests and ~500 KB each time a bettor expanded Football, which is most of why expanding a big sport felt slow on a phone. Exactly the failure the Caddyfile comment already recorded for the banner-image route in 2026-08; **any new public image route has to be added to that matcher**. The `<img>` tags also carry `loading="lazy"` now, so a tree that opens 105 marks only fetches the dozen on screen. |
| Frontend design | Live (2026-04-18) | Ported from Claude Design handoff bundle. Grid shell: top-bar + left sidebar + main + right-rail bet slip. Route groups: `(auth)` = login/signup, `(main)` = home/sport/match/account/bets/wallet, `admin/*` keeps its own layout. Sidebar order: CS2 → Dota 2 → LoL → Valorant → every other active sport (alphabetical); `efootballbots` + `ebasketballbots` are hidden via both the feed-ingester blocklist (`BLOCKED_ODDIN_SPORT_SLUGS`) and a defensive filter in `sidebar.tsx`. Tokens in `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`; legacy `--color-*` aliases kept for admin/bets/wallet pages. |
| Web SSR replica fan-out + request-id tracing | Live (2026-05-11) | Storefront runs three Next.js replicas (`web1` / `web2` / `web3`) behind Caddy `lb_policy least_conn` with active health checks on `/healthz` (5 s interval; replica drops out within 7 s if it goes down). `web1` always runs; `web2` + `web3` are gated on the `scaled` Compose profile so local dev keeps the single-process behaviour. Per-replica `mem_limit` is 400 MiB (down from the previous single-replica 640) so the three together fit cleanly in 8 GB alongside postgres + api + workers. Rolling deploys via `make recreate-web` cycle the replicas serially and wait for each healthcheck, so updates never take all three down at once. Cross-process tracing rides an `x-request-id` header: the Next.js middleware ([apps/web/src/middleware.ts](./apps/web/src/middleware.ts)) generates or echoes one per page render and forwards it on every server-side fetch (`lib/server-fetch.ts`, `lib/auth.ts`); Fastify's `genReqId` picks it up so `reqId` lands on every api log line; ws-gateway logs it on upgrade; the api echoes it back to the browser via `onSend`. A single grep with the request id finds the SSR render + every API call + WS upgrade it triggered (`make weblogs` interleaves all three replicas; the production-side jq recipe is in [docs/OPERATIONS.md](./docs/OPERATIONS.md#logs)). |
| Bet slip + placement | Live | Singles + combos (up to 20 legs, cross-match only — same-match legs rejected server-side). Always-visible right rail (`apps/web/src/components/shell/bet-slip-rail.tsx`) drives the `BetSlipProvider` store, with a Single/Combo toggle and a USDT/OZ currency switcher (default OZ for the demo flow). The chosen currency is persisted in localStorage and forwarded as `currency` on `POST /bets`; the API debits the matching `(user_id, currency)` wallet. Withdrawals also block non-active users at request time. |
| bet-delay worker | Live | LISTEN + 1s sweep + 5% drift tolerance |
| Oddin AMQP feed (feed-ingester + settlement) | Live | AMQPS over `:5672` (not 5671), vhost `/oddinfeed/{customer_id}` URL-assembled by hand to preserve `%2F`. Bookmaker 142 |
| Recovery flow | Live | `POST /v1/{product}/recovery/initiate_request` triggered on every (re)connect for both producers + on `alive subscribed=0` + on `alive` timestamp drift > 5s. **Suspend-before-recover is unconditional (2026-05-11):** every AMQP (re)connect runs `store.FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog` BEFORE asking Oddin to replay — flips every active market on a `not_started`/`live` match to `status=-1`, moves every `live` MATCH to `status='suspended'` so the match leaves the offer with its markets (suspending markets alone left matches asserting `live` with nothing to walk the claim back — 675 such rows had piled up by 2026-09-03, oldest from 2026-04-18; only `live` is moved, and `UpdateMatchStatus` carries a narrow exception letting `suspended` return to `not_started`), and nulls outcome `published_odds`/`raw_odds`/`probability`. The replay re-activates only what Oddin re-confirms; anything Oddin omits stays suspended and drops from listings via the catalog filter. Rationale: even a few seconds of feed gap can leave a market quoting odds Oddin already moved off of, and a single placement at stale odds is real-money loss. The 2026-05-09 disk-full incident wedged 33 matches this way (Oddin's replay didn't carry their terminal status because they were no longer in Oddin's active state by reconnect time). The auto-path does SUSPEND only — no DELETE — because the bulk DELETE races with concurrent settlement INSERTs on `settlements.market_id` (no `ON DELETE` action) and trips an FK violation that aborts the whole tx; SUSPEND is race-safe because UPDATE on the parent doesn't trigger child FK checks, and the catalog filter gates on `markets.status=1` so a suspended row is invisible whether or not its parent match still exists. The recovery request itself clamps the cursor to `now - 24 h` (per `handler.recoveryWindowCap`) to stay in Oddin's lenient rate-limit tier (< 30 min) and bound replay latency; anything older falls through as "stayed suspended". Admin `POST /admin/feed/recovery` (with `flushOdds=true` default) now uses the SAME suspend-based flush as the auto-path — it `UPDATE`s every active `not_started`/`live` market to `status=-1` and nulls outcome odds/probability, then rewinds the cursor + `pg_notify('feed_recovery')`, all in one transaction (atomic: no half-flush, no rewound-cursor-without-replay). It used to `DELETE FROM markets` first, but that bulk delete deadlocks against the live feed-ingester's concurrent upserts (the "something went wrong" 500 an operator hit on 2026-06-01) and FK-races settlement INSERTs — so the DELETE was dropped entirely. The flush is wrapped in a `lock_timeout`-bounded retry (`RECOVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`) and returns a typed 503 `recovery_lock_contended` instead of an unhandled 500 if the feed stays too busy. Storefront end-state is identical (a `status=-1` market is invisible to the catalog filter, same as a deleted one). Stale market rows Oddin no longer re-confirms remain as invisible suspended rows (same as the auto-path; physical row cleanup, if ever needed, belongs in a feed-ingester sweeper, not the API). **Alive watchdog (2026-05-29):** suspend-before-recover only fires on a *successful* (re)connect, so it cannot help when the token is revoked server-side — every reconnect fails, `OnConnect` never runs, and the catalog keeps quoting frozen odds (the 2026-05-29 token-revocation incident: ~8k markets stayed bettable for over an hour with the feed dead). The time-based `runAliveWatchdog` (in [`services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go`](./services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go)) closes that gap: it reads `lastAmqpMessageUnix` (bumped on EVERY delivery — alive heartbeat, odds_change, anything) and when no message has arrived for `FEED_STALE_SUSPEND_SECONDS` (default 20s = two missed ~10s alives) it runs `FlushAndSuspendActiveCatalog` + broadcasts `marketStatus=-1` frames, independent of connection state. Suspends once per silence episode (guarded), clears the guard when data resumes; re-activation rides the existing recovery paths. `<=0` disables it. **The earlier `handleAlive` gap-detection only acts on alives that *arrive* — it never fired when alives stopped entirely; the watchdog is the missing absence-detector.** |
| Wedged matches admin tool | Live (2026-05-11) | `/admin/wedged-matches` lists matches at `not_started` more than 1 h past `scheduled_at` with active markets (the same shape the suspend-before-recover flush is designed to clean up automatically). Each row exposes Refresh from REST → `pg_notify('fixture_refresh', urn)`; feed-ingester re-fetches via `RefreshFromFixture` and applies the fixture body's `status` attribute, which carries Oddin's authoritative current state. Routes in [`services/api/src/modules/admin/wedged-matches.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/wedged-matches.ts), page at [`apps/web/src/app/admin/wedged-matches/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/wedged-matches/). |
| Storefront catalog time-gate | **Removed 2026-09-07** (added 2026-05-11) | Every list endpoint used to drop a `not_started` match whose kickoff was more than six hours old, on the reasoning that a fixture which never went `live` is broken feed data — the 2026-05-09 disk-full incident had wedged 33 such rows. **The operator had it removed, and the reasoning is worth keeping.** It fixed nothing: the gate is a LIST predicate and placement never consulted it, so `POST /bets` went on accepting any market on a `not_started` match regardless of age and `/match/:id` served it by direct link. A wedged match stayed bettable at frozen odds and merely stopped appearing where anyone — us included — would notice; the gate hid the evidence and left the exposure. It also cost the operator their own book: a custom event's `scheduled_at` is a publication time, not a kickoff, so six hours later the rule read it as broken data. That was patched with a `custom_event_config.ends_at` carve-out which then had to live in FOUR copies of the gate (`hasActiveMarket`, `/catalog/sports`, the tournament facet of `/catalog/search`, and the bookable count on `/admin/categories`) and only ever reached the first — so on 2026-09-07 the Custom sport vanished from the sidebar while `/catalog/sports/custom` still served its events. Measured on production at removal: the gate was hiding exactly **two** matches, both operator-authored events, and **not one** wedged feed match; taking it out moved the listed set 2744 → 2746 and the sport list 39 → 40. What actually contains a wedged match is structural and already in place — suspend-before-recover on every AMQP reconnect, the alive watchdog's 20 s silence flush, and `/admin/wedged-matches` for the leftovers — and those set markets to `status=-1`, which removes the row from lists AND refuses placement. A wedged match is visible now, which is the point: it is a bug to fix, not noise to hide. **Do NOT add a placement guard to compensate.** Removing the gate does not by itself stop a wedged match being placed on, and the obvious next move is an age check in `POST /bets` — the operator considered and refused it on 2026-09-07: a wedged match is to be caught and its CAUSE removed, not left in place with a rule papering over it. The same rule would also reject a legitimately delayed fixture, and it would recreate the exact property that made the list gate worthless — a symptom suppressed where it happened to show, with the real defect still live. The defences that address the cause are the ones named above, and `/admin/wedged-matches` is where the leftovers surface. **What survives** is the lifecycle clause the gate was bolted onto — `status IN ('not_started','live')`, which keeps a closed or cancelled match out of the lists even when a stray market row stayed at `status=1`. It lives in `bookableWindow()` in [`catalog-predicates.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/catalog-predicates.ts) and **all four call sites go through it**, taking the SQL alias (`m`, `mm`) where the query reaches `matches` through one rather than through the Drizzle table. Sharing it is the other half of the lesson: the clause that broke was not wrong in itself, it was wrong in three of the four places it had been copied to, and nothing failed when they drifted. |
| `<sport_event_status>` is the lifecycle source of truth | Live (2026-05-06) | The Oddin spec (§2.4) lists exactly 8 message types: `fixture_change`, `odds_change`, `alive`, `bet_cancel`, `rollback_bet_cancel`, `bet_settlement`, `rollback_bet_settlement`, `snapshot_complete`. **Neither `match_status_change` nor `bet_stop` is in the protocol** — the integration broker for bookmaker 142 never emits them; our `handleMatchStatusChange` and `handleBetStop` handlers stay wired but only as a graceful no-op for any bookmaker that did emit them. The authoritative lifecycle signal is `<sport_event_status status="…"/>` carried inside every `odds_change` (per §2.4.1.2: documented values are `0` not_started, `1` live, `4` closed, `5` cancelled). `handleOddsChange` forwards every observed code through `store.UpdateMatchStatus`, whose SQL guard rejects regressions (terminal → anything else, anything → `not_started`). Net effect: a match appears on the live offer the first odds_change it ships with `status=1`, and drops off the offer the odds_change it ships with `status=4` or `5`. |
| All-markets-terminal close | Live (2026-05-06) | Per spec §2.4.2: "When a market or market line is settled with a bet_settlement message, it will be automatically removed from all subsequent match odds_change messages." So once the last market settles, no further odds_change arrives — a final `<sport_event_status status="4"/>` may never land. Settlement now calls `store.MarkMatchClosedIfAllMarketsTerminal(event_urn)` after every `bet_settlement` and every `bet_cancel`. The single SQL flips `matches.status='closed'` only when no market on the match is at `1 / 0 / -1 / -2` — so per-map market settlements during a live match (where the match-winner is still active) don't false-positive. Forward-only via the same status guard. |
| Phantom-drain ticker | **Removed** (2026-05-06, PR #122) | Was a 15-min REST sweep that mopped up matches whose `matches.status` never advanced. Removed because (a) `<sport_event_status>` inside every `odds_change` is the authoritative lifecycle signal post-PR #121, and (b) the drain was masking real Oddin / ingest bugs by silently overwriting rows whose in-band path had failed. A drifting match is now visible — debug it. |
| Per-message outcome diff cleanup | Live (2026-05-25) | Per Oddin / UOF protocol, each `odds_change` carries the FULL current outcome set for the markets it references — outcomes Oddin omits are no longer on offer. The previous code only upserted the outcomes IN the message and left omitted rows at their stale `active=true, published_odds=…`. Confirmed production bug: a "Winning margin 5-7" stayed quoted at 2.15 on /match/707005 long after Oddin had pulled it from offer. Fix lives in [`store.DeactivateMissingOutcomes`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/markets.go) (one UNNEST anti-join per message), called from `handleOddsChange` right after `UpsertOutcomesBulk` and scoped to status=1 markets only (suspended/handover/terminal markets are gated by `markets.status` upstream and stripping their last-quoted odds would make the routine mid-round "Suspended" rendering look broken). Defense in depth: bet placement re-reads `market_outcomes.active` and rejects with `outcome_not_active`, so even if a live WS subscriber hasn't refreshed, a stale slip click fails at the boundary. |
| Live-stream embeds (Twitch / YouTube / Kick / Gjirafa) | Live (2026-05-06) | Oddin's `/v1/sports/{lang}/sport_events/{matchURN}/fixture` returns a `<tv_channels><tv_channel name=… language=… stream_url=…/></tv_channels>` block per the public schema. `oddinxml.FixtureTvChannelsList` parses it; resolver `merge()` JSON-encodes the rows into `matches.tv_channels` (migration 0022). Fixture `STREAM_URL` change_type 106 now also triggers `RefreshFromFixture` so the column tracks broadcaster swaps mid-event. **Belt-and-suspenders:** feed-ingester's `handleOddsChange` also fires `pg_notify('fixture_refresh', urn)` on every not_started → live transition because Oddin frequently attaches broadcasters around match start *without* emitting the 106 event — the existing 5-min per-URN cooldown in `runFixtureRefreshListener` keeps repeated live ticks from hammering Oddin's REST. **That transition refresh is necessary but NOT sufficient, and on its own it caught almost nothing** (fixed 2026-08-31): it fires seconds after kickoff, and Oddin attaches broadcasters at or *after* that moment, so the fetch returns a fixture with no `<tv_channels>` and — with the 106 event not arriving in practice — nothing ever asked again. Measured on production before the fix: **649 of 724 live matches had NULL `tv_channels`, and ZERO of 523 upcoming matches had any**, while Oddin's fixture endpoint was serving three channels for a match stored as NULL; a single manual `pg_notify` populated it, proving the parse + persist paths were fine and this was purely a timing race. The fix is `runStreamBackfillSweeper` in [`main.go`](./services/feed-ingester/cmd/feed-ingester/main.go) — every 60 s it selects live matches with NULL `tv_channels` whose `live_started_at` is between 1 and 15 minutes ago ([`SelectLiveMatchesMissingStreams`](./services/feed-ingester/internal/store/catalog.go), batch of 40) and re-emits `fixture_refresh` for each, riding the same listener cooldown. Both bounds are load-bearing: the 1-minute floor skips the moment the transition already covered, and the 15-minute ceiling stops us re-asking forever about the many matches that genuinely have no broadcaster. `/catalog/matches/:id` classifies each entry into `{platform: twitch \| youtube \| kick \| gjirafa \| vpplayer \| other, embedId, url, name, language}` and surfaces it as `match.streams`. **`vpplayer` is `host.vpplayer.tech`, Gjirafa's white-label player host** — Oddin labels those channels "Gjirafa" but serves them from that domain, not `video.gjirafa.com`, so before 2026-08-31 they classified as `other` and silently never embedded (one of three advertised sources on a live eFootball match). It is the only platform with a null `embedId`: the URL already IS the player page (`/player/<account>/<video>.html`) and goes into the iframe verbatim, so the api shape-checks the path before classifying it. Needs its own CSP `frame-src` entry. The storefront `apps/web/src/components/match/match-streams.tsx` renders an iframe (Twitch via `https://player.twitch.tv/?channel=…&parent=<host>`, YouTube via `youtube-nocookie.com/embed/…`, Kick via `https://player.kick.com/<channel>`, Gjirafa via `https://video.gjirafa.com/embed/<slug>`) with a tab strip when there are multiple feeds. Anything that doesn't classify as embeddable is hidden (no outbound-link row) — only when the match has zero embeddable feeds at all does a single fallback card link out. The CSP `frame-src` (emitted by Next.js middleware in [`apps/web/src/middleware.ts`](./apps/web/src/middleware.ts), not Caddy) whitelists `https://player.twitch.tv https://www.twitch.tv https://www.youtube.com https://www.youtube-nocookie.com https://player.kick.com https://video.gjirafa.com`. |

Reject restricted to `requested` only (use mark-failed for already-approved rows); user-cancel and admin-reject / fail now write a `wallet_ledger` audit row (`adjustment` / `withdrawal_cancel` / `withdrawal_reject` / `withdrawal_fail`) for the lock release. |

Per match: list of all markets with their outcomes + winner highlight (from `market_outcomes.result`), an inline 24h SVG chart, and a per-market **Odds history** button (30-day window since 2026-07-02; was 7-day) → `/admin/logs/matches/[id]/markets/[marketId]` with the full 7-day chronological table. **Feed log** button opens `/admin/logs/matches/[id]/feed` with a kind-filterable `<details>` viewer of raw AMQP XML. Counts and feed-message lookups join on `event_urn = matches.provider_urn` so rows inserted before the auto-mapper created the match (race condition) are still surfaced. feed-ingester writes every match-scoped kind to `feed_messages`; hourly `runFeedMessageCleanup` goroutine sweeps + backfills `match_id`. | Admin: FE Settings (markets display order + market groups) | Live; real per-sport tab set since 2026-09-05, per-market curation since 2026-09-06 | `/admin/fe-settings/markets-order` per-sport, per-tab editor with HTML5 drag-and-drop. **Each sport is offered the tabs its own offer produces, not a fixed list.** Until 2026-09-05 the screen enumerated `match` + `map_1..map_5` + `top` for every sport — the esports shape — so football was offered five Map tabs no football fixture has ever had, while the tabs bettors were actually looking at (1st half, 2nd half, Corners, Yellow cards, Players — derived from Fonbet's `variant` sub-events since that line landed) could be neither ordered nor reordered. Scopes: `match` (the base event), `map_<N>` (one independent list per map tab), `fb_<kinds>` (one per Fonbet sub-event, including the collapsed `fb_players`; migration 0106), `top` (curated highlights, empty by default), `custom_<key>` (admin-created curated groups). **Discovery, not enumeration**: the tab set is not stored anywhere — the match page derives it per request from the markets it is about to render — so [`services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts`](./services/api/src/lib/fe-market-scopes.ts) re-derives it over the sport's CURRENT offer using the same shared module the storefront derives from ([`packages/types/src/market-scope.ts`](./packages/types/src/market-scope.ts)), Redis-cached 120 s (bump `CACHE_PREFIX` on any payload-shape change). Bounded to open matches and to the market statuses the match page renders: the all-time alternative reads ~17M rows in ~9 s and answers a different question — tabs from fixtures that finished months ago. Per-player Fonbet variants are collapsed in SQL before the DISTINCT. Measured on production 2026-09-05: football 28 tabs, ice hockey 15, tennis and basketball 13, an esport 6. **The screen does two different jobs and now looks like it** (2026-09-06): a FEED tab (Match / Map N / a sub-event) already holds its markets — membership is the feed's call — so it renders ONE list in the order bettors see, and dragging changes that order. It used to render "Ordered (0)" beside "Unranked (6)", which reads as "this tab is empty" when in fact all six markets were on it and merely unpinned; an operator reported exactly that. A CURATED tab (Top, customs) is opt-in membership, so it keeps two columns, and its right-hand pool is **every market on the sport, one entry per (type, sub-event)** with a filter box and the owning tab on each row — 110 picks for football where the pmid-keyed pool showed 14, because `provider_market_id` is the catalogue TABLE and Fonbet reuses one table across every sub-event (migration 0109 carries the sub-event on the row). Blank rows are gone too: a few Fonbet tables have no name of their own — their whole caption is the sub-event prefix ("1st half: ") — and now fall back to `Market #<id>`. Map tabs share one market pool, since which kinds are quoted on map 4 right now is an accident of what is live and an operator has to be able to order Map 5 before a series reaches it; curated tabs draw from the whole sport; a market an operator already ordered is always listed even when nothing is live under it. PUT `/admin/fe-settings/markets-order/:sportId/:scope` writes a transactional replace into `fe_market_display_order` and takes either bare ids (feed tabs) or `{providerMarketId, variant}` pairs. **Market groups (tabs)** are managed at `/admin/fe-settings/markets-order/[sportId]/groups` (create / rename / delete custom groups + reorder ALL tabs), backed by `/admin/fe-settings/market-groups/:sportId` (list / POST / PATCH / DELETE / PUT order). Tab order lives in `fe_market_groups` (migration 0084): tabs WITH a row sort first by `display_order`, the rest keep the default (Top, Match, Map 1..N, then sub-events by Fonbet kind then nesting depth); first configuration seeds anchor rows for every tab the sport actually has, so a new custom group appends at the end. **`/catalog/matches/:id` needed no change for the tab work** — it already keyed both the per-tab market ordering and the tab order off the group id, so a saved `fb_*` row reaches the storefront the moment the CHECK lets it exist; what changed there is that the sub-event derivation moved out of the route into the shared module, and that curated groups now honour the row's variant. The storefront's `All` tab is intentionally NOT configurable — it just aggregates the per-scope groups in native order. List endpoints (`/catalog/sports/:slug`, `/catalog/matches`) also include `topMarket` per match so storefront list cards render a [Match \| Top] toggle inline (`apps/web/src/components/match/match-list-tabs.tsx`); custom groups and sub-event tabs are match-detail-only. |
| Admin: team logos | Live (2026-05-06; upload + remove added 2026-05-08) | `/admin/competitors` lists every team with sport/q/missing-logo filters, inline edit of `logo_url` / `brand_color` / `abbreviation`, **plus per-row Upload + Remove buttons** (migration 0040). Same upload pipeline as `/admin/sports`: `POST /admin/competitors/:id/logo` (multipart, SVG / PNG / JPEG / WebP, ≤1 MB) stores bytes in `competitors.logo_data BYTEA` + `competitors.logo_mime TEXT` and stamps `logo_url='/api/competitors/<id>/logo?v=<unix-ms>'`; `DELETE /admin/competitors/:id/logo` clears all three columns in one tx. Public byte-serve at `GET /competitors/:id/logo`. Backed by [`services/api/src/modules/admin/competitors.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/competitors.ts) with audit logging on every mutation. **Auto-resolved logos hot-link directly to Oddin's CDN** — Oddin is our authorised data partner and `cdn.oddin.gg` is built for this. The single resolver, [`packages/db/src/resolve-logos.ts`](./packages/db/src/resolve-logos.ts), is one SQL UPDATE — copy `competitor_profiles.icon_path` (populated by feed-ingester from Oddin's `/v1/sports/{lang}/competitors/{urn}/profile` endpoint, ~1861/2135 profiles populated) onto `competitors.logo_url`. Idempotent; `pnpm --filter @oddzilla/db db:resolve-logos` runs in well under a second. Coverage on the current feed: ~87% of competitors have an `icon_path`; rows without one render the name alone (`TeamMark` draws nothing without a picture since 2026-09-05). Caddy CSP `img-src 'self' data: https:` accommodates the cross-origin `<img>`; `TeamMark` unmounts itself on `<img onError>` so a missing icon never leaves a broken-image placeholder in the row. |
| Admin: sport icons | Live (2026-05-08) | `/admin/sports` per-sport row editor for `logo_url` + `brand_color` (existing) **plus a per-row file upload + Remove logo button** (migration 0039). Upload endpoint `POST /admin/sports/:id/logo` (multipart) accepts SVG / PNG / JPEG / WebP up to 1 MB; stores bytes in `sports.logo_data BYTEA` + `sports.logo_mime TEXT` and stamps `logo_url='/api/sports/<slug>/logo?v=<unix-ms>'` so the storefront's existing `<img>` chain (`SportGlyph` → `useSportLogo` → bundled SVG → `FallbackGlyph`) picks the upload up automatically and re-uploads bust the browser cache via the `?v` param. `DELETE /admin/sports/:id/logo` clears bytes + URL in one tx. PATCH that overwrites `logo_url` with anything other than the byte-serve URL also wipes `logo_data` / `logo_mime` so bytes can't go orphan. Public byte-serve at `GET /sports/:slug/logo` ([`services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/catalog/routes.ts)) returns the bytes with `cache-control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. PNG/JPEG are stored as-is — true raster→SVG vectorisation is lossy and out of scope; admins should upload SVG directly when they have the source. |
| Admin: tournament logos | Live (2026-05-08) | New `/admin/tournaments` page ([`apps/web/src/app/admin/tournaments/`](./apps/web/src/app/admin/tournaments/)) with sport / q / missing-logo filters, per-row inline edit of `logo_url` + `brand_color`, **and per-row Upload + Remove buttons** (migration 0041 — first-class tournament branding columns). Backed by [`services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/admin/tournaments.ts): list + patch + multipart upload + delete + sport-filter dropdown source. Public byte-serve at `GET /tournaments/:id/logo`. Storefront sidebar tournament sub-tree (`apps/web/src/components/shell/sidebar.tsx` `TournamentItem`) renders a 14-px logo next to the name when `logoUrl` is set, with graceful `<img onError>` hiding so a stale URL never breaks the layout; `TierMark` (gold star) is unchanged and still surfaces tier 1/2 tournaments. `/catalog/sports/:slug/tournaments` now selects `logoUrl` + `brandColor` so the data flows end-to-end without a separate fetch. |
| Server security hardening | Live | Non-root inside every container (`node:1000` for TS, `app:100` for Go); `cap_drop: ALL` + `no-new-privileges:true` on every service in `docker-compose.yml`; Caddy keeps only `NET_BIND_SERVICE` (PR #130). Per-service `mem_limit` budgeted for the 4 GB CPX22 (postgres 1G, web 640M, api 512M, ws-gateway 256M, Go workers 320M, redis 320M, caddy 96M). Per-service `json-file` log rotation (10 MiB × 5 files) closes the disk-full repeat surface from `project_disk_full_incident`. SSH `PasswordAuthentication no`, `PermitRootLogin no`, `MaxAuthTries 3`, `X11Forwarding no`; repo dir `chmod 750`. **Nonce-based CSP** emitted by Next.js middleware (per-request nonce passed to the inline theme-boot script via `headers().get("x-csp-nonce")`); Caddy's static CSP removed. HSTS now has `preload`. `NODE_ENV=production` on server so auth cookies get `Secure` flag. `docker-compose.override.yml` renamed to `docker-compose.dev.yml` so a forgotten `-f` in production cannot auto-mount the source tree. `fail2ban` was installed briefly then removed (kept banning legit operator connections — password auth off makes brute force impossible anyway, see `project_security_hardening.md` memory). |
| Postgres backups | Live | `/usr/local/bin/oddzilla-pg-backup` (from `infra/hetzner/backup/pg_backup.sh`) runs daily at 03:00 UTC via root cron, `docker exec` into postgres container, writes `/var/backups/oddzilla/*.sql.gz` (root:team 640). **Count-based retention `RETENTION_COUNT` (default 2, lowered from 4 on 2026-06-17)**; prunes BEFORE the dump + atomic `.part` rename so a full disk can't block rotation or leave a truncated dump. PR #130 hardening: reads only `POSTGRES_USER` / `POSTGRES_DB` / `BACKUP_GPG_RECIPIENT` from `.env` (no full source). **The password never touches the host** (2026-09-03): every script that runs `pg_dump` / `psql` in the postgres container — this one, the pre-deploy `infra/deploy/dump-db.sh`, `audit_chain_check.sh`, `odds_retention.sh`, `settlements_retention.sh` — goes through `docker exec <container> sh -c 'export PGPASSWORD="$POSTGRES_PASSWORD"; exec …' sh <user> <db>` so `PGPASSWORD` is read from the container's own environment; the earlier `docker exec -e PGPASSWORD=<value>` form put the real password into `ps` for every local user and into the `sudo` journal line for the duration of each run. Never reintroduce `-e PGPASSWORD=`. `BACKUP_GPG_RECIPIENT` → each dump GPG-encrypted (`.sql.gz.gpg`). **Off-host copy wired 2026-08-26**: operator workstation Task Scheduler job `OddzillaBackupPull` pulls the newest dump daily (sha256-verified, keeps 7 local); the on-box cron pins `RETENTION_COUNT=1`. |
| odds_history retention | Live (2026-06-17; partition model since 2026-08-26) | `/usr/local/bin/oddzilla-odds-retention` (from [`infra/hetzner/backup/odds_retention.sh`](./infra/hetzner/backup/odds_retention.sh)), root cron `30 3 * * *`. Since the 2026-08-26 conversion the table runs on daily dated partitions (`odds_history_pYYYYMMDD` + safety DEFAULT `odds_history_default3`): the cron pre-creates partitions 7 days ahead and DETACH CONCURRENTLY + DROPs those older than `ODDS_RETENTION_DAYS`. Partition DROP returns space to the OS instantly — zero bloat, no high-water, no future reclaims. **The script's default is 35 but production runs `ODDS_RETENTION_DAYS=5`** (explicit in the root crontab) — an operator measure against disk pressure, meant to be temporary, but 35 is not currently reachable: measured 2026-09-06, a full day costs **~20 GB** with indexes since the Fonbet line went live (~1.9 GB/day before it, a ~10x step), so 35 days would need ~700 GB on a 150 GB disk and the 5-day window is already ~51 GB. `/admin/logs` asks for 30 days (`HISTORY_DAYS`) and therefore shows at most 5; ZillaTips is unaffected (permanent `prematch_odds` snapshot) and settlement never reads history. **The write rate is the thing to fix first, not the window** — `ODDS_HISTORY_SKIP_PMID_MIN` is still unset on prod, so the documented Fonbet brake has never been pulled; pull it and re-measure a complete day before widening the window. History: single catch-all DEFAULT + batched DELETE until 2026-08-26 (heap plateaued ~60 GB, never shrank); one-time reclaims ran 2026-07-02 (95%→50%) and 2026-08-26 (export→drop→restore swap, window 45→35, 90%→55%). See docs/OPERATIONS.md. |
| Published odds margin | Active but **0 bp globally** | `odds_config` row `scope='global'` has `payback_margin_bp=0` because Oddin already ships odds with margin baked in. `applyMargin()` divides `raw / (1 + bp/10000)`, snaps the result down onto the quote ladder, and renders at up to 4dp with trailing zeros trimmed to a ≥2dp minimum. The 4dp intermediate is arithmetic, not a price: a 5% margin on 2.00 is 1.9047 and publishes as **1.90** (2026-09-07; the raw 4dp value shipped to the storefront from 2026-05-25 until then, and before that it was floor-truncate to 2dp + a 1.01 floor). Values under 1.01 skip the ladder and keep every digit, so "5% margin on 1.003" still publishes 0.9552. No low-side floor on the PUBLISHED price — the storefront shows whatever Oddin sends, including 1.003 or a flat 1.00. **Two things follow from that, both fixed 2026-08-27:** (1) the storefront must render through [`formatOddsDisplay`](./packages/types/src/odds.ts), not `toFixed(2)` — `OddButton`, the compact match-row button, and the slip's per-leg odds all used a fixed 2dp, which printed a genuine 1.003 as "1.00" (a price that doesn't exist, and indistinguishable from a real 1.0000); (2) placement rejects any leg whose authoritative price is `<= 1` with `outcome_odds_too_low` ([`bets/service.ts`](./services/api/src/modules/bets/service.ts)) — a 1.00 leg returns exactly the stake on a win while carrying full loss/void risk, contributes nothing to a combo's payout, and a non-zero `payback_margin_bp` on any scope divides near-1.0 odds *below* 1.0 (`publisher_test.go` pins "5% margin on 1.003" at `0.9552`). The prior guard only rejected `<= 0`. **Operator decision (2026-08-27): sub-1.01 odds are first-class — no floor, no hiding.** A price of 1.003 displays and places normally end-to-end (`NUMERIC(10,4)` storage, `multiplyMicroByOdds` scales by 10 000, Go `SinglePayout` floors `stake × eff` — a 10 OZ bet at 1.003 pays 10.03). The floor sits at exactly **1.00 and below**, which is *displayed but not offered*: [`isBettableOdds`](./packages/types/src/odds.ts) (`odds > 1`) greys the cell to an em dash in both `OddButton` and the compact match-row button, the same treatment a suspended outcome gets. Deliberately NOT hidden and NOT floored upward — flooring to 1.01 would be a negative-margin quote and would undo `a9bcf44`. Keep `isBettableOdds` and the placement guard in step: the UI must never offer a price `POST /bets` would refuse. Oddin does not appear to quote 1.00 in practice, so this is a defensive floor — but a non-zero `payback_margin_bp` makes sub-1.0 publishing reachable, which is what it really guards. Admin `/admin/margins` still works for per-sport/tournament/market-type overrides when needed. |
| Wallet HD address derivation | Live | Done by the isolated [`services/signer`](./services/signer/) Go service. The API talks to it over a Unix socket on a tmpfs volume that only those two containers mount. The signer reads `HD_MASTER_MNEMONIC` once at boot and `os.Unsetenv`s it; no other process holds the secret. `cap_drop: ALL`, `no_new_privileges`, `read_only: true`, `mem_limit: 64m`. The API has no offline derivation fallback — `SIGNER_SOCKET_PATH` is required. Parity test pins ETH idx=0 to the canonical 0x9858EfFD232B4033E47d90003D41EC34EcaEda94 from the BIP44 abandon-mnemonic vector. |
| Wallet deposit scanners | Live, gated on RPC URLs | Boots idle if `TRON_RPC_URL` / `ETH_RPC_URL` absent. **Reorg-safe (PR #130)**: ETH scanner captures `blockHash` per Transfer log into `deposits.block_hash`; processor calls `Scanner.VerifyDeposit(dep)` before crediting (`eth_getBlockByNumber` lookup, compares hash). On divergence the row flips to `status='orphaned'` instead of crediting. ETH client also re-verifies log `Address` per row (defends against an RPC endpoint that ignores the address filter) and hard-errors on missing `0x` prefix in `Data` (the previous behaviour silently parsed as 0 and advanced the cursor — losing real Transfers). Tron `normalizeTronAddressOK` returns `(_, false)` on unrecognised shapes; scanner aborts the batch (cursor stays put) so a deposit cannot be quietly dropped. TRC20 `event_index` captured so multi-Transfer txs no longer collide on `(network, tx_hash, log_index=0)`. |
| Wrong-token deposit visibility | Live (2026-05-13, migration 0048) | Two complementary surfaces for "user sent the wrong coin to the USDC receive address". (1) Intent path: when a user pastes the tx hash of a non-USDC Transfer, `Inspect()` in [`services/wallet-watcher/internal/ethereum/scanner.go`](./services/wallet-watcher/internal/ethereum/scanner.go) does a second pass for any Transfer-to-receive-address from a non-USDC contract and the processor routes through `RejectIntentWrongToken` which stamps `detected_token_contract` + `detected_token_amount_raw` (uint256, NUMERIC(78,0) — decimals are unknown at insert time) on the rejected intent. `failure_reason='wrong_token'`. (2) Wider scan: `DiscoverIncoming` now runs a SECOND `eth_getLogs` (`GetAllTransfersTo` — no `address` filter, only the recipient topic) over the same block range as the USDC scan. Every non-USDC Transfer becomes an `unattributed_deposits` row keyed by `(network, tx_hash, log_index)`. Token symbol + decimals are best-effort `eth_call` enrichment (`symbol()` / `decimals()` selectors, ABI dynamic-string decoder, per-contract in-memory cache); failed enrichment leaves NULL columns and the admin UI renders the raw uint256 amount plus the contract address. Cursor only advances after both scans succeed, so a transient `eth_getLogs` failure idempotently retries next tick (INSERTs are unique-key guarded). Dedup: inserting an unattributed row is skipped when a `deposit_intent` already represents the same `(network, tx_hash)`; conversely, rejecting an intent as wrong_token opportunistically acks any matching unattributed row inside the same transaction. Admin surface: `/admin/deposits` gains a tab strip (All / Pending / Confirming / Credited / Rejected / **Wrong Token** / **Unattributed**), unack-only by default on the alert tabs with an explicit Acknowledged / Both toggle. Acknowledge endpoints `POST /admin/deposits/:id/acknowledge` (wrong_token intents) and `POST /admin/deposits/unattributed/:id/acknowledge` both write `admin_audit_log` and accept `{undo: true}` for re-opening. The admin sidebar polls `GET /admin/deposits/alert-counts` every 60 s and renders a red numeric badge on the Deposits entry (red dot when collapsed). |
| Wallet withdrawal on-chain submission | **Manual** | Admin marks-submitted with tx hash from external signer/wallet. Pre-launch needs a dedicated signer container |
| News scraper | **Removed** | Service + `news_articles` table deleted via migration 0003; no longer in scope |

| Cash-out | Live | Migration 0015 adds `cashout_config`, `cashouts`, probability columns on `market_outcomes` / `odds_history` / `ticket_selections`, and a `cashout` value on `wallet_tx_type`. Algorithm is Sportradar §2.1.1 (`stake × ticketOdds × Π(legProb)`) with optional §2.1.2 deduction ladder; full-stake prematch window is configurable per-scope. Oddin already populates `probabilities="0.368"` on every active outcome (verified on the integration broker 2026-04-28); engine falls back to `1/oddsCurrent` when the attribute is missing. Endpoints: `GET /tickets/:id/cashout/quote`, `POST /tickets/:id/cashout`, plus `/admin/cashout-config` cascade. Settlement is unaffected — `maybeSettleTicket` already gates on `t.Status == "accepted"`, so `cashed_out` tickets are never re-paid. |
